# ZTronAssetKit — architecture

**Status.** Stage 1 contracts, contract-fix round 4 (2026-10-04). Merges the three Phase A designs (`docs/design/store.md`, `sync.md`, `transport.md`) and the six Phase B reviews (`docs/design/reviews/*.md`) into one authoritative design. Where this document and a design disagree, **this document wins**; the designs remain the detailed rationale, measurements and probe evidence. Decisions are ADR-001…055 in `DECISIONS.md`. Passages amended at the Stage 2 level-2 merge (WP-B, WP-D, WP-E) are marked "(ADR-050)", by the iOS Simulator test build "(ADR-051)", at the Stage 2 level-3 merge (WP-C) "(ADR-052)", at the Stage 2 level-4 merge (WP-F) "(ADR-053)", at the Stage 2 level-5 merge (WP-G) "(ADR-054)", and at the Stage 2 level-5 WP-K merge "(ADR-055)".

**Standalone package (owner directive, ADR-006).** Nothing is ever written outside this repository, except the device containers of the iOS Simulators that agents run the tests on (under `~/Library/Developer/CoreSimulator`; owner-accepted 2026-10-09, ADR-051). `../ZTronCarouselCore` and `../ZTronCarousel` are read-only prior art. R-CAR-6's compatibility shims live in `Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests` (ADR-033).

**How to read it.** §1–§2 the package, its ownership and work-package plan, and its concurrency model; §3 every file on disk; §4–§8 the per-layer design; §9 the hard problems H-1…H-10, each with a decision and exact test names; §10 the REENTRANCY ledger; §11 every requirement and budget → target → work package → test; §12 every review finding → resolution; §13 the dispositions of the four contract-fix reviews (§13.2 is round 2, §13.3 round 3, §13.4 round 4); §14 coverage seams; §15 hygiene items for the human.

---

## 1. Targets, dependencies and ownership

| Target | Depends on | Contract files (Stage 1, orchestrator-owned) | Implemented by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ZTronAssetModel` | — | `Identity/`, `Manifest/`, `Policy/`, `Scope/`, `Errors/`, `Environment/`, `Transfer/`, `Events/`, `Reports/`, `Tier/` | WP-A (manifest decoding, validation; everything else is complete) |
| `ZTronAssetStorage` | Model | `Store/{AssetStore,StoreState,StoreCoreState,StoreInternals,AssetRecord,StoreError,StoreTypes}.swift`, `Layout/StoreLayout.swift`, `Snapshot/AssetSnapshot.swift`, `Staging/StoreStagingSink.swift`, `FileSystem/LiveFileSystem.swift`, `Eviction/*`, `Cleanup/*`, `Integrity/*`, `Migration/*` | WP-B and WP-C, file by file as §1.1 lists |
| `ZTronAssetTransport` | Model | `HTTPRemoteSource.swift`, `Session/ForegroundSession.swift`, `Policy/{PriorityAndSize,TransportSecurity}.swift`, `Classify/TransferClassifier.swift`, `Conditions/LiveDeviceConditionsSource.swift`, `Background/BackgroundSupport.swift`, `BackgroundSessionBackend.swift` | WP-D, **every body**, the ones implemented in Stage 1 included (§6.1, §6.4) |
| `ZTronAssetAppleHosted` | Model | `PackTypes.swift`, `AppleHostedRemoteSource.swift`, `AppleHostedTier.swift`, `LiveAssetPackBackend.swift` | WP-E |
| `ZTronAssetSync` | Model, Storage | `Planning/SyncPlan.swift`, `Engine/{SyncEngine,EngineSupport,JobState}.swift`, `Refresh/RefreshDecision.swift` | WP-F |
| `ZTronAssetKit` | all above | `Facade/`, `Mirror/`, `Prefetch/`, `Fallback/`, `Composition/`, `Observability/`, `Memory/` | WP-G |
| `ZTronAssetTestSupport` | Model, Storage | every file (fake interfaces; `ManualTimeSource`, `InlineBlockingExecutor`, `SteppedBlockingExecutor`, `ScriptedSnapshotSink`, `SeededRandom`, `FakeDeviceConditionsSource`, `FakeMemoryPressureSource`, `FakeLegacyDefaults`, `RecordingLogTap`, `FakeTransferHandle`, `FakeTransportLog` with `FakeRemoteSource.starts`/`controlLog` (round 4), `FakeAppVersionSource`, `FakeRemoteSourceFactory` with `StagingAttachable`, `GeneratedPayload` are complete; `FileSystemConformance` (round 4) is WP-H's; ADR-045) | WP-H |
| Test targets | — | one per production target, `ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks` (parent suite `ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks.swift` and `QualityBudgets.swift`), `ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests` (R-CAR-6, ADR-033), and every `*ContractTests.swift`, including the package-wide scans `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/PackageScanContractTests.swift` (R-CAR-9, R-SEC-3, R-ENV-2…5; round 4) | the owning WP, in its own files; the `*ContractTests.swift` files are the orchestrator's (§1.2) |

What "contract" means here: every public and package type, protocol and signature is fixed; small pure functions whose semantics the design pins exactly are implemented and tested now (tier selection, layout and blob-name parsing, snapshot lookup, error mappings, transition table, refresh decision, backoff, size guard, priority table, security-policy check, pack release planner, prefetch window, candidate extensions, mirror install/ack, prefetch-handle release ordering, the purgeable-cache relay, `AssetLog` and the log sinks, the planning-policy and store-configuration wiring, the production background-backend construction, `GeneratedPayload`). Everything else is a `fatalError("WP-x: … not implemented")` stub that names its owner. A work package may add internal types freely; changing a contract signature is an orchestrator change (CLAUDE.md P1).

Only the composition root (`ZTronAssetKit/Composition/LiveEnvironment.swift`) names live conformers. Sync talks to Transport and AppleHosted only through Model protocols (`RemoteSource`, `TransferHandle`, `StagingSink`).

### 1.1 Storage: the WP-B / WP-C split (ADR-046)

`AssetStore` is one actor implemented by two work packages that run one after the other (§1.2), in disjoint files. An actor extension can neither add stored properties nor see `private` state, so the contract declares the state: `AssetStore.environment` (internal) and `AssetStore.state: StoreState` (internal), one value with one sub-struct per owner. Each sub-struct lives in a file of its owner, who may add fields to it; the Stage 1 fields are contract fields the other package relies on.

| Directory | Owner | Files (Stage 1) | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Store/` | WP-B | `AssetStore.swift` | the actor, `configuration`, `environment`, `state`, WP-B's API: `open`, `flush`, `close`, `makeStagingSink`, `planningView`, `location`, `availability`, `freeSpace`, `apply`, `admit`, `markSynced`, `markSyncDue`, `adoptionReport`, `stagingInventory`, `publish(_:binding:expecting:urgency:)`, `recordAccesses`, `metrics`, `nextDeadline`, `runDueWork`, and since the level-5 merge two package members the facade's B-15 reopen uses, added by WP-G under rulings L5-R4 and L5-R20: `unavailableReason` (the recorded reason, no I/O) and `verifyRootNow()` (async: the commit's own root check, `StoreLock.verifyRoot`, on demand, one stat on the metadata executor; ADR-054) |
| `Store/` | orchestrator | `StoreState.swift` | the aggregate `StoreState { core, cleanup, eviction, integrity, migration }` (changing it is an orchestrator change) |
| `Store/` | WP-B | `StoreCoreState.swift` | `StorePhase`, `DoomInfo`, `BlobState`, `StoreCoreState` (phase, layout, lock, epoch, committed generation, degraded, applied manifest, ETag, records, stamp, sync-due, purge epoch, blob table, publishing, metrics) |
| `Store/` | WP-B | `StoreInternals.swift` | the primitives WP-C calls: `ensureOpen()`, `setResidency(_:of:dooming:)`, `markDirty(_:)`, `requireCommitted(_:)`, `publishSnapshot(urgency:)`, `reapDeletable(maxKeys:)`, `hashFile(at:)`, `adoptVerifiedFile(at:digest:byteCount:for:)`, and since the level-3 merge `doomOrphans(_:)` (ruling R2 / CCR-1: dooms each listed orphan that is neither in the blob table nor being placed as `doomed(unreferenced)` with its listed size, eligible `deletionGrace` after the doom on the awake clock, removes it from the launch orphans, marks `.lazy`; internal, like the others; ADR-052) |
| `Store/` | WP-B | `AssetRecord.swift`, `StoreError.swift`, `StoreTypes.swift` | value types (contract) |
| `FileSystem/`, `Layout/`, `Snapshot/`, `Staging/` | WP-B | `LiveFileSystem.swift`, `StoreLayout.swift`, `AssetSnapshot.swift`, `StoreStagingSink.swift` | as today; `Layout/` and `Staging/` were in nobody's write set before round 3 |
| `Index/`, `Blobs/`, `Lock/` | WP-B | (created by WP-B) | index codec and load rule, streaming hash and blob placement, lock handling |
| `Eviction/` | WP-C | `EvictionState.swift`, `StoreEviction.swift` | `retentionScope`; `evictionPlan`, `setRetentionScope`, `setPinned`, `evictIfNeeded`, hook `applyEviction(_:)` |
| `Cleanup/` | WP-C | `CleanupState.swift`, `StoreLeases.swift`, `StoreCleanup.swift` | `leases`, `lastLeaseToken`, `lastCleanupAt` (`lastMaintenanceAt` removed in round 4); `acquireLease`, `releaseLease`; `collectStaging`, `collectGarbage`, `purge`, `storageReport` |
| `Integrity/` | WP-C | `IntegrityState.swift`, `StoreIntegrity.swift` | the verification `cursor`; `reportUnreadable`, `verifyIntegrity`, `recordIntegrityFailure`, `recordFailure(_:identity:reason:)` (which leaves a record bound to a live blob at `identity` unchanged, L4-R16; ADR-053) |
| `Migration/` | WP-C | `MigrationState.swift`, `StoreMigration.swift`, `LiveLegacyDefaults.swift` (complete) | `LegacyMigrationPhase`, `phase`, `legacyStamp`, `progress`; `migrateLegacy`, `adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)` (`urgency` defaulted to `.userInitiated` since the level-5 WP-K merge, L5K-R14; ADR-055), hook `runLegacyPhaseOne()` |

Files WP-C added at L3, in its own directories (ADR-052): `Eviction/` `EvictionOrder.swift`, `QuotaEviction.swift`, `HolderScan.swift` (the turn's one scan of the records for blobs whose known ids do not account for their references, C-21); `Cleanup/` `CleanupUnits.swift`, `OrphanCollection.swift`, `StagingCollection.swift`, `StorageReporting.swift`, `StorePurge.swift`; `Integrity/` `DeepVerification.swift`, `IntegrityBookkeeping.swift`; `Migration/` `LegacyPhases.swift`, `LegacyPlacement.swift`, `LegacyPlan.swift`, `LegacyRemoval.swift`, `LegacyUnits.swift`. Under orchestrator rulings WP-C also edited WP-B's `StoreInternals.swift` (R2), `AssetStore+Apply.swift` (R1, R3, R6) and `ApplyPlan.swift` (R3, R6: `ApplyEvictionChunks`); their ownership is unchanged (WP-B). At the level-4 merge, under rulings L4-R5 and L4-R16, WP-F edited WP-C's `Integrity/StoreIntegrity.swift` (the `recordFailure(_:identity:reason:)` guard, body and doc: a record bound to a live blob at `identity` is left unchanged, a stale record still fails) and two WP-C test files, `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/EvictionOverlapTests.swift` (the `failure` case of `R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need` retargeted to a stale record, its assertions unchanged) and `Cleanup/IntegrityTests.swift` (the new `R-NET-6: recordFailure leaves a record present at the failed content unchanged, and still fails a stale one`); their ownership is unchanged (WP-C; ADR-053). At the level-5 merge, under rulings L5-R4 and L5-R20, WP-G edited WP-B's `Store/AssetStore.swift` (two package members, `unavailableReason` and `verifyRootNow()`, each with a `///` comment naming the facade's B-15 reopen; REENTRANCY row g-12, §10.6) and added `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Core/StoreUnavailableReasonTests.swift` (its header cites L5-R4/CCR-G3 and L5-R20; three R-STO-9 tests and one R-STO-4 test); their ownership is unchanged (WP-B; ADR-054). At the level-5 WP-K merge, under rulings L5K-R13, L5K-R14, L5K-R17, L5K-R18, L5K-R20 and L5K-R23, WP-K edited WP-C's `Migration/LegacyPhases.swift` (bodies: one adoption per legacy file, `awaitLegacyAdoption`, `endLegacyAdoption`, `awaitLegacyFlight`, `endLegacyOnDemand` and `processLegacyFileOnDemand`; `performMigrateLegacy`'s wait for an adopt-on-demand of the last claimed files and its return right after a coalesced publication, what is left read after the publication's await; `performAdoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)` and its user-initiated present path; REENTRANCY rows k-5…k-7, §10.7), `Migration/MigrationState.swift` (the internal fields `adopting` and `onDemand`) and the contract file `Migration/StoreMigration.swift` (the package signature `adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)`, defaulted, and its doc comment), and added `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/LegacyOnDemandRaceTests.swift` (14 tests: 10 R-STO-11, 4 R-STO-13); their ownership is unchanged (WP-C; ADR-055).

Rules that keep the split mechanical:
1. **WP-C → WP-B** only through WP-B's package API and the `StoreInternals.swift` primitives; WP-C writes `StoreCoreState` fields only where that file says so (`purgeEpoch`, `stamp`, `syncDue` (every purge clears the stamp and sets it before its first own await, SPEC R-CLN-6; C-5), record `pinned`/`integrity`/`verifiedAt`, metrics counters), always followed by `markDirty(_:)` or `requireCommitted(_:)`. An orphan-GC sweep writes `blobTable`, `blobBytes` and `orphans` only through `doomOrphans(_:)`, which clears `state.core.orphans[key]` in the turn it dooms the key, so ruling R1's rule-1 exemption is not used (C-23); every other doom goes through `setResidency(_:of:dooming:)` (rule 3), and WP-C writes no other WP-B bookkeeping. Besides the primitives and the package API, WP-C calls WP-B's internal lifecycle helpers `awaitOpen()`, `checkLive()`, `checkWritable()`, `runUnit(on:)` and `waitForKey(_:)` (a purge's wait for an in-flight deletion) and reads two bookkeeping fields, `blobBytes` (the bytes a quarantine or a failure releases, ruling R7) and `commits.mutationSerial` (legacy phase 3's guard, C-24) (ADR-052). **Legacy migration (round 4)** adds four writes it cannot make any other way, because `adoptVerifiedFile` only marks *existing* records present, no primitive inserts records, and `runLegacyPhaseOne()` runs inside `open()`, where every async API (`apply` included) awaits `open()` (REENTRANCY R1): phase 1 installs the legacy manifest as `appliedManifest` (with `etag` `nil`) only while the applied manifest is empty; it inserts records only for ids that manifest lists and the index lacks, each as `absent(.neverFetched)` with `expectedBlob` (store.md §7 "merge into records"; ADR-029's "lookups hit adopted legacy files early" and the H-10 test `R-STO-11: lookups hit adopted legacy files while migration is still running…` depend on it); it sets `degraded` when the legacy `manifest.json` cannot be decoded (store.md §7). Phase 3 sets `syncDue`, directly or through `markSyncDue()`, when not every legacy entry was adopted (and `stamp` when every one was). Since the level-3 merge phase 1 builds these writes off the actor from snapshots and assigns them whole while `opening` (C-24), and a launch whose legacy manifest cannot be decoded adopts nothing: its files stay in the legacy root (C-13, NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW; ADR-052).
2. **WP-B → WP-C** only through two hooks, each run only on opt-in: `runLegacyPhaseOne()` from `open()` when `configuration.legacy.enabled`, and `applyEviction(_:)` from `apply` when it receives a plan, once per chunk of whole blobs (about `EvictionState.chunk` = 1,000 records) for a larger plan, the actor released between chunks behind the write fence (ruling R6, row c-8; ADR-052). WP-B's tests use `LegacyMigrationPolicy(enabled: false)` and `eviction: nil`, so WP-B is complete and green before WP-C exists. WP-B **reads** WP-C's contract fields (`state.cleanup.leases` in the reaper, `state.eviction.retentionScope` in `adoptionReport`, `state.eviction.chunk` in `apply`'s chunked eviction, ruling R6) and its codec persists WP-C's persisted ones (`migration.phase`, `migration.legacyStamp`, `cleanup.lastCleanupAt`, STAMPS section). `lastCleanupAt` is `StorageReport.lastCleanupAt` ("the last GC, eviction or purge"): only WP-C's own operations (orphan and staging GC, quota eviction, purge) stamp it, and only when the run removed, doomed, evicted or purged something (C-4; a purge is stamped once committed and again after its wait; ADR-052); the reaper's deletions of pruned blobs in `runDueWork()` are not a cleanup run and do not. `lastMaintenanceAt` was removed in round 4: no reader existed and no API the engine reaches set it.
3. **Deviations from the reviewer's list, with reasons.** `recordAccesses` moved to WP-B instead of WP-C: every acknowledged install (REENTRANCY R6, WP-B's snapshot path) delivers `InstallAck.accesses` to it, so a WP-C stub there would make WP-B's commit path untestable. The I-3 deletion machinery (doom → deletable → reap, `nextDeadline`, `runDueWork`) is WP-B's, because it lives in the blob table, acknowledgements and commits; WP-C dooms through `setResidency` and reaps through `reapDeletable`. The streaming hash is WP-B's (`publish` cannot exist without it). The requirement column of §11 follows: R-INT-1/2/5/6, R-CLN-1, R-CLN-7, P-6 and P-13 move from WP-C to WP-B, and R-CLN-5 becomes B (acknowledgement and grace) plus C (leases).
4. **What the merged WP-B store gives WP-C (ADR-050).** (a) `ensureOpen()` checks only the phase; WP-B's writing APIs also call the internal `checkWritable()` (throws `.storageUnavailable(.rootVanished | .ioFailure)`, B-15, B-34), so WP-C's mutating APIs call it too, or a read-only or replaced-root store mutates memory that no commit can persist (their `requireCommitted` throws). (b) `setResidency(.present)` is honoured only for a key that is live or doomed `unreferenced` and not in `publishing`; otherwise nothing changes (B-14, B-18). (c) `requireCommitted` waits while `apply`'s commit fence is up (row b-8); `applyEviction(_:)` runs synchronously inside the fence; for a plan larger than `EvictionState.chunk` records `apply` cuts the plan on the bulk executor (`ApplyEvictionChunks`: runs of one blob, each chunk ending at the first blob boundary at or past the chunk) and calls the hook once per chunk with a no-op metadata unit in between, behind the fence; a plan that names a blob apart is grouped by key and its report put back in plan order (ruling R6, row c-8; ADR-052). (d) Records ⊆ applied ids must hold after legacy phase 1: a record outside the applied manifest is persisted as *detached* (B-4). (e) A key in `publishing` may already be `live` while its name is replaced (B-18), so R10's sweep set `blobTable ∪ publishing` protects it. (f) The test-target seams of B-16 (`testSetLease`, `testClearLeases`, `testSetRetentionScope`, `testSetIntegrity`, `testBumpPurgeEpoch`, `testExpect`, `testInsertRecord`, and B-23's `testRaiseApplyFence`; internal, never public, never in `Sources/`) write WP-C's fields the way WP-C's APIs will; WP-C replaces them by its real APIs where those exist, and covers the package initialisers of `AssetLease`, `EvictionVictim`, `EvictionPlan` and `VerificationReport` through those APIs (G4: no coverage-only test; the orchestrator re-checks Storage per-file coverage after L3). Done at L3 (C-17; ADR-052): `testSetLease`/`testClearLeases` → `acquireLease`/`releaseLease` (`StoreDeletionTests`), `testSetRetentionScope` → `setRetentionScope` (`StoreReentrancyTests`), `testSetIntegrity` → two `recordIntegrityFailure` calls and `testBumpPurgeEpoch` → an empty `purge` (`StoreApplyTests`), their declarations deleted; `testExpect`, `testInsertRecord` and `testRaiseApplyFence` stay (no API replaces them).

### 1.2 Work-package plan (ADR-046)

Work packages run in **dependency levels**, each in its own git worktree at `.worktrees/<wp>` on branch `wp/<wp>` (ADR-009) with its own scratch path `.build-<wp>`; the orchestrator merges a level, in this order, before the next starts, and commits.

| Level | WP | Writes (sources) | Writes (tests, fixtures) | Benchmarks file (nested in `ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | H test support | `Sources/ZTronAssetTestSupport/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/TestSupport/**` | — |
| L1 | A model | `Sources/ZTronAssetModel/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/**` (not `ModelContractTests.swift`, `PackageScanContractTests.swift`) | `BenchModel.swift` (P-3) |
| L2 | B storage core | Storage `Store/`, `FileSystem/`, `Layout/`, `Snapshot/`, `Staging/`, `Index/`, `Blobs/`, `Lock/` (§1.1) | `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Core/**`, `Tests/Fixtures/index/**` | `BenchStorage.swift` (P-2, P-5, P-6, P-13), `MemoryProbe.swift` (§13.5) |
| L2 | D transport | `Sources/ZTronAssetTransport/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetTransportTests/**` (not `TransportContractTests.swift`) | `BenchTransport.swift` (P-10) |
| L2 | E Apple tier | `Sources/ZTronAssetAppleHosted/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetAppleHostedTests/**` (not `AppleHostedContractTests.swift`) | — |
| L3 | C integrity, cleanup, eviction, migration (on merged B) | Storage `Integrity/`, `Cleanup/`, `Eviction/`, `Migration/` (§1.1) | `Tests/ZTronAssetStorageTests/Cleanup/**`, `Tests/Fixtures/legacy-v0/**` | `BenchCleanup.swift` (C2 actor holds of WP-C's operations at 10,000 and 100,000 records; no P-n budget, the C2 rule's 10 ms; ruling R4, ADR-052) |
| L4 | F sync engine | `Sources/ZTronAssetSync/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/**` (not `SyncContractTests.swift`) | `BenchSync.swift` (P-4, P-7, P-11, P-14; ten C2 benchmarks of WP-F's actor turns at 10,000 members (the probe chain at 1,000) against the C2 rule's 10 ms, `c2Budget`, the level-3 ruling R4 precedent of `BenchCleanup` (ADR-052); ADR-053) |
| L5 | G facade | `Sources/ZTronAssetKit/**` | `Tests/ZTronAssetKitTests/**` (not `KitContractTests.swift`) | `BenchKit.swift` (P-1, P-8, P-9, P-12) |
| L5, after G | K compatibility shims | `docs/MIGRATION.md` (L5K-R10; ADR-055) | `Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests/**` (not `CompatibilityShimTests.swift`) | — |
| end | I docs | `docs/**` except `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `TRACEABILITY.md`, `adr-drafts/**` and `MIGRATION.md` (WP-K's, L5K-R10; ADR-055); `README.md` | — (WP-I owns no test since round 4: the package-wide scans it held are the orchestrator's) | — |

**Files WP-F added at L4 (ADR-053).** Sources (`ZTronAssetSync`): `Engine/` `DeadlineHeap.swift`, `EngineCatchUp.swift`, `EngineCommands.swift`, `EngineConditions.swift`, `EngineDispatch.swift`, `EngineEvents.swift`, `EngineFailures.swift`, `EngineJobs.swift`, `EngineLifecycle.swift`, `EngineManifest.swift`, `EngineOriginGate.swift` (the one pure evaluation of the origin's gates at `now`, F-40), `EnginePresence.swift`, `EngineReadmission.swift`, `EngineRuns.swift`, `EngineState.swift`, `EngineTimers.swift`, `InFlightBytes.swift`, `LaneQueues.swift`, `SubscriberQueue.swift`, and `Planning/PlanBuilder.swift`; bodies and internal members in the contract files `SyncEngine.swift`, `EngineSupport.swift` and `SyncPlan.swift`, no signature changed. Tests: `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/**` (among them the harness `EngineHarness.swift`, `FakePackBackend.swift`, a subset of WP-E's fake whose header names it and ADR-040 (L4-R1), the regression suites of the fix rounds and close-out cycles, and the gate model `EngineGateModelTests.swift`) and `Planning/{PlanFixture,PlannerPropertyTests}.swift`; `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchSync.swift` (P-4, P-7, P-11, P-14, ten C2 benchmarks and a prune-announcement ratio benchmark, §11). Authorised edits outside WP-F, ownership unchanged: `Package.swift` (`ZTronAssetSyncTests` gains a test-only `ZTronAssetAppleHosted` dependency for the end-to-end R-APL-3 test, L4-R1, orchestrator commit `7cf647c`; §13.5 issue 1), and WP-C's `Integrity/StoreIntegrity.swift` with two WP-C test files (L4-R5, L4-R16; §1.1).

**Files WP-G added at L5 (ADR-054).** Sources (`ZTronAssetKit`): `Facade/ClientLifecycle.swift` (the current store and engine pair, the single-flight reopen and the facade's prefetch registry, G-1), `Facade/EventForwarder.swift` (one consumer's event stream across reopens, G-2, G-16) and `Observability/DiagnosticReportBuilder.swift` (G-4); bodies and internal members in the contract file `Facade/AssetClient.swift` (its private `store` and `engine` replaced by the internal `lifecycle`, `mirror` internal), no public or package signature changed. Tests: `Tests/ZTronAssetKitTests/` `CompositionTests`, `EventContinuityModelTests` (the L5-R13 model), `FacadeBehaviourTests`, `FacadeLifecycleTests`, `FacadeRaceTests`, `FacadeReopenTests`, `FacadeUnitTests`, `FallbackChainTests`, `ObservabilityTests`, `PrefetchFacadeTests` and the harness `KitHarness` (73 tests; 85 in 11 suites with `KitContractTests`); `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/BenchKit.swift` (P-1, P-8 ×2, P-9, P-12). Authorised edits outside WP-G, ownership unchanged (`docs/adr-drafts/g.md` "Authorised edits outside WP-G (close-out)"): WP-F's `Engine/EngineState.swift`, `EngineCommands.swift` and `EngineJobs.swift` (the pending claims, L5-R1, L5-R15), `EngineLifecycle.swift` (the shutdown from `created`, L5-R2; claims cleared at shutdown, L5-R1, L5-R15), `EngineDispatch.swift` (`isReleasedOnly`'s guard only, L5-R21) and `SubscriberQueue.swift` (`EventSlot.clientEnd`, L5-R19), internal state and bodies only; WP-F's L4 tests `EngineUnitTests.swift` (`(.clientShutDown, .client)` → `(.clientShutDown, .clientEnd)`) and `EngineEventsTests.swift` (`#expect(slots.last == .client)` → `#expect(slots.last == .clientEnd)`, the bound unchanged), each assertion as strict as before (L5-R19); the new `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineGroupClaimTests.swift` (14 tests), `EngineCreatedShutdownTests.swift` (3) and `EngineClientSlotTests.swift` (1); WP-B's files (§1.1); and the orchestrator-owned `Tests/ZTronAssetKitTests/KitContractTests.swift` (`GatedRegistry.waitUntil` ends on cancellation, G-8, assigned by ADR-050's Level 2 close; assertions unchanged). None is a pre-existing CDN test, so `MIGRATION.md` does not apply (L5-R4, L5-R19).

**Files WP-K added at L5 (ADR-055).** Tests (`Tests/ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests/`, R-CAR-6): the harness `CompatHarness.swift` (`CompatHarness`, `AutoAdvancingClock`), the suites `ConfigurationShimTests` (9 tests), `LegacyAPIShimTests` (12), `LocalStoreShimTests` (10), `MigrationLaunchShimTests` (4), `NetworkMonitorShimTests` (5), `PriorArtCallSiteTests` (1), `ProviderShimTests` (7), `ResolverShimTests` (6) and `SyncCoordinatorShimTests` (14): 68 tests, 71 in 10 suites with the orchestrator's `CompatibilityShimTests` (3); the shims under `Shims/`: `BackgroundAssetsCDNProvider`, `CDNAssetResolver`, `CDNConfiguration` (with `CDNProviderFactory`), `CDNInstallPoint`, `CDNLocalStore`, `CDNShimError`, `CDNSyncCoordinator`, `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor` and `StaticManifestCDNProvider`; in the Stage 1 shim files only the `CDNProviding` doc comment of `Shims/CDNSyncProgress.swift` (`Shims/CDNAsset.swift` unchanged). Docs: `docs/MIGRATION.md`, WP-K's file, carved out of WP-I's `docs/**` (L5K-R10). Authorised edits outside WP-K, ownership unchanged (`docs/adr-drafts/k.md` "Authorised edits outside WP-K (close-out)"): WP-F's `Engine/EnginePresence.swift` (the pre-transfer read, §7.1: L5K-R1, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R18, L5K-R21, L5K-R23, L5K-R25), `EngineRuns.swift` (the parked launch run, the migration loop's re-arm, `batchCalls`), `EngineState.swift` (`storeDeadlineUpdates`, the `storeDeadlineReadHold` seam, `MigrationProgress.batchCalls`, `launchRunWaiting`'s doc), `EngineLifecycle.swift` (`reconcileLaunch` returning `Bool`, the re-arm after launch reconciliation's publication, shutdown releasing the seam), `EngineTimers.swift` (`applyStoreDeadline`, `fireDue`, `refreshStoreDeadline`, the test seam), `EngineCommands.swift` (the lease release restructured into a private `releaseLease(_:)`, the re-arms of a failed maintenance step and a failed `reportUnreadable`, the tripwire `invariantViolations(storeDeadlineDue:)` and `storeDeadlineSettled`), `EngineDispatch.swift` (the `publish` re-arms and L5K-R25's guard in `drive`), and `EngineFailures.swift` and `EngineManifest.swift` (re-arm additions L5K-R16's enumeration found: L5K-R16 authorises them, L5K-R28's list omits them), internal state and bodies only; WP-F's tests: the new `Tests/ZTronAssetSyncTests/Engine/EngineLegacyMigrationTransferTests.swift` (28 tests: 15 R-STO-11, 12 R-STO-13, 1 R-SYN-14) and `EngineModelTests.swift` (L5K-R16's tripwire after every operation and at idle, through `idleViolations()`, which returns a superset of the earlier check; no assertion weakened); WP-C's files (§1.1). No Kit or WP-B file changed. None is a pre-existing CDN test (those are mapped one to one in `MIGRATION.md` §3).

Shared and orchestrator-only: `Package.swift`, `.gitignore`, the contract files above (a WP implements bodies in them but changes no signature), every `*ContractTests.swift` (the package-wide scans of R-CAR-9, R-SEC-3 and R-ENV-2…5 among them, in `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/PackageScanContractTests.swift`), `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/{ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks,QualityBudgets}.swift`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `docs/TRACEABILITY.md`. B and C are sequential, yet their files stay disjoint (§1.1), so ownership is decided by path alone and a merge conflict is an ownership bug (CLAUDE.md P2). Three rules keep the shared documents conflict-free (round 4):
- **ADR drafts.** `docs/adr-drafts/<wp>.md` is one file per work package, owned by that package and carved out of WP-I's `docs/**`. A WP records its decisions there; the orchestrator numbers them into `DECISIONS.md` at merge, so no two worktrees edit one file.
- **REENTRANCY rows.** When a WP adds an await that no row of §10 covers, it proposes the row (await, what is re-validated) in its `docs/adr-drafts/<wp>.md` and cites it in the code comment (`// REENTRANCY: <wp>-n (proposed) — …`); the orchestrator copies it into §10 at merge, keeping the proposed identifier as its final number; the code keeps citing `<wp>-n (proposed)`, the word being historical (ADR-049 for h-n, ADR-050 for the level-2 rows, ADR-052 for c-n, ADR-053 for f-n, ADR-054 for g-n, ADR-055 for k-n). An await covered by a proposed row is not a review failure.
- **`docs/TRACEABILITY.md`** is regenerated by the orchestrator after each merge (`bash Scripts/verify.sh --gates-only` rewrites it). A WP restores it before handoff (`git checkout -- docs/TRACEABILITY.md`) whenever it ran `--gates-only` or `--full`, or runs only `--quick`, so branches of one level never conflict on its counts.

**iOS Simulator (owner, 2026-10-09; ADR-051).** Every test target builds and passes on the iOS Simulator as well as on macOS. Every test a WP adds follows ADR-051's gating convention (`docs/adr-drafts/ios.md` item 2: both platforms in every gate; on a `@Test` function or the helper that uses the fake, never on a `@Suite`; no new `#available` in `Sources/`; `ZTronAssetTestSupport` at the package floors), CPU-bound case loops yield once per case, benchmark files build for iOS too (the simulator test action builds the benchmarks bundle without running it), and the WP runs ADR-051's simulator test command (at least `build-for-testing`) before hand-off, on its own named simulator under `caffeinate -i`. `verify.sh` does not run it (`Scripts/` is frozen), so `VERIFY: PASS` alone does not show it.

## 2. Concurrency model and environment

- **Actors hold state.** `AssetStore` (Storage) and `SyncEngine` (Sync) are actors, and so are the facade's internal `ClientLifecycle` (the current store and engine pair, the reopen and the facade's prefetch registry; G-1) and `EventForwarder` (one per consumer event stream; G-2) (ADR-054). `AssetClient` is a `Sendable` final class with immutable stored properties. `SnapshotMirror`, `PrefetchWindowController` and `BackgroundEventsRelay` are `@MainActor` classes with `nonisolated init`, so the nonisolated `AssetClient.init` creates them (transport-2 T2-16).
- **One environment value** (SPEC §4.2, ADR-038): `AssetEnvironment` (Model) carries `FileSystem`, two `BlockingExecutor`s (metadata, bulk), `TimeSource`, `RandomSource`, `DeviceConditionsSource`, `RemoteSourceFactory`, `AppVersionSource`, `LegacyDefaults`, optional `MemoryPressureSource`, a `LogSink` and an optional `LogTap`. `.live()` is built in the facade, performs no I/O (P-12) and builds fresh conformers per call (ADR-038). **Logging (ADR-047):** every line goes through `AssetLog.emit(_:sink:tap:)` / `LogRoute`; `.live()` installs `OSLogSink` (the only writer to the unified log) and an environment assembled by hand defaults to `TapOnlyLogSink`, so tests never persist a line outside this repository; the route reaches the store (`StoreEnvironment.log`) and the remote sources (`RemoteSourceFactory.makeRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)`).
- **Blocking work** (ADR-003 as fixed by ADR-038): `BlockingExecutor.run(_:)` takes a bounded, non-throwing `@Sendable` unit; `run(throwing:)` rethrows typed errors through `Result`. Units: one rename, one ≤ 1 MiB read plus hash update, one index commit, up to 64 unlinks within `BlobDeletionUnit.budget` (1 ms) of the injected monotonic clock (§4.4; ADR-050), one listing. Live: `DispatchBlockingExecutor` (serial queue, `autoreleaseFrequency: .workItem`); tests: `InlineBlockingExecutor`, and `SteppedBlockingExecutor` to hold a unit at a known point (H-5). The live conformers outside the store run their blocking first reads in one unit too: the bundle enumeration (`AssetFallbackChain.prepare`) and `Info.plist` (`LiveAppVersionSource`) (ADR-038).
- **Time** (H-8): `TimeSource` has a wall clock (`now`, persisted stamps and LRU only), a monotonic clock (`monotonicNow`, every in-process deadline), an awake clock (`uptimeNow`, deletion grace only, ADR-017) and `sleep(until:)`. Tests use `ManualTimeSource`, and the deterministic suites never sleep for real (R-ENV-1's test of `LiveTimeSource` itself uses only past deadlines and a cancelled one); only benchmarks sleep on the live clock, among them the P-11 benchmark's loopback latency, P-9's `Heartbeat` on `LiveTimeSource`, and the Kit benchmarks, which run on `LiveTimeSource` and live dispatch executors over the fakes, so the store's commit windows pass in real time (L5-R7; ADR-054).
- **Randomness** (ADR-024): one seed; every draw is keyed by what is drawn.
- **Tasks.** The store owns no task (ADR-022). The engine owns: one alarm (iff its deadline heap is non-empty), one conditions watcher (iff work exists), one driver per transfer in flight, at most one run, one task per store-touching entry point (ADR-039), and one memory-pressure observer (iff a live `PurgeableCache` is registered, ADR-044). Every owned task is keyed by a unique spawn id. The foreground transport owns no long-lived task: its only unstructured tasks end by themselves (a handle's `cancel()` discards the partial and then cancels the data task in one short task; `SessionGate.invalidate` reads the session stream in one task until `.invalidated`). The background coordinator owns one pump (`Task.detached`) only while a background transfer is outstanding (transport-2 T2-10); each background handle control and each terminated handle stream hops to the coordinator in one short unstructured task, and a terminated conditions stream stops its observation in one short task (ADR-050). The facade owns, outside the engine, one pump task per active event stream (`EventForwarder`; it ends with its stream and is not engine-owned, so P-14 and R-SYN-14 do not count it), the single-flight reopen task (`ClientLifecycle`), and short hand-off tasks (the background-events hand-off, the memory-pressure relay's changes, `deinit`'s shutdown, a forwarder's `cancel()` hop, and a `PrefetchHandle`'s registration and release tasks) (G-1, G-2; ADR-054).
- **Unsafe budget** (C1, R-ENV-4): **0 of 3** escapes are used in all of `Sources/`, `ZTronAssetTestSupport` included (`UNSAFE escapes found=0`). Test fakes with synchronous APIs use `Synchronization.Mutex` behind `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)`; production uses it only inside the iOS 26-gated Apple tier, in exactly `PackTransferStop`, `HashingChunkSource` and `FileHandleChunkReader`, never `@unchecked`, with no await or I/O in a critical section (ADR-003 amendment, ADR-050); tests that use the fakes carry `@available(macOS 15.0, iOS 18.0, *)` on each `@Test` function (or on the helper type or function that uses the fake), never on a `@Suite`, and Apple-tier tests `@available(macOS 26.0, iOS 26.0, *)`; every gate names both platforms, because `@available(macOS N, *)` alone vanishes on iOS (ADR-014 as amended by ADR-051, verified on macOS and on the iOS Simulator). Memory probes for P-6/P-7 live in `Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks` (ADR-040). No planned escape exists; if a WP believes it needs one, it reports to the orchestrator first.
- **Typed throws** (ADR-004, ADR-042): public async APIs throw `AssetError` (`.shutDown` after shutdown, R-SYN-15); `withAsset` stays untyped; internal layers use `ManifestError`, `TransferError`, `StoreError`, `StagingError`, `PackError`, `FileSystemError`.

## 3. On-disk layout (R-STO-1, ADR-001, ADR-012, ADR-013)

The root is `<base>/ZTronAssetKit/v1/`, where `<base>` is `Library/Application Support` (default, excluded from backup, protection `completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`), `Library/Caches`, or a custom directory (`StorageLocation`). The R-CAR-6 shim `CDNLocalStore(baseDirectory: X)` (a test-target type the owner copies into ZTronCarouselCore, ADR-033) passes `.custom(X.deletingLastPathComponent())`, so its root is `<X's parent>/ZTronAssetKit/v1/` beside the prior art's `X`, which it migrates as the legacy root (K-6, NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW: storage location; ADR-055). Every URL is built by `StoreLayout`; no manifest-supplied text is ever a path component (R-SEC-2). This is every file the module creates:

| Path (relative to `<base>`) | Kind | Writer | Content and format | Write protocol | Read by | Removed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ZTronAssetKit/` | dir | `open()` | container | `createDirectory`, then barrier-sync of `<base>` (store-1 S1-02) | — | never by the module |
| `ZTronAssetKit/v1/` | dir | `open()` | the root; backup-excluded, protected | created, attributes applied and re-checked at every open (R-STO-8) | — | never |
| `v1/store.lock` | file | `open()` | empty; `O_EXLOCK` held for the store's lifetime; token records `(st_dev, st_ino)` | `acquireExclusiveLock` | kernel; identity re-checked before every commit (store-1 S1-08) | never |
| `v1/index.bin` | file | commit unit | the current generation: 64-byte header (magic `ZTAKIDX\0`, `schemaVersion` 1, flags, `generation`, `bodyLength` ≤ 64 MiB, `writtenAtMillis`, `storeID`, launch epoch, section count), sections STAMPS / STRINGS / RECORDS / DOOMED (with `DoomReason`), SHA-256 trailer (store.md §2.3 + ADR-012; STAMPS has no `lastMaintenanceAt` since round 4) | promotion of a loaded `index.tmp` first (§4.1 step 0) → temp → sync → rename `bin→prev` (conditional) → rename `tmp→bin` → root sync (§4.1; ADR-050) | load rule (§4.2) | replaced by the next commit |
| `v1/index.prev` | file | commit unit | the previous generation, same format | rename of `index.bin` (step 4, under the condition of §4.1); renamed back to `index.bin` (exclusive) when a commit's step 4 or 5 fails (ADR-050) | load rule (fallback) | replaced (step 4, under the condition of §4.1); removed on ENOSPC at commit only while `index.bin` is the file the store knows to hold the committed generation (ADR-015 as amended by ADR-050 / B-28) |
| `v1/index.tmp` | file | commit unit | the generation being committed | `write(.truncate)` + barrier (ordered) or full sync (durable) | load rule (may win, also when a killed process wrote it without syncing it; the next commit attempt syncs it before any rename, §4.1 step 0; ADR-050) | renamed to `index.bin` (step 5, or the promotion of step 0 when the launch loaded it), removed when its write or sync fails or by the ENOSPC fallback, or overwritten by the next step 2 (ADR-050) |
| `v1/blobs/` | dir | `open()` | content-addressed blobs | created; dirtied at every open (ADR-015) | — | never |
| `v1/blobs/<aa>/` | dir | publish, adoption | fan-out by the first two hex digits | created on demand; barrier-synced before the commit that references a blob in it | — | never |
| `v1/blobs/<aa>/<64 hex>.<ext>` | file | publish, legacy and Apple adoption | the asset bytes; name = SHA-256 of the bytes (I-1); `<ext>` re-validated `[a-z0-9]{1,8}` | claim → size → hash → sidecar removed → barrier → `rename(.exclusive)` (§4.3); a `corrupt` name is replaced (ADR-018) | readers via URL; launch reconciliation lists names only | the reaper, after ack + awake-clock grace + commit + no lease (§4.4) |
| `v1/staging/` | dir | `open()` | in-progress transfers | created | — | never |
| `v1/staging/<TransferID>.<epoch>-<seq>.part` | file | transport (data-task appends, ADR-019; background download moves) | partial or complete download bytes | `createPartial` / `appendPartial` / `continuePartial` (exclusive) / `adoptDownload` (exclusive) | `publish` (after claiming it), resume | staging GC (§4.5), `discard` |
| `…​.parttmp` | file | Apple-tier writer; the store's extra-extension copy (`bindCopy`, B-18; ADR-050) | a `.part` being written by a renaming writer, or a verified copy of a blob for a second extension, named `c-<hash>.<launch epoch>-<seq>.parttmp` with a store-allocated `seq` counting down from `UInt32.max`, hashed while copied and renamed into `blobs/` only with exactly `byteCount` bytes hashing to its name | write then exclusive rename to `.part` (the store's copy: placed into `blobs/`) | — | staging GC (also a copy a close left behind); the store removes a failed copy (B-26) |
| `…​.resume` | file | transport | `ResumeSidecar` JSON: name, `If-Range` validator, received and expected bytes; **no URL, header or key** | write `.resumetmp`, rename | resume planning, launch reconciliation | removed by `publish` before its barrier (store-2 S2-05), by GC, or when a complete `.part` of the same transfer exists |
| `…​.resumetmp` | file | transport | the sidecar's atomic-write temporary | — | — | staging GC |
| `…​.verifying` | file | `publish` | a `.part` claimed by the store: no producer writes this name (store-1 S1-12) | exclusive rename of `.part` | `publish` | renamed into `blobs/`, or removed on failure / at launch |

**Nothing else is written.** No `UserDefaults` (the legacy key is read once and removed after a durable "done" commit, ADR-029), no `tmp` (foreground transfers are data tasks, ADR-019), no URL cache (ephemeral, cache-less session). OS-owned files: a background download's temp file is moved into staging before the delegate returns (possibly after the store closed: it is then reconciled at the next launch, ADR-039); Apple packs are the OS's (their bytes reach `StorageReport.sourceCopyBytes` through `RemoteSource.sourceCopyBytes()`, ADR-027).

**Names.** `TransferID` is `c-<sha256>` (content with a checksum), `l-<sha256(id ‖ 0x00 ‖ remotePath)>` (checksum-less, ADR-034) or `m-manifest`. `epoch` is the store's launch epoch (the generation the launch commit carries; persisted, and unique only across launches whose launch commit succeeded over a readable index, §4.5; ADR-050), `seq` the engine's start counter (the store's own copies use sequences counting down from `UInt32.max`, B-18); so attempt tokens order across relaunches (across launches whose launch commit succeeded over a readable index, §4.5; transport-2 T2-06; ADR-050) and two starts never share a name (an exclusive rename reports `nameInUse` in the cases §4.5 lists). Anything in `staging/` that does not parse is foreign and collected; anything in `blobs/` that does not parse as `<64 lowercase hex>.<[a-z0-9]{1,8}>` in the right fan-out is foreign and deleted at launch, never adopted.

## 4. Storage (H-1, H-2, H-10; WP-B, WP-C)

store.md §1–§7 is the detailed design. Amendments from the reviews are normative and listed here.

### 4.1 Commit protocol (R-STO-4, I-5; ADR-015)
0. Promotion, only while the generation the launch loaded from `index.tmp` is pending (`CommitBookkeeping.pendingPromotion`): stat `index.tmp`; if it vanished, the promotion is complete without a commit and nothing new counts as committed; otherwise `synchronizeFile(index.tmp)` at the commit's level (a killed process may have written it without step 3), then steps 4–5 for it (with their condition and undo), then sync the root; only then does its generation count as committed, in `index.bin`. A failed sync renames nothing and leaves the promotion pending; only the stat decides that the file vanished (ADR-050).
1. Barrier-sync every fan-out that received or **adopted** a blob since the last commit, then `blobs/` if dirty (it is dirty after every open).
2. `write(index.tmp, .truncate)`. On ENOSPC remove `index.tmp`, and `index.prev` only while `index.bin` is the file the store knows to hold the committed generation (from the load's full validation or its own renames; never from a header) and a stat still finds it a file; then retry once (ADR-050).
3. `synchronizeFile(index.tmp, .barrier)` for an ordered commit, `.full` for a durable one.
4. `rename(index.bin → index.prev, .replacing)`, only when `index.bin` exists and either `index.prev` is absent or `index.bin` is the file the store knows to hold the committed generation (as in step 2); otherwise step 5 renames straight over the invalid, foreign or uncommitted `index.bin`, so `index.prev` keeps the committed generation. A step-4 rename that reports failure while `index.bin` is gone took effect and is undone (`rename(index.prev → index.bin, .exclusive)`) (ADR-050).
5. `rename(index.tmp → index.bin, .replacing)`; when it fails after step 4 moved `index.bin`, step 4 is undone (`rename(index.prev → index.bin, .exclusive)`, so nothing that did reach `index.bin` is overwritten): `index.bin` is never missing after a failed commit (ADR-050).
6. `synchronizeDirectory(root, .barrier)` (ordered) or `.full` plus `ZTronAssetKit` and `<base>` (durable).

The generation is allocated **when the state is captured**, before the unit is submitted, and is incremented for every attempt (store-1 S1-16, store-2 S2-13). DOOMED is derived inside the commit unit from the blob table captured with the state (C2), including the doom of every key whose deletion unit is in flight (`deletingDooms`) and of every key under replacement (`replacingDooms`: `live` in the table while a verified file replaces a `purged`/`corrupt` name, so a crash before that rename never adopts the rotten bytes; B-24) (ADR-018 as amended by ADR-050). Commits are coalesced to ≤ 4/s; the last commit of a run flushes as soon as the window allows (P-11 tail, transport RK-4). On ENOSPC at step 2 the fallback of step 2 runs and the write is retried once; the in-memory state stays authoritative and deletions that need the newer generation wait (ADR-050). The store carries which index name holds the committed generation (`CommitBookkeeping.committedFile`): set from the load's full validation once `open()`'s root barrier succeeded, `index.bin` after a successful commit or durable promotion, and followed through every rename a unit makes (a file renamed over it, or an `index.bin` that vanished, drops it). Nothing counts as committed until a root sync ordered the names the load read (the barrier, or a commit's step 6; B-36). The scheduled commit (`nextDeadline()`, `runDueWork()`) backs off after failed attempts: it opens at `lastAttemptAt + min(300 s, commitMinimumInterval × 2^failures)`; explicit callers (`requireCommitted`, hence `flush`, `apply`, `admit`, `markSynced`, `markSyncDue`, `adoptionReport` and checksum-less or rescue publications) wait only for the coalescing window, and `close()` commits durably at once; none waits for the backoff; only the first failure of a streak logs an error (B-29) (ADR-050).

### 4.2 Load rule
Read `index.bin`, `index.tmp`, `index.prev` in ≤ 1 MiB units; validate size (`64 + bodyLength + 32` exactly, after checking `bodyLength ≤ 64 MiB`), magic, schema and trailer; never trap (store-1 S1-04); a stat or read of an index file that fails with an error (a read that returns fewer bytes is torn, not an error) names the file unreadable; the launch is then degraded and read-only (§4.5): it commits nothing in that lifetime, every writing API throws `storageUnavailable(.ioFailure)`, and a later launch reads again (B-34; ADR-050). `nextGeneration = 1 + max(generation, launch epoch)` over headers that parsed (either ≥ 2⁶² makes the header malformed; ADR-050), computed with `addingReportingOverflow` (unreachable, since a header at or above 2⁶² does not parse; an overflow would saturate at that ceiling; ADR-050). Choose the newest valid generation; candidates whose `storeID` differs from the newest valid one's are ignored; when the chosen file is `index.tmp`, the committed generation is the newest valid one outside it (`survivingGeneration`) until a commit promotes it (§4.1 step 0) (ADR-050). `degraded` when none is valid, when `index.bin` exists but is invalid, when any candidate has a newer schema, when no index file exists but `blobs/` holds blobs, when the chosen generation carries the degraded flag, or when an index file was unreadable (B-6, B-34; ADR-050). In degraded mode the reaper deletes nothing at all (the doom set itself is untrusted, B-6) and orphans are protected until the next `apply` plus adoption (store-1 S1-11; ADR-050). At load, `DOOMED := DOOMED \ referenced` and the repair is counted. Per-entry quarantine diagnostics are not persisted: after a reload `ValidatedManifest.quarantined == []` while `quarantinedCount` is kept, so a diagnostic report built after a relaunch shows the count only (B-4; ADR-050).

### 4.3 Publication (R-STO-3, R-INT-1/2/6)
`publish(staged, binding: ids, expecting: identity, urgency: lane)`: (1) ids are validated one by one before any hashing: unknown and changed ids are skipped; `.unknownAsset`, `.assetFailed` or `.superseded` is thrown only when none remains; the returned dictionary lists the ids bound (B-11; ADR-050; sync.md X-3); (2) claim: `rename(.part → .verifying, .exclusive)`; (3) size check against `byteSize` and the cap, without hashing; (4) streaming SHA-256 in ≤ 1 MiB units on the bulk executor, cancellation-aware; (5) compare with the checksum (or record the digest for checksum-less entries, R-INT-5/6); (6) `removeItem(.resume)`; (7) `acquireReference(key)` (ADR-018: awaits an in-flight publication or deletion of the same key, rescues `doomed(.unreferenced)`, dedupes onto `live`); (8) barrier `.verifying`, create the fan-out, `rename(.verifying → blob, .exclusive)` (EEXIST: compare sizes; a `corrupt` key is replaced with `.replacing`); (9) after every await re-validate the records' identity: on a change throw `.superseded`, never return a URL (store-2 S2-15); (10) records `present`, integrity history cleared, `markDirty(.soon)`, snapshot published at the caller's `urgency` (§5). Steps 7–10 are `adoptVerifiedFile(at:digest:byteCount:for:)`, which legacy migration reuses (§1.1). Ids sharing a checksum but not an extension get one blob per extension: the destination key is acquired first, a live or rescuable one is bound in place, otherwise the placed blob is copied to a `.parttmp` of the store's own, verified and placed (B-11, B-18; ADR-050). The cap of step 3 is `StoreConfiguration.limits.maxAssetBytes` (`.oversize`, fed from `AssetClientConfiguration.limits`, ADR-048). Checksum-less and rescue publishes are committed before they return (store-1 S1-05); they share the ≤ 4/s coalescing window, so a checksum-less bulk sync publishes at most `concurrency × 4` assets per second (B-7, accepted for the legacy tier; a note for WP-F, ADR-050). Integrity failures delete the staged bytes, count `StoreMetrics.integrityFailures` and throw `.integrity`; the engine then persists the R-INT-3 bookkeeping through `recordIntegrityFailure`, one path for transport and store failures (ADR-032 as amended in round 3), so `publish` (WP-B) never calls a WP-C stub.

### 4.4 Deletion and the I-3 protocol (H-2; ADR-016, ADR-017, ADR-018, ADR-022)
`deletable(k)` ⇔ `doomed(k)` ∧ an acknowledged snapshot **of this epoch** without `k` exists ∧ `uptimeNow ≥ ackUptime + deletionGrace` ∧ the generation that dropped `k` is committed (counted only once a root sync ordered it, B-36) ∧ no lease ∧ no publication of `k` in flight ∧ a fresh no-op install after the deadline was acknowledged ∧ the store is neither degraded (B-6) nor unavailable (`rootVanished`, B-15; an unreadable index file, B-34) (ADR-050). A fresh install started after the deadline that comes back unacknowledged leaves the key acknowledgement-blocked with no deadline; every later `runDueWork()` retries the fresh install (B-8; ADR-050). Only time-blocked keys contribute to `nextDeadline()`; lease release, acknowledgement and commit completion re-arm the engine's alarm. The machinery (blob table, `deletable`, reap batches in `runDueWork()` and `reapDeletable(maxKeys:)`) is WP-B's; the lease condition reads `state.cleanup.leases`, which WP-C's `acquireLease`/`releaseLease` maintain (§1.1). Unlinks run in sorted order in units of at most 64 (`deletionBatchSize`) and at most `BlobDeletionUnit.budget` (1 ms) of the injected monotonic clock: a unit makes at least one unlink and, before each later one, stops when the time spent plus the slowest unlink it has seen would pass the budget; the keys it did not attempt go first into the next unit, unchanged (no backoff) (B-10; ADR-050). A failed unlink keeps the key doomed with backoff. Launch DOOMED keys (from a previous instance) enter the blob table as doomed and wait for the first ack plus grace (store-2 S2-07). Doom reasons `purged` and `corrupt` are never re-adopted or rescued. An orphan doomed by orphan GC (`doomOrphans`, never in any snapshot) carries `eligibleAt` = doom time + `deletionGrace` on the awake clock instead of an acknowledgement's, and still needs a fresh acknowledged install after that deadline, no lease and a store neither degraded nor unavailable; a publication (`acquireReference`) or an adoption before the reap rescues it. It is the only doom whose grace starts at the doom, and orphan GC's reap window relies on that (C-23, ruling R2; ADR-052).

### 4.5 Launch (store.md §2.6 as amended; ADR-028, ADR-029)
`open()`: resolve the base and create `ZTronAssetKit` and `v1` (syncing `<base>` after creating the container) → lock → apply attributes, create `blobs/`, `staging/` → load (§4.2) → list blobs (256 units) → **list nothing in staging yet** → reconcile records: a present record whose blob is missing or the wrong size becomes `needsHeal` and the bad name is doomed `corrupt`; a listed blob whose key is live, unreferenced, or doomed `unreferenced` (rescued) — never doomed `purged` or `corrupt` — is bound to every record that expects it (with its expected size) and accepts adoption: `absent(neverFetched|evicted|healing)`, `needsHeal` or stale (adoption dirties the fan-out); `apply`'s and `admit`'s adoption bind by the same rule except `absent(.evicted)`, which only the launch adoption takes (`ApplyPlan` does not list such records and `adoptKnownBlob` refuses them, so neither can undo an eviction; ruling R3, OI-15: kept literal at L3, so an evicted record whose new content is already live elsewhere is downloaded again), and `apply` binds a launch orphan only while `state.core.orphans[key]` still equals the size its re-stat confirmed (ruling R1) (B-12; ADR-050, ADR-052) → OS-purge detection resets the stamp (R-CLN-7) → legacy phase 1 (WP-C's `runLegacyPhaseOne()` hook, only when `configuration.legacy.enabled`, §1.1) → root barrier (the loaded generation counts as committed, in the file the load validated, only once this barrier succeeded; B-36) → the launch commit, ordered and made by every `open()` (it carries the launch epoch and promotes a loaded `index.tmp`, §4.1 step 0; a failure leaves the store open on the loaded state with the commit due), skipped when an index file was unreadable: that lifetime is read-only and degraded (B-34) → publish the first snapshot → delete foreign names under `blobs/` (ADR-050). Staging is listed only by the engine's launch reconciliation (§6.4), and collected after it.

**Launch epoch (ADR-050; B-5, B-34, B-36).** The launch epoch is the generation the launch commit carries. It is unique only across launches whose launch commit succeeded over a readable index: a read-only launch (B-34) computes `nextGeneration` without the header it could not read and may take the previous launch's epoch, and after a launch whose root barrier and launch commit both failed (B-36) a power loss can bring back older names, so a later launch may take that epoch again (a wiped root also starts again at 1). L3's staging reconciliation and staging GC (WP-C, WP-F) therefore never take epoch equality alone for "this launch" (or the engine stages nothing while the store is `storageUnavailable(.ioFailure)`); exclusive renames report `nameInUse` on a collision (ADR-013 as amended).

### 4.6 Cleanup and space (R-CLN-*)
Prune; orphan GC (`orphanGrace`; ADR-052, C-23): one run at a time (a concurrent call returns an empty result); after a full listing it decides off the actor; a key is swept only if unknown to the blob table and `publishing` both before the first listing unit and after the listing, expected by no record and older than `orphanGrace`, the store neither degraded nor unavailable; swept keys are **doomed** `unreferenced` (`doomOrphans`, §4.4), not unlinked, in chunks of 1,000 with a no-op metadata unit between, each chunk only while the reap window is closed (else the run stops and the next collects, OI-12); the reaper unlinks them under §4.4 and a publication or adoption rescues them; the result, `gcBytes` and the stamp count bytes released to the reaper. Staging GC (`stagingClaimGrace`, `stagingMaxAge`; ADR-052, C-9): the caller's claims only, a name of ours older than `stagingMaxAge` goes even when claimed, foreign names and non-files at once, launch epochs never compared. Quota eviction (ADR-052; C-1, C-2, C-3, C-21): candidates are blobs, evicted with all their records; entries outside the retention scope first, then LRU; a run evicts to make room only when `StoragePolicy.evictionPermitted`, ADR-048, while maintenance quota eviction always runs; ties larger first, then key (hash, then extension); never essential, pinned, leased, being placed or accessed within `max(minimumResidency, deletionGrace)`; the need is `bytesToFree(used, available, pendingReclaim)`: the budget resolved over usage + free space + pending reclaim, triggering when usage exceeds it or free space + pending reclaim is below the reserve, evicting down to `min(hysteresis × budget, used − reserve shortfall)` (store-2 S2-06, C-2; `FreeSpace.budgetBytes` (WP-B, reported) resolves without the pending reclaim, so the two differ for a fractional budget); candidates are computed off the actor and marked in chunks of whole blobs, each ending at the first blob boundary at or past `EvictionState.chunk` (1,000) records with a no-op metadata unit between, each re-validated against its holders read with at most one scan of the records per turn; every byte any eviction, `apply`'s plan, a purge, a quarantine, a recorded failure or a record's last integrity failure released since the run measured counts toward its need (`EvictionState.releasedBytes`, C-2 cycles 6–7, ruling R7; residual: `apply`'s prune, OI-14); Apple-adopted assets are not ranked after ordinary ones (records carry no tier, C-6); the result counts bytes released to the reaper (C-3). `apply` evicts an eviction plan larger than `EvictionState.chunk` records one chunk of whole blobs per actor turn behind its write fence, through the unchanged hook `applyEviction(_:)`, which re-validates each chunk's victims in that chunk's turn; the bytes each chunk dooms count toward a running maintenance eviction's need (ruling R6, row c-8; ADR-052). Purge (ADR-052, C-5): `purgeEpoch` bumped and the stamp cleared with `syncDue` set before its first own await, both again in the turn of the last chunk when it purged something; the selection is grouped and sorted off the actor and applied in chunks that end at blob boundaries (an `.evictable` blob is skipped whole when a current holder became essential or pinned or the blob leased or being placed); it returns after the selection is reaped, rescued or deferred by a lease, its reclaim pass chunked, also waiting (key waiters) for a blob of its selection that another reaper run is deleting; a purge during legacy migration leaves records still waiting for a legacy file (`absent(.neverFetched)`) to later batches. Quarantine (`corrupt` doom). Deep verification (ADR-052, C-8): cursor by `verifiedAt`, the pass tracked in memory, the due list built off the actor; a hash whose blob incarnation changed is discarded and the key stays due. `lastCleanupAt` is stamped only by a run that changed something (C-4; ADR-052). Every operation is idempotent and cancellation-safe (store.md §6.9).

### 4.6a Retention scope and the adoption report (H-7; ADR-027)
`setRetentionScope(_:)` (in-memory, any phase, starts as `.all`, pushed by the engine after `open()` and on every app change) orders eviction and defines `AdoptionReport.excludedRemotePaths`; it deletes and fetches nothing. `adoptionReport()` is atomic: it captures the applied manifest's remote paths, the present-at-current-content records and the exclusions synchronously, commits the holding generation **durably**, and returns only what it captured (REENTRANCY R15), so a pack is released only for adoptions that survive power loss (store-1 S1-10).

### 4.7 Legacy migration (H-10; ADR-029)
Phase 1 inside `open()` through the `runLegacyPhaseOne()` hook (plan from `manifest.json` and `local-checksums.json` decoded under `StoreConfiguration.limits` with duplicates kept first, driven by a **non-following** recursive listing; entries matched against listed regular files, store-2 S2-10; duplicates keep the first; one ordered commit). Phase 1 installs the legacy manifest as the applied manifest (no ETag) while none is applied and inserts `absent(.neverFetched)` records with `expectedBlob` for ids the index lacks; an undecodable legacy manifest sets `degraded` instead (§1.1 rule 1, round 4). Phase 2 after `open()` in engine-owned batches (`migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)`, hashing with `hashFile(at:)` and adopting with `adoptVerifiedFile`, §1.1), with adopt-on-demand for a resolve or lease and, while migration is pending, for every job's pre-transfer read (`adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)`, §7.1; L5K-R1, ADR-055). Phase 3 sets the stamp (every entry adopted) or `syncDue`, then commits durably; phase 4 tree removal, then the key. The stamp migrates only when every legacy entry was adopted.

**Level-3 amendments (ADR-052; C-12…C-15, C-24, rulings R5, R9).** (1) Phase 1 is skipped (nothing written, the stamp kept) when the lifetime cannot write, the root cannot be stat-ed, or the root overlaps the store's directory (path and identity comparison, the `storeHolds` walk and `reachesStore`; fan-out directories of `blobs/` are excluded as harmless; C-13). (2) An I/O error on a stat, on a read of `manifest.json` or `local-checksums.json`, a short chunk read or a listing (or either stat of a listing unit) decides nothing in that lifetime: no write, no commit, no phase change, the key kept (C-13). (3) Decoding, planning, phase 1's record writes and the reversed queue are built in one bulk unit from snapshots and assigned whole while `opening` (C-24). (4) An undecodable or unusable legacy manifest degrades the launch and adopts nothing, and its files stay in place (C-13); a checksum-less entry without a local checksum is dropped (C-12); both are deviations, NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW. (5) Phase 2 commits the phase-1 plan before the first move; a file-system error other than not-found or cross-device, or a full volume, defers the file (back to the head of the queue, the batch ends); after 3 deferrals in one lifetime the file is kept in place, counted dropped and excluded from phase 4 (C-14 cycle 2); a root on another volume is dropped (C-14); both NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW. (6) Phase 3 migrates the stamp only when the applied manifest is still the one phase 1 decoded (`manifestDigest`) and every legacy entry is present, never over a stamp the engine wrote; its check runs in chunks of 5,000 entries under `commits.mutationSerial`, and after 3 interrupted passes the call decides nothing (`remaining == 0`, `finished == false`) (C-15, C-24). (7) Phase `done` is committed durably before phase 4 on every call; phase 4 removes only what the prior art wrote (the entries' files, `local-checksums.json`, the entries' directories deepest first, `manifest.json` last, the root only if empty; never recursively, never a kept file, nothing after a failed read or listing), then the key, only once the tree is done (C-15, NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW). (8) The caller (WP-F) calls `migrateLegacy` again while `!progress.finished`, backing off (for example to the next maintenance window) when a call returns `remaining == 0 && !finished` (ruling R9; RE-S29).

**Level-5 WP-K amendments (ADR-055; L5K-R13, L5K-R14, L5K-R17, L5K-R18, L5K-R20, L5K-R23).** (1) `adoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)` publishes at `urgency` (`.userInitiated` by default; the engine passes `.bulk` for a job of the bulk or prefetch lane, §7.1); a `.userInitiated` call installs before it answers an asset that is present already too, since the adoption that made it present may be in a coalesced snapshot only (L5K-R18), and a call that waited for another processing publishes at its own urgency. (2) One adoption per legacy file, whoever asks: `MigrationState.adopting` marks a file whose verified bytes a batch or an adopt-on-demand is placing and binding, set in the turn the call decides and cleared on its every exit; `MigrationState.onDemand` marks an adopt-on-demand's whole processing, from its claim to its published snapshot (installed at once only at `.userInitiated`). An adopt-on-demand that meets either mark waits and starts again (row k-5), sharing that processing's hash and answering the location its adoption bound; a call whose own hash ended, successfully or not, waits out another adoption of the file before it decides (row k-6), so a `notFound` read caused by the other call's rename, or its key in `publishing`, reads as `alreadyAdopted`; a file a batch holds but has not finished hashing stays adoptable at once (the two hashes may overlap, one adoption binds); a processing that ended without an outcome (its caller cancelled, a transient failure that deferred the file) leaves the file to the waiter, which processes it itself; an asker answers `nil` only when there is no prior-art file to adopt, when the file was dropped or kept, or when its own attempt deferred it, never for a failure the other adopter caused; `close()` resumes every waiter through the processing it waits for (`docs/adr-drafts/k.md` enumerates every state and pair of processors). (3) A call that finds the queue empty and every claimed file an adopt-on-demand's waits for one such processing at a time instead of returning at once, then re-reads the queue, the claims and the phase (row k-7): once no file is left it finishes the migration itself; when a processing it waited for ended without an outcome for the file (its caller was cancelled, or a transient failure deferred the file), the file is back in the queue and the call returns with `remaining > 0`, for the next call to process it (L5K-R17). (4) No call suspends behind its own coalesced publication (L5K-R20, L5K-R23): a batch that leaves files to process publishes at bulk urgency, the last batch (nothing queued or claimed) at `.userInitiated`, installed at once; when a publication left the trailing install due, what is left is read again after its await: with files left (queued, or claimed by an adopt-on-demand) the call returns right after it with `remaining > 0`, and with none it installs at once before phases 3 and 4, so (8)'s back-off signal never follows a batch that left nothing. (8) and RE-S29 are unchanged.

## 5. Snapshot and the main-actor mirror (R-STO-13; ADR-016)

`AssetSnapshot` (`package`) holds only present assets with precomputed `AssetLocation`s; lookup is one dictionary probe plus a case-insensitive extension match. `SnapshotMirror.install` swaps only for a greater `SnapshotKey(epoch, version)` and returns `InstallAck { installed, accesses, retired }`; the store counts an acknowledgement only when the sink swapped in exactly the offered snapshot of its own epoch (`retired != nil` and `installed == offered`); a refusal never counts, even in favour of a newer snapshot of the same store (B-17; ADR-050); `retired` lets the store release the replaced snapshot off the main actor (sync-2 S2-12); `reset()` is explicit: at shutdown, and by the facade after a `rootVanished` reopen, before the replacement store's first install (B-17; WP-G's `ClientLifecycle`, G-1, §8; ADR-054): a replacement store's epoch comes from the index files alone and can repeat or be smaller than the mirror's, so without the reset readers keep the old store's snapshot (ADR-050). The engine never hands out a URL derived from a snapshot copy (store-2 S2-12).

**Install rate (S2-12, P-8; ADR-016 as amended in round 3).** The store is the only caller of `SnapshotSink.install`. `publish(_:binding:expecting:urgency:)` takes the lane of the work that produced the file: `.userInitiated` installs before `publish` returns; `.prefetch` and `.bulk` install at most once per `StoreConfiguration.snapshotInstallInterval` (from `SyncTuning.snapshotInstallInterval`, default 0.1 s ⇒ ≤ 10 Hz, clamped to ≥ 0 by `SyncTuning.clamped()`, passed by `AssetClient.storeConfiguration(for:)`). A publication inside the window marks a trailing install due: `nextDeadline()` reports `lastInstall + interval`, the engine's alarm calls `runDueWork()`, which installs and reports `StoreWorkReport.installedSnapshot` (ADR-022). A user-initiated install also flushes any pending bulk changes (it is a whole-state snapshot). `apply`, `purge`, `reportUnreadable` and `adoptLegacyNow` at its default `.userInitiated` install at once (the engine's pre-transfer read of a bulk or prefetch job passes `.bulk`, L5K-R14); migration batches at bulk urgency, a batch returning right after a publication that left the trailing install due while files remain once that publication returned; otherwise, and for the last batch, which phases 3 and 4 follow in the same call, it installs at once (L5K-R20, L5K-R23; ADR-055). A 10,000-asset bulk sync therefore costs at most 10 snapshot builds and main-actor hops per second instead of one per commit, while the page the user just opened appears at once; the returned `AssetLocation`s come from actor state either way (ADR-016). Regression test: `R-STO-13: bulk publishes install at most one snapshot per snapshotInstallInterval and a user-initiated publish installs at once` (WP-B).

**During legacy migration, and the trailing install (ADR-055; L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R20, L5K-R21, L5K-R24, L5K-R25).** The bound holds during legacy migration too (L5K-R14). The engine schedules every coalesced publication's trailing install: after every store call that can leave an install due it re-reads `nextDeadline()` into its heap (RE-S40; L5K-R16's enumeration, §7.10), skipping the read in O(1) while its alarm already wakes for the store within about two install intervals (F-12), so a coalesced publication reaches the readers' snapshot within about two install intervals (0.2 s by default) unless a commit is still pending. A store call that publishes at a coalesced urgency returns right after that publication (L5K-R20); the one exception, a doom's removal-only install ahead of `recordFailure`'s and `recordIntegrityFailure`'s ordered commit, holds back no adopted or committed asset (I-3 keeps the bytes until a snapshot without the asset is acknowledged; L5K-R25). A user-initiated asker the pre-transfer read answers from the store is answered only once the readers' snapshot holds the asset (L5K-R21, §7.1); the engine's other answer sites (the commit path, `ensureAvailable`'s present members, the resolve's fast hit, joins answered from the ledger) keep ADR-016's lag: their answer, a location valid at once, can precede the readers' snapshot by at most about two install intervals (L5K-R24; Stage 3 holds an optional `installIfDue()`, ADR-055).

## 6. Transport and the Apple tier (WP-D, WP-E)

### 6.1 Sessions (R-NET-2, ADR-019)
**WP-D owns every body in `ZTronAssetTransport`**, including those implemented in Stage 1 (`HTTPRemoteSource`'s initialisers, `SessionDelegate.init`, `ForegroundTransferBackend.init`/`deinit`/`makeConfiguration`, `makeBackgroundBackend`); it changes no signature. One ephemeral, cache-less foreground session per `HTTPRemoteSource`, built in `start()`; `httpMaximumConnectionsPerHost` clamped 1…8 (P-10); no `httpAdditionalHeaders`; `waitsForConnectivity = false`; request timeout on the session **and** every request. Data tasks; the stateless `SessionDelegate` (immutable `let`s, `Sendable` without escapes) appends each chunk into staging through `StagingSink` on the delegate queue; per-task routing through the task's `TaskMailbox`. **Per-task decisions live on the task (D-1; ADR-050):** before `cancel()` the delegate writes a `TaskMarker` (`ztak-marker <verdict>`) into `taskDescription`, an atomic `copy` property (NSURLSession.h:785) that is free on foreground tasks because they route through their mailbox. Delegate verdicts (`oversize`, `exceeds`, `staging`, `range`, `final`, `nothttp`) overwrite a `pause` or an earlier verdict but never a `discard`; a `pause` (and the `discard` of a terminated consumer stream) is written only onto an unmarked task; the client's `discard` replaces only a `pause`. The completion honours the marker whatever ended the task: `discard` is always `cancelled(.byClient)` with no sidecar, and a verdict is applied before the URL error is classified. A handle's `cancel()` marks `discard`, discards the partial in one short task and only then cancels the task, so `cancelled(.byClient)` follows the discard; a `cancel()` on a completed task does nothing. **Task creation and invalidation meet on one `SessionGate` actor per backend (D-11):** `start` creates the data task with its priority, expected size, stream termination and mailbox inside the gate and is refused (an already-terminal `cancelled(.byClient)` handle) once invalidation began; `invalidate` calls `invalidateAndCancel()` once and one task reads `sessionEvents` to `.invalidated`, resuming every waiting caller. The production source builds the backend with the package initialiser `ForegroundTransferBackend.init(delegate:maxConnectionsPerHost:requestTimeout:appKey:log:)`; the public initialiser builds a keyless request factory (D-7). `invalidate()` returns after the foreground delegate's last callback (transport-2 T2-04), which is what keeps staging writes from landing after `store.close()`: `StagingSink` is immutable and promises nothing after close (ADR-039); `deinit` invalidates too (T2-19). **Session-level events** have no task mailbox: each `SessionDelegate` creates, in its `init`, one `AsyncStream<SessionEvent>` (`package let sessionEvents`) and its continuation (`sessionEventSink`, internal), both `Sendable`, so the delegate stays escape-free (round 3; it compiles in this round's debug, release and iOS builds under `-warnings-as-errors`). `didBecomeInvalidWithError` yields `.invalidated`, which `ForegroundTransferBackend.invalidate()` awaits; in `.background` mode the download-completion and `urlSessionDidFinishEvents` callbacks yield `.finished` and `.backgroundEventsFinished`. WP-D adds the delegate methods in `Session/`. The source logs `transport` lines through its `log: LogRoute` (ADR-047).

### 6.2 Redirects and the key (R-NET-4, R-SEC-1)
`RedirectPolicy.decide`: refuse past 5 hops, refuse non-HTTPS (plain HTTP only for loopback under the package-only `loopbackTesting` policy), refuse hosts outside the allow-list; keep the key only while every hop stays on the original origin, never re-add it. The key is attached per request by the request factory after `TransportSecurityPolicy.check`. A 403 after we withheld the key is `rejected(.keyWithheldOnRedirect)` (transport-1 TR1-6). No `URLRequest`, `URLResponse`, `URLError` or `NSError` is stored in any error, event, report or log; only `ResponseFacts`. On the background path the coordinator's request factory carries the key only when `keyRedirectExposure == .acceptedByOwner` (`makeBackgroundBackend` passes `nil` otherwise), independently of routing, which keeps every key-bearing request in the foreground under `.refuse`; so no background request can carry the key under the default even if a routing defect sent one there. The OS follows background redirects without asking, so a finished background download whose final origin differs from its original one is refused as `redirectRefused(.unenforceableInBackground)` and nothing is adopted (D-7; ADR-026 as amended by ADR-050).

### 6.3 Manifest, size, resume (R-NET-5/7/8)
Conditional GET with `If-None-Match`, 304 → `notModified`, never a cache answer; the 32 MiB cap enforced while streaming. `SizeGuard.verdict` aborts on written > cap, announced > cap + slack, or written > `byteSize` (TR1-8); the staged size is compared with `byteSize`, never `Content-Length`. Resume (ADR-050; D-2…D-5): `Range: bytes=<offset>-` + `If-Range: <strong ETag | Last-Modified>` from our sidecar. A sidecar is written (pause, connection loss or timeout) only for checksummed content, only after a 200/206 that carried a strong `ETag` or a `Last-Modified` (1…256 printable bytes), and only when the partial holds bytes; `paused(resumable:)` and `connectionLost(resumable:)` report whether it was written (D-5). The staged size and the sidecar's size are read back from staging, never counted in memory (D-2). A 206 to a resumed request continues the earlier partial only when its `Content-Range` starts at the offset and the partial (moved with `continuePartial`) is exactly that long; a 206 without `Content-Range`, another start or a vanished earlier partial is `transient(.resumeRejected)`, as are −1002 and 416 on a resumed request; a 200 answering a resumed request (validator changed) takes over the old partial's name and restarts it from zero (D-3; TR1-4, T2-07, T2-08). A 206 or 416 to a request that did not resume is `rejected(.unexpectedStatus)` (the engine retries `resumeRejected` at once, so this avoids a loop, D-4); a 3xx delivered as final is `redirectRefused(reason)` recomputed from `Location` and the hop count, `.invalidLocation` without a usable `Location`; any other 1xx/2xx is `unexpectedStatus`. The announced length is `nil` under any `Content-Encoding` other than identity and offset + `Content-Length` (saturating) for a 206; non-success bodies are counted, never staged, and past 64 KiB the task is stopped and still classified by status. Checksum-less transfers are never resumed (TR1-3), on either path. Resumed bytes are always hashed. Background continuations of URLSession resume data: §6.4.

### 6.4 Background session and launch reconciliation (R-NET-3, H-6; ADR-026, ADR-028)
Off by default; bulk and key-free only unless the owner accepts redirect exposure. **Production wiring (L2, ruling D-14.1; ADR-050):** `HTTPRemoteSource(configuration:staging:log:)` builds its background backend with `makeBackgroundBackend(_:staging:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:makeDriver:)` (every added parameter defaulted, so the contract call `makeBackgroundBackend(_:staging:policy:)` is unchanged; `makeDriver` defaults to `LiveBackgroundSessionDriver` and lets tests pass a fake): `nil` for `.disabled` or an invalid namespace, else a `BackgroundTransferCoordinator(driver:staging:options:policy:appKey:requestTimeout:log:)` over that driver and its own `.background` `SessionDelegate`; the key reaches it only under `.acceptedByOwner` (§6.2); building it creates no `URLSession` (the driver's `activate()` does, after the store opened, ADR-028). WP-D owns the coordinator's and the driver's bodies.
