The device-only driver (`BackgroundSessionBackend.swift`, ≤ 250 lines, excluded from coverage) is decision-free; the coordinator, tag codec, reconciler and relay are tested on macOS with `FakeBackgroundSessionDriver`. Launch order: open → source → `reattachedTransfers()` (which activates the background session itself: the coordinator calls `driver.activate()` before snapshotting tasks; `RemoteSource` has no `activate` member) → `stagingInventory()` → `TransferReconciler.plan(live:staged:wanted:)` (`wanted` = applied entries not present) → execute → `collectStaging(keeping:)`. `AssetClient.handleBackgroundEvents` stores UIKit's handler in the relay and completes it after the engine reports the session's preceding events staged (at once after shutdown), through the facade's reopen path, so an engine a reopen retires meanwhile is replaced rather than completing the handler before the replacement made the session (G-5; ADR-054). Shutdown releases the background session without waiting for the OS's tasks (`finishTasksAndInvalidate`); invalidation itself cancels no OS task, but the engine cancels its drivers before `remote.invalidate()` (ADR-039) and terminating a background handle's stream cancels its OS task (the Model's drop-to-cancel rule), so an explicit `shutdown()` cancels in-flight background downloads (best effort: a cancellation hop that reaches the coordinator after `invalidate()` began cancels nothing), while a killed or suspended process cancels nothing (D-10, **NEEDS-HUMAN-REVIEW**; ADR-039 as amended by ADR-050); a file the OS moves into staging after close is an ordinary staged file for the next launch's reconciliation (ADR-039).

**Routing (ADR-026 as amended; D-8, D-15, D-19.4; ADR-050).** `HTTPRemoteSource.transfer` uses the background backend only for `.backgroundEligible` + `.bulk` + `.asset` requests without a Range resume, whose `byteSize` is known, ≥ 0 and ≤ the cap (`TransferJob.backgroundByteSize`: the OS downloads a background task whole, so only the staged size can be checked), that carry no key unless the owner accepted redirect exposure, and that the coordinator admits through `startIfAdmitted(_:)`, which checks the cap and reserves a slot in one actor turn (not disabled for the process by −995/−996/−997, not invalidated, live + reserved < `maxTasks`, re-attached tasks included, T2-17); `nil` sends the transfer to the foreground and leaves background enabled. **Coordinator (D-9).** Attach-or-create by content: a buffered staged outcome of the job's `byteSize`, else the newest live OS task for that content that no cancel or pause is ending and whose terminal was not processed, else a new tagged download; a new download whose attempt name is still attached or being cancelled is refused `localIO(EEXIST)`; a joined task's staged file of another size is delivered `integrity(.truncated)`/`integrity(.sizeMismatch)` and stays in staging for the engine's discard or staging GC. Controls act only while the handle's attach token still holds the name. Unclaimed successes are buffered (oldest dropped past `maxTasks`, files left for reconciliation), unclaimed failures dropped; `reattached()` reports live tasks (`isRunning: true` for running and suspended) plus the buffered outcomes and hands those over. A download finished while the app was dead is reported by `reattached()` deterministically only in the background-relaunch case; on a foreground launch it is best-effort and the store's staging inventory adopts the file anyway (ADR-028), so WP-F's end-to-end R-NET-3 test must not depend on `reattached()` reporting it. **Pause and resume (T2-05, ruling D-14.2).** A pause records the intent before `driver.cancel(…, producingResumeData: true)` and ends `paused(resumable: data != nil)`; the data is kept in memory only (newest `maxTasks`) and only for checksummed content (a checksum-less pause reports `resumable: false`); the engine's next attempt of that content continues from it once (`startDownload(…, resumeData:)` → `downloadTask(withResumeData:)`). The task tag `ztak1 <name> <size | -> [resume]` records a continuation (`BackgroundTaskTag.forStart`, both drivers), so in every process the background delegate stages a 206 with `Content-Range` only for such a task of checksummed content (as `resumed: true`; the OS's file is the whole body, compared with `byteSize`, hashed at publication) and reads its −1002, 416 or range-less 206 as `resumeRejected`; any other 206 is `unexpectedStatus(206)` (D-19.1, D-19.2).

### 6.5 Reachability and power (R-NET-9)
`LiveDeviceConditionsSource` builds one `ConditionsObservation` actor per `conditions()` call: it owns a `PathObserving` (live: `NWPathMonitor` behind `LivePathObserver`, whose marker `Sendable` conformance is iOS 17+ and not enforced, APPLE-API-VERIFICATION #7) and the power and thermal notification tokens, observed on the injected `center` through an `OperationQueue` over the one serial queue the path observer also uses. The path observer starts before the notification observers, so the first element is the path observer's first delivery; every element is a full snapshot (`.bufferingNewest(1)`); terminating the stream stops the observation. `ConditionsMapping` is pure (implemented, tested). **Test seam (WP-D, implemented; ADR-050):** the package `init(center:makePathObserver:power:)` takes a scripted path observer and power reading, so both halves are driven without the network; `ObservationRegistry` counts active observations (`activeObservations`, `waitForObservations(equalTo:)`, event-driven), so termination is provable; `livePathObserver` lets the required test `R-NET-9: the live conditions source yields the current state first, then on change, without polling` wrap the live observer and assert power readings = path deliveries + posts on the observation's queue (posts go to the source's injected `center`, never `.default`). A notification arriving between `NWPathMonitor.start` and its first update can still read the started monitor's `currentPath` (device-only residual, D-18.5).

### 6.6 Apple tier (R-APL-*, H-7; ADR-027)
Everything runs against `PackBackend`; only `LiveAssetPackBackend.swift` (≤ 250 lines, excluded) imports BackgroundAssets, using the iOS 26.0 surface. The installed catalog is read from the essential pack first; the best-effort update check is then scheduled in a source-owned task, single-flight, and never awaited, and its `removedSources` reach the next fetch, once each, on `notModified` too (T2-14, T2-20, E-3; ADR-050). The digest, `"sha256:<64 lowercase hex>"` with the double quotes included, is the pseudo-ETag (E-2). Assets are streamed into staging and published through the hash path; skew is `transient(.sourceVersionSkew)` (T2-12). `PackReleasePlanner` (implemented) releases a media pack only when every catalog entry in it is adopted in a durably committed generation or excluded by the keep-scope; its inputs come from `AssetStore.adoptionReport()` (§4.6a), the keep-scope being the client's retention scope. A pack is released at most once per pack per catalog generation (the catalog pseudo-ETag) and never re-armed within one (ADR-027 as amended by ADR-050); the release survey runs in the caller's task in slices of 1,000 entries, off the state actor (E-16). A pack id can appear in `ReclaimReport.released` again in a later generation, so the engine treats `released` as "removed now", not as a set it accumulates (E-10). `sourceCopyBytes()` sums `PackBackend.installedBytes` for `StorageReport` (`nil` and negative values count 0; the sum saturates at `Int64.max`) over every media pack holding adopted content in the last `AdoptionReport`, released or not, every pack the source released in this process (a released pack reports no bytes; one that came back after its release, restored by the OS or downloaded again on demand, stays installed, and counted, until a reclaim in a later catalog generation releases it), and every pack whose planned removal has not yet succeeded (this third set lands with the L2 follow-up `wp/l2fu`). The set lives in memory, so a fresh source reports 0 until its first `reclaimSourceCopies` (E-11; ADR-050). BackgroundAssets cannot honour `TransferRequest.access` / `ManifestRequest.access`: the tier ignores them by necessity, and the engine gates Apple-tier dispatch and update-checking manifest fetches on its own `DeviceConditions` (E-17; WP-F; ADR-050). **The package's single `#available`** (all of `Sources/`, `ZTronAssetTestSupport` included, ADR-014) is in `AppleHostedTier.resolve`, which feeds `TierSelection.select` (pure, Model, tested over all 16 inputs).

### 6.7 Loopback server (ADR-011)
An actor over `NWListener` bound to `127.0.0.1`: `requiredLocalEndpoint` 127.0.0.1 with any port and `requiredInterfaceType = .loopback`; `acceptLocalOnly` is deliberately not set, because the iOS Simulator resets every 127.0.0.1 connection to a listener that sets it (probe table in `docs/adr-drafts/ios.md` item 3). The parameters come from the internal `LoopbackHTTPServer.listenerParameters()` and are pinned by `Test support: the listener is bound to 127.0.0.1 on the loopback interface and does not set acceptLocalOnly` and `Test support: a connection to the server's port on ::1 is refused and never reaches the server`; address the server as `127.0.0.1` (`RedirectLoopbackTests` reach it as `localhost` on both platforms, through URLSession's fallback after the refused `::1` attempt, which never reaches `acceptedConnections`: an inference from the pin test and those passing tests, not a separate measurement) (ADR-051). Never RST (non-deterministic, transport.md E-16). When a FIN directly follows the bytes (`closeAfter`), URLSession sometimes reports −1005 before delivering bytes it read (4 of 5 runs at 50 KB; 1 of 4 runs of the R-NET-8 scenario), so a gating test whose outcome depends on how many bytes arrived drops the connection with `stallAfter` + `stop()` after the client reported those bytes; `closeAfter` is used only where the outcome does not depend on the byte count. URLSession holds the first bytes of a response without `Content-Type` (MIME sniffing), so stalls happen after 512 KiB, not 1 byte. Mid-flight states use `stallAfter` + `stop()`; `LiveLoopbackDelay` sleeps only in the P-11 benchmark (ADR-050).

## 7. Sync engine (WP-F)

sync.md is the detailed design; these sections are normative, including every amendment. The defaults referred to as "ARCHITECTURE §7" in `SyncTuning` are those of sync.md §7.1 with the review amendments recorded in ADR-036.

### 7.1 Per-asset state machine (R-SYN-2)

`absent` and `present` are derived from the store; only outstanding work has a job (keyed by `TransferID`, ADR-020). Legal transitions are exactly `JobStateKind.allows(from:to:)` (implemented and tested):

| From | To | Events (sync.md §5.2 rows) |
|---|---|---|
| — (absent) | queued | interest added for an applied or point-admitted entry (T1); quarantined → no job (T2) |
| queued | downloading | dispatched by `pump()` (T3) |
| queued | blocked | lane gated by policy, breaker, throttle or space (T4) |
| downloading | verifying | `.staged` (T5) |
| downloading | failed | `.failed` with a retry disposition (T6) |
| downloading | blocked | offline / space / policy pause (T6, T16 + policy) |
| downloading | queued | `.paused` after preemption; a policy pause that arrives when its lane is admitted again (re-queued at the front); an `.offline` failure that arrives after a satisfied-path edge was applied while the attempt ran, with the path satisfied now; the immediate re-queues of §7.6 (`cancelled(.byClient)`, `transient(.resumeRejected)`, `transient(.backgroundSessionUnavailable)`); or content superseded (T7, T17, T19, T22; F-30; ADR-053) |
| verifying | committing | store verification passed (T8) |
| verifying | failed | integrity failure (T9) |
| verifying | queued | content superseded with interests left (T22) |
| committing | failed | store failure |
| committing | queued | `.superseded` with interests left (T11) |
| failed | queued | alarm, satisfied-path edge, user request (T18–T20) |
| failed | failed | disposition re-armed |
| blocked | queued | the gate admits (T17) |
| blocked | blocked | the reason changed |
| any | — (job removed) | committed → present (T10); last interest left, not verifying/committing, and no pending claim names its content (T12, T13; L5-R1, ADR-054): a job only claims keep leaves when its content's last claim ends, that is when its group's join reaches the member, its group's release or its join's give-up drops the claim (at most `releasesPerTurn` a turn, so a released group's claims still count until dropped), or the engine shuts down; verifying/committing finished with no interest (sync-1 S1-01); pruned (T21); shutdown (T23) |

Removal goes through one synchronous funnel (`retire`): a job with no interest that is not verifying or committing is removed and its driver cancelled; out of verifying/committing with no interest it is removed instead of retried (sync-1 S1-01). Every interest removal (a release, a waiter's withdrawal, a caller's cancellation) goes through `removeInterest`, which keeps a job whose content a pending claim names (`claimHeld`; it keeps its state, place, running transfer and lane, `Job.heldLane`) instead of retiring it; the content's last claim to end retires it in that turn (`dropClaim`). The engine's other exits (a pruned or changed entry, presence answered from the store before the transfer, failure, commit) are unchanged (L5-R1, L5-R17; §7.2; ADR-054). Re-attached background transfers get a system interest the first run claims or releases. An illegal transition is a debug assertion and a `MetricsSnapshot.illegalTransitions` increment; every engine test asserts 0. A transfer stream that ends without a terminal element fails the job with `transient(.incompleteCallbackSequence)` (sync-1 S1-11).

**The pre-transfer read during legacy migration (ADR-055; L5K-R1, L5K-R13, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R21, L5K-R23, L5K-R25).** Every attempt's driver asks the store before any bytes (`drive` → `answeredFromStore`, row f-11), whatever interest created the job: a run of any trigger, `ensureAvailable`, a prefetch join, a resolve's waiter, a reroute, a system interest, the parked launch run (§7.10), a later launch that resumes an interrupted migration. While `state.migration.pending` (set in the turn the engine becomes `running` when `open()` reported legacy migration pending; cleared only when `migrateLegacy` reports `finished`; a deferred migration keeps it) that read is `adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency:)` per id, `.userInitiated` for a job of the user-initiated lane and `.bulk` otherwise (row k-4), so no transfer starts for a prior-art file migration can still adopt (R-STO-11); the store runs one adoption per prior-art file (§4.7), and a thrown `StoreError` reads as "nothing stored". Each bulk-urgency read is followed by the RE-S40 re-read, before the job's next read; ids of the same content that join the job during the reads, or during the answer site's publication, are read too, until none is new (L5K-R23). After the last ordinary await the answer site checks the job's interests: when the job holds a user-initiated interest and an id was answered by a read that installed nothing at once, one `adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency: .userInitiated)` installs the whole state before the synchronous answer (through the last such id, or, when it is no longer present, the earlier ones, latest first; L5K-R21, L5K-R25; §5). An attempt the reads leave no longer current (its job gone, or its epoch or attempt moved on) sends no request: `drive` returns before `remote.transfer`, ends the attempt's draining record and pumps (L5K-R25). With no migration pending the read stays the O(1) `location(for:)`. The enumerations of the transfer paths (L5K-R1), of every engine call of a store method with its deadline re-read and whether it suspends after publishing (L5K-R16, L5K-R20), of every engine loop around a store call (L5K-R17) and of every site that answers a user-initiated asker from the store (L5K-R21, scoped by L5K-R24) are in `docs/adr-drafts/k.md`.

**Engine invariants (ADR-053; `f.md` F-1, F-31, F-33…F-35, F-42).** `invariantViolations()` lists every broken invariant, and the model tests (`R-SYN-2: 10,000 seeded random operations never leave the transition table and keep every engine invariant`, the gate model and the seeded release walks) assert it empty after every operation: every waiter not answered from the store holds an interest in the job it names, a waiter holds an interest only in that job, and every waiter interest has its waiter; a job without interests is verifying or committing, or is kept by a pending claim that `claimHeld` maps to it, and the counted pending claims match the registered and releasing groups' pending members (`claimViolations`; L5-R1, ADR-054); `downloading` ⇔ an active attempt; running ≤ `maxConcurrentTransfers`; every pending group member holds its interest; the lane queues hold at most twice the jobs plus 16 entries, and each subscriber's order at most twice its live slots plus 16; no illegal transition; no drain of an attempt whose driver ended, and every claim of the run is held by its job; no job held for a manifest catch-up that nothing performs; no breaker probe held for a manifest fetch that is neither in flight nor about to begin (F-42); the space-parked, in-flight (set and bytes), lane-parked, breaker-parked, throttle-parked and failed indexes match the job table; re-queues are owed only to jobs still parked or failed, and a turn is scheduled for them; released groups keep interests only while a turn is scheduled to remove them, and a released group is not also registered (a known false positive since the level-5 merge: a registered group whose join's large give-up left claims in `releasing`, L5-R15, is reported as released and registered; no production code calls the check and every test reads it after `releasing.isEmpty`; Stage 3 flags a registered token only while its `Release` holds members or pending interests, L5-R22 (4), ADR-054); reachability: every queued job has a valid entry in its queue, put back, or set aside while only released groups want it (F-31); a live alarm armed at or before the earliest deadline (`unfiredDeadline`, F-34); no stranded pending run, except one the alarm's next turn starts (`startsInAlarmTurn`, F-33, F-34); no throttle-parked job without the `.throttle` deadline or an owed re-queue (`unwakeableThrottleParks`, F-35). Since the level-5 WP-K merge (ADR-055, L5K-R16): when the store reports a deadline (`nextDeadline() != nil`, which it does whenever a coalesced publication's trailing install is due) while the engine is settled (`storeDeadlineSettled`: running; no job, run or migration loop; no alarm store work, deadline re-read, settling or presence read, `apply` or catch-up in flight; no owned task but the alarm, the watcher and the memory-pressure observer), the heap holds a store entry (`invariantViolations(storeDeadlineDue:)`, defaulted to `false`, so every other caller is unchanged). The test helper `EngineHarness.idleViolations()` reads the engine, the store, then the engine again, and checks only when both reads were settled with the same alarm and store entry; the engine model asserts it after every operation and at idle after its walk, and every test of `EngineLegacyMigrationTransferTests` at idle.

### 7.2 Single-flight, interests and continuations (R-SYN-4, R-CAR-4, H-3)

Interests (`InterestToken`) are waiters (`resolve`), groups (prefetch handles, `ensureAvailable`), runs and system interests. A job's lane is the maximum of its interests; withdrawing the highest demotes it (and re-runs admission, sync-1 S1-07). **Every continuation kind** — waiters, groups, runs, `start` and `sync` joiners, `waitUntil` — lives in a token-keyed table and is resumed only through `removeValue`; every table is settled at registration (a run with zero fetches completes at once, sync-1 S1-02), on each transition, on withdrawal, on purge (the active run ends `failed(.cancelled)`) and at shutdown. A caller's cancellation hops to the actor and withdraws only its token; that includes `waitUntil`, a census-style test seam, which ends on its caller's cancellation as TestSupport's census waits do (`CensusWaiting`, l2fu-1; ADR-050). Shared work (start, manifest fetch, runs, store-touching entry points) runs in engine-owned tasks, never in a caller's task (ADR-039).

**Pending claims (ADR-054; L5-R1, L5-R15…L5-R17, G-13).** `registerGroup` answers before its group holds any interest (its join adds them in an owned task after a store read), so the registering turn records a pending claim per member (`recordGroup`; `ClaimKey` = a checksummed entry's content hash, a checksum-less entry's transfer); a member the engine's copy does not list yet is claimed when `adoptApplied` lists it (`claimNewlyListed`). Until the group's join reaches the member (joins or skips it, `joinMember`), the group is released (claims dropped before interests, chunked with them at `releasesPerTurn`), the join gives up (`endClaims(of:)`; more than `releasesPerTurn` claims go through the release follow-up, L5-R15) or the engine shuts down (`resumeEverything`), no other group's release, waiter withdrawal or caller cancellation retires that content's job or cancels its transfer, whether the job existed at registration or not. The join's promotion or demotion of a running transfer reaches the transport as `setPriority`, never a second start (R-CAR-3, sync-1 S1-05). A claimed-only job behaves as if the released interest were still there, except on failure: it ends on its first failed attempt as `failed(.nextRun)` with `assetFailed`, and the successor's join starts a fresh transfer (L5-R16; a claim that inherits the held lane's retry policy is Stage 3's). A claim names the content its member had when claimed: a member whose entry changes is protected again only once its join reaches it. `recordGroup` and `spawnJoin` are a pure split of the registration, no production seam (L5-R17). C2 at 10,000 members (release-mode probes on macOS 27 at a load of about 4.9–5.5, not a device; `docs/adr-drafts/g.md` C2 figures): the registering turn 4.27–4.40 ms (the claims about 0.85 ms, the rest the existing de-duplication and sort), a release turn 0.14–0.16 ms, `claimNewlyListed` 1.01–1.05 ms, a large give-up's turn 3.53–3.70 ms.

### 7.3 Planner and stable order (R-SYN-1, R-SYN-8, P-4)

`SyncPlanner.plan(manifest:snapshot:scope:policy:freeSpace:)` is pure: one pass over the manifest (O(1) record probes), one pass over the applied manifest for prunes, a sort of the fetches only. **Fetch order is `(AssetPolicy.rank, manifestIndex)`**: essential before standard before on-demand, then publisher order (the prior art's serial order); relocations, reuses and skips in manifest order; prunes in applied-manifest order. No output depends on dictionary or set iteration. Reconciliation (relocations, reuses, prunes, the adopted manifest) is committed by `AssetStore.apply` before any fetch is enqueued (R-SYN-8); a seeded property test pins `ApplyReport == plan.reconcile` (ADR-030). Planned fetches become jobs only within the pipeline plus `runLookahead` (sync-1 S1-15); the rest stay a cursor into the plan. The merged `apply` diffs against current state and makes no digest comparison; a digest short-circuit, if the engine keeps one, is the engine's, and it stays correct after a point admission: `admit(_:)` sets the applied digest to `AssetStore.admittedDigest(after:revision:)` (SHA-256 of a domain tag, the previous digest and the manifest revision), which no fetched manifest's digest can equal, so the next fetched manifest is always applied (B-30; ADR-050). The planner reads `AssetRecord.isStale`, `referencedBlob` and `expectedKey` (package, B-13).

### 7.4 Lanes, dispatch and promotion (R-SYN-3, H-4)

Three lanes, `userInitiated` (split into `single` before `batch`) > `prefetch` > `bulk`; one slot reserved for user work; bulk ages ahead of prefetch after `bulkAging`. The pipeline cap gates **non-user lanes only** (sync-2 S2-10). Promotion of a running transfer is `handle.setPriority` inside the synchronous `join` (ADR-021); a queued job jumps to the user queue; when no slot is free a lower-lane transfer is preempted with `pause()` (never the breaker's probe). `pump()` loops after marking a victim (sync-1 S1-16). Priority changes are issued before any other start in the same actor turn (sync-1 S1-06). `interactiveConcurrencyAtCritical` caps user work at `.critical` thermal state. **Lane-queue order (ADR-053; F-30, F-31, L4-R18).** Each lane-queue entry keeps its place (`LaneQueues.Entry.place`: appends count up, front pushes count down). A scan sets aside, once, the entry of a job that only released groups want, and that job waits in no queue (a token still registered in `groups` is never read as released, even while claims its join gave up wait in `releasing`: `isReleasedOnly`, L5-R21; a job only a pending claim keeps counts as released-only while a release is in progress and is otherwise dispatched at its held lane, §7.2; ADR-054); if an interest at its own lane wants it again, it returns to its original place (`reinstate`, merged by place at the next pass), and `restoreAside` merges by place rather than prepending. Members re-joined in one turn keep their order and never pass earlier queued work beyond R-SYN-3's lane rules. Compaction to twice the live entries also runs when jobs leave (`removeJob`). A re-join that changes the lane, or comes from user work, still goes to the back through `relane` (Stage 3, L4-R24(4)).

### 7.5 Priority → URLSession (R-NET-10)

| Engine lane | Engine scheduling | `URLSessionTask.priority` | Session |
|---|---|---|---|
| `userInitiated` | first; reserved slot; may preempt a lower lane | `highPriority` = **0.75** | foreground |
| `prefetch` | second | `defaultPriority` = **0.5** | foreground |
| `bulk` | last; ages ahead of prefetch after 30 s | `lowPriority` = **0.25** | foreground; background only under ADR-026 |

Implemented as `AssetPriority.urlSessionTaskPriority` and tested. The priority is a hint; H-4 is guaranteed by the engine's lanes. Effective in-flight transfers per origin stay ≤ `httpMaximumConnectionsPerHost` (≤ 8).

### 7.6 `TransferError` → engine action → `AssetError` (R-NET-6, R-CAR-7)

| `TransferError` | Engine action | Breaker input | `AssetError` to waiters |
|---|---|---|---|
| `offline(reason)` | `failed(.whenOnline)`; re-queued on a satisfied-path edge (R-SYN-13), and at once when a satisfied-path edge was applied after the attempt started and the path reads satisfied now (the attempt records `networkEdgeSerial` when it starts, so this happens at most once per edge; F-30, ADR-053); counts as blocked for run settling | none (a probe returns to half-open) | `.offline` |
| `transient(.timedOut / .connectionLost / .cannotConnect / .dnsFailure / .secureConnectionFailed / .badServerResponse / .unknown, nil)` | `failed(.at(backoff))`, early re-queue on a path edge | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)` |
| `transient(.serverError, retryAfter)` | backoff (Retry-After as lower bound) | 5xx window | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` |
| `transient(.throttled, retryAfter)` | origin throttle until the clamped deadline; jobs `blocked(.throttled)`; a throttle-only blocked run sets a pending run (sync-2 S2-03). A user-initiated request waits only while the throttle ends within `interactiveMaxWait`, and the bound covers its whole wait: when a later 429 extends the throttle beyond it, the user-initiated `resolve` and `ensureAvailable` interests of the jobs it parked fail fast with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)`, in turns of their own of at most `parksPerPump` jobs, while other interests keep waiting (F-36). Every answer to a refusal by the throttle (a fail-fast joiner, a throttled run's `.blocked(.throttled(until:))`, one settled on its claims included, a fetch-first `ensureAvailable`'s and an unknown-id `resolve`'s `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)`) reports the origin's end as read when it answers (F-36, F-38, F-39); each extension moves a throttle-gated pending run to the new end (F-39). A fetch-first `ensureAvailable` whose manifest fetch the throttle refuses, and whose scope the store cannot answer, fails at once with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` carrying the time left; one whose manifest is in hand reports its scope, a member a throttle extension answered counting as missing (L4-R23; ADR-053) | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` |
| `transient(.resumeRejected)` | discard partial and sidecar; immediate fresh attempt (no backoff) | none | — (retried) |
| `transient(.authenticationChallenge)` | backoff; heals on a path change (proxy, captive portal) | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)` |
| `transient(.sourceVersionSkew)` | backoff + one best-effort update check (Apple tier) | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)` |
| `transient(.backgroundSessionUnavailable)` | retry in the foreground; background disabled for the process | none | — (retried) |
| `transient(.localIO)` / `.incompleteCallbackSequence` | backoff | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)` |
| `permanentMissing` | `failed(.untilEntryChanges)`, persisted through `AssetStore.recordFailure(ids, identity:, reason: .notFound)` (WP-C); `apply` clears it when the entry's content identity changes (WP-B), so no run and no relaunch requests it before then (ADR-032). The engine first re-reads presence and answers from the store the ids it serves at their current content (row f-10), and the store leaves a record bound to a live blob at `identity` unchanged, while a stale record still fails, its old blob doomed `unreferenced` (L4-R5, L4-R16; ADR-053) | none | `.notFound` |
| `authentication(.forbiddenAmbiguous(e))`, asset | `failed(.nextRun)`; auth-suspect counter by distinct asset; **never quarantined** (sync-2 S2-05) | auth (3 distinct assets) | `.authRejected(.ambiguous)` |
| `authentication(.forbiddenAmbiguous(e))`, manifest, `e.hint == .keyRejectedLikely` | run ends `blocked(.circuitOpen)` | auth opens at once (an engine inference, never a transport verdict) | `.authRejected(.confirmed)` |
| `integrity(…)` (transport) / `StoreError.integrity` | R-INT-3 bookkeeping in the store; backoff, then `failed(.untilEntryChanges)` after `maxIntegrityAttempts` | none | `.integrityFailure` |
| `insufficientSpace` | Decided on a fresh free-space read (ADR-053). (1) With pending reclaim: `blocked(.awaitingSpace)`, and the attempt counts toward the lane's retry budget (`countFailedAttempt`, L4-R4); woken by `runDueWork`'s reaped bytes or by a purge's reclaim. It waits only while something will run the store's due work (a store deadline, the alarm's store work, a deadline re-read, a settling read or an owed sweep); otherwise it is decided on a fresh read: re-queued if the volume holds it, else `blocked(.insufficientSpace)` with that read's figures (F-25, F-26). (2) No pending reclaim, but the read holds the job: a retry with backoff within the lane's attempts, so a volume that keeps answering ENOSPC while reading roomy ends in a typed `insufficientSpace` (L4-R4, F-20). (3) Otherwise `blocked(.insufficientSpace)`, re-evaluated at every free-space read, promoting join and fail-fast join. Re-admissions follow the pump's order, each admitted against the room the jobs ahead leave (F-28). An attempt that fails with ENOSPC first discards its partial, or keeps it for resume through a sidecar (L4-R6) | none | `.insufficientSpace(required:available:)`: `required` is the bytes the attempt needs (the ENOSPC's figure when the transport reports one, else the entry's size; for a publication, the staged file's size), `available` the job's room above the reserve (its lane's volume less the other jobs in flight and the bytes committed since the read), the planner's convention (§7.10; F-22). An `ensureAvailable` whose member was failed for lack of space answers it while its scope is incomplete (F-25) |
| `rejected(violation)` | `failed(.nextRun)`; counted by violation | none | `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter: nil)` |
| `cancelled(.byClient)` | not a failure (T13 or T7) | probe → half-open | `.cancelled` |
| `cancelled(.bySystem)` | re-queued by the next run | probe → half-open | — |

`TransferError.failureClass` and `assetError(now:)` (Model) and `StoreError.assetError` (Storage) implement the right-hand columns and are tested; the engine overrides the two inferences noted (confirmed auth, the bytes needed against the room above the reserve; ADR-053).

**What the merged transport guarantees the engine (ADR-050; D-4, D-5, D-9, D-13, D-15, D-18.3).** The transport never answers `resumeRejected` to a request that did not resume (a 206 or 416 there is `rejected(.unexpectedStatus)`), so the immediate fresh attempt cannot loop. `backgroundSessionUnavailable` from −995/−996/−997 disables background in the coordinator itself, so the retry is routed to the foreground; the HTTP source never surfaces it for a job without a background size (`startIfAdmitted` returns `nil` and the transfer runs in the foreground with background left enabled); only a direct `BackgroundTransferCoordinator.start` of such a job, which no production caller makes, answers it with `NSURLErrorUnknown`. A timeout writes a sidecar too while `.timedOut` carries no resumable flag, so the engine looks for a sidecar before the next attempt rather than trusting a flag. A background `integrity(.truncated)` / `integrity(.sizeMismatch)` from a joined task leaves the staged file in staging for the engine's discard or staging GC. A foreground handle's `cancel()` marks `discard`, discards the partial and then cancels the task, so a completion already running may still report `staged` for a file that is gone: publishing a transfer the engine itself cancelled treats `ENOENT` as that cancellation, not as a failure to retry (WP-F).

### 7.7 Circuit breaker (R-SYN-5; ADR-031)

Per origin (`RemoteSource.originID`), a pure value (`CircuitBreaker`) owned by the engine. **Auth:** opens at once on a manifest 403 whose hint is key-rejected; ambiguous object 403s open it after 3 distinct assets with no intervening object success; cooldown 300 s doubling to 3,600 s with keyed jitter. **5xx:** sliding window of 20 outcomes, opens at ≥ 5 samples and ≥ 50 % 5xx; cooldown 30 s doubling to 600 s. **Half-open:** exactly one probe; success closes and resets trips (a cached manifest 304 never counts, sync-2 S2-21); a same-cause failure re-opens with `trips + 1`; **any other exit**, an exit without a verdict (a 404, a 400, an integrity failure, offline, a different cause, and abandoned: cancelled, paused, superseded, shut down), returns to `halfOpen(probe: nil)`, re-admits in that turn every job the breaker parked (the first dispatched probes, the others park again), drops the old probe's watchdog, and owes a pump follow-up whether or not anything was parked (F-40, F-42; ADR-053); a probe silent for `probeWatchdog` (60 s) is abandoned: past the watchdog the gate admits the next transfer as the probe (`CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`), and a manifest probe schedules its watchdog like a transfer probe (F-40). Every `.breaker` deadline turn owes a pump follow-up (F-41, F-42). While open, `resolve` fails fast and runs end `blocked(.circuitOpen)` with a pending run that starts once the breaker's gate admits at `now` (§7.10; ADR-053). **The half-open probe and runs (F-41, F-42; ADR-053).** A pending run that waits for a breaker admitting only a probe (open past its cooldown, half-open without a probe, or past a silent probe's watchdog) starts only after the pump that dispatches the jobs re-admitted for that probe (`dispatchOwed`). A run that starts while the breaker admits only a probe holds that probe for its manifest fetch from its start turn, so its `circuitChanged(.halfOpen)` follows `syncStarted`. This does not apply when the origin is throttled at `now`, or when the run joins a manifest fetch already past the gates; a fetch that finds the origin throttled at its admission lets the probe go. So "the half-open probe is the next run's manifest fetch" (sync.md §8.3) holds whenever a run starts while the breaker admits only a probe.

### 7.8 Timers and idle silence (R-SYN-14, P-14, H-8; ADR-022)

Deadlines are data in one min-heap `(deadline, seq, reason)`: retries, throttle end, breaker cooldown and probe watchdog, store deadlines (`AssetStore.nextDeadline()`), pending-run debounce or gate; the merged heap also holds the trailing progress emission (`.progress`, sync-2 S2-24), a quarantined asset's re-download (`.heal`, R-CLN-8) and a manifest fetch's deadline (`.manifest`, transport-2 T2-14) (`Engine/DeadlineHeap.swift`; ADR-053). One alarm task exists iff the heap is non-empty; firing drains due entries in `(deadline, seq)` order, re-validating each against epochs, then calls `store.runDueWork()` if a store deadline was due and re-arms; every answer of the store's deadline (a re-read, this report, a lease release) is applied through `applyStoreDeadline`: when another update landed while it was in flight the earlier deadline is kept, at worst one early wake whose report re-arms from the newest deadline (L5K-R16; ADR-055). Stale entries are removed eagerly on withdrawal and settle (sync-1 S1-10). The conditions watcher exists iff work exists and is cancelled by the engine when it goes idle. **Idle** = no job, no run, no pending run, empty heap ⇒ `EngineIntrospection.isIdleSilent`, plus the environment census (`ManualTimeSource.pendingSleepers == 0`, `FakeRemoteSource.activeTransfers == 0`, `FakeDeviceConditionsSource.activeObservers == 0`, awaited with `waitForObservers(equalTo:)`). A registered `PurgeableCache` keeps one memory-pressure observer, which is work: R-SYN-14 and P-14 are measured with none registered.

### 7.9 Events and backpressure (R-SYN-12, H-9; ADR-023)

Pull-based per-subscriber queues behind `AsyncStream(unfolding:onCancel:)`; registration on first pull; released on cancel, deinit and shutdown. Progress events coalesce newest-wins per key at ≤ 10 Hz with a trailing emission after a quiet period (sync-2 S2-24). Terminal events coalesce per subject and are never dropped; bulk per-asset outcomes fold into `syncProgress`/`syncFinished`; a pruned id's subject retires with its `assetRemoved`; a transfer's progress key retires with the transfer. `clientShutDown` is delivered last on every path, waiting and lagging subscribers alike. Client lifecycle events never coalesce with each other: `clientStarted` and `clientShutDown` have a terminal slot each (`EventSlot.client`, `.clientEnd`), so a shutdown never replaces a `clientStarted` its subscriber has not read; the per-subscriber bound grows by that one constant slot, and `clientShutDown` stays last because nothing is published after it (L5-R19; ADR-054, ADR-023 amendment).

### 7.10 Runs, space, refresh, purge, maintenance, shutdown

- **Run:** single-flight (`sync()` joiners share the outcome), over the retention scope (ADR-027) → conditional manifest fetch (single-flight with point admission; a point-admission fetch never stores its ETag, sync-2 S2-28) → decode on the bulk executor (P-3, C2) → `freeSpace()` → plan (off-actor) → space decision **before any mutation** → `store.apply(manifest, etag:, eviction:)` → enqueue → settle → `markSynced(_:ifPurgeEpoch:)` only for a `.complete` run with a fresh manifest. A run whose manifest fetch passed `manifestFetchDeadline` falls back to the applied manifest and ends `.complete` with `report.manifestFresh == false`; it writes no stamp and leaves `syncedThisLaunch` false, so the sync stays due under every refresh policy (L4-R2, F-15; ADR-053). **The parked launch run (ADR-055; L5K-R1, L5K-R16).** A launch run due while legacy migration runs waits for it (`launchRunWaiting`). Any run that starts meanwhile takes its place (`startRun` clears the flag in the turn its run is in place: it applies the same manifest, and its jobs adopt the prior-art files migration has not reached, §7.1), and the migration loop's end starts the launch run only if the flag is still set and the engine runs, clearing it in every case, shutdown included, so one launch syncs once (option (a) of the parked-run decision, distinct from CCR-K1's option (a), which L5K-R1 ruled insufficient alone). The loop re-reads the store's deadline after every call, so migration's later adoptions reach the readers whether or not a launch run follows (L5K-R16). The engine sends one `apply` at a time to the store, the next only after it has followed the previous one (`takeApplyTurn`/`passApplyTurn`), so it follows its own applies in the store's commit order whatever the task priorities; queued applies count as catch-ups in flight, and at shutdown every queued apply sends nothing (`.failure(.closed)` → `.shutDown`) (F-25). **Settling (ADR-053; L4-R20, F-36…F-42).** A run settles blocked only by gates that reject at `now` as the next dispatch reads them. That reading is one pure evaluation (`originGate(at:)`: the throttle's end against `now`, and `CircuitBreaker.admission(of:at:)`, which admits the next transfer once the cooldown has ended, without a probe, or past a silent probe's watchdog). A claim parked by a gate that admits is re-admitted in that turn (`readmitOpenedOriginGates`) and counts as working. A run stays unsettled while any of its claims (i) is owed a re-queue by a re-admission follow-up turn (`originOwed`, `edgeOwed`, an owed conditions pass for an admitted lane) (F-36); (ii) is parked `insufficientSpace` while a follow-up free-space sweep is owed, and that sweep checks the run afterwards (F-37); or (iii) is parked `insufficientSpace` but admitted by the headroom ledger: the run first re-checks the space-parked jobs as a read does, and settles blocked only if the claim stays parked (F-38). **Pending runs (ADR-053).** A pending run starts only through `reconsiderPendingRun`, once its gate reads open at `now` (F-40); one that waits for a breaker admitting only a probe starts only after the pump that dispatches the jobs re-admitted for that probe (`dispatchOwed`, F-41); a run that starts while the breaker admits only a probe holds that probe for its manifest fetch from its start turn (F-42, §7.7). A run that ends offline leaves a conditions-gated pending run: the first such run since the last satisfied-path edge is retried once, after `pendingRunDebounce`, once conditions admit bulk work; a later one that ends offline while the path reads satisfied waits for a satisfied-path edge (`PendingRun.awaitsNetworkEdge`), with no timer. A satisfied edge resets the streak, and so does any run that ends otherwise. This is one bounded retry, not polling, so R-SYN-13 holds (L4-R3, F-16).
- **Space (R-SYN-11, ADR-025):** `available = volume(opportunistic for bulk/prefetch, important for user work) − reserve + pendingReclaim`; `budgetRoom = max(0, budget − (used − pruneBytes))`; a plan with zero fetch bytes always fits (sync-2 S2-06); unknown sizes are not estimated (S2-08); over budget → largest fitting prefix (essential first) or, with the policy off, `insufficientSpace` with zero side effects. Eviction makes room only when `StoragePolicy.evictionPermitted` (default `true`, ADR-048); the engine builds the planner's policy with `PlanningPolicy(storage:includeOnDemand:now:)` (implemented and tested), so the switch, unverified admission and the prefix rule cannot be dropped on the way. Per-fetch headroom ledger counts bytes in flight **and committed since the last free-space read** (S2-09). Since the level-4 merge (ADR-053): the planner's `insufficient(required:available:)` reports the plan's known bytes against `room` (the volume above the reserve plus pending reclaim, within the budget), and the engine's own `insufficientSpace` follows the same convention (F-22, §7.6); `state.freeSpace` keeps the measured reading, and only the planner and `ensureAvailable`'s own space decision see the important-for-opportunistic copy (L4-R6); a free-space read is adopted in the turn it returns, the ledger keeping the bytes committed since the read began (L4-R7); a purge's reclaim re-admits the jobs parked for space at once, on a fresh read (F-26); re-admission after a read follows the pump's order, each job against the room the jobs admitted ahead of it leave, and re-admitted jobs count until they start (F-28, F-29); before a run settles blocked for lack of space, it re-checks the space-parked jobs when the ledger admits one of its claims (F-38).
- **Unknown ids (prior-art resolver parity):** one single-flight conditional fetch; a waiter is answered from the newest manifest seen (fetched or applied), never `.notFound` while a run's fetch holds a newer one (sync-1 S1-13); negative cache 60 s, LRU-bounded.
- **Refresh (R-SYN-9):** `RefreshDecision.isSyncDue` (implemented, tested). The stamp lives in the index (R-STO-2).
- **Purge (R-CLN-6):** `purgeEpoch` bumped before any await; the active run ends `failed(.cancelled)`; non-user interests on purged ids withdrawn; user waiters re-queue; the store bumps its own `purgeEpoch` and clears the stamp, with `syncDue` set, before its first own await and again in the turn of its last chunk when it purged something (ADR-052).
- **Maintenance (R-SYN-16):** deadline on the monotonic clock; staging GC → orphan GC → eviction → deep verification in bounded batches; cancellation honoured between batches.
- **What the store's cleanup and migration calls mean for the engine (ADR-052).** `migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` is called again while `!progress.finished`, with a backoff (for example to the next maintenance window) when a call returns `remaining == 0 && !finished` (RE-S29). After every call, returned or thrown, the engine re-reads the store's deadline (RE-S40, L5K-R16). A call whose bulk publication left the trailing install due returns `remaining > 0` while files remain (L5K-R20; what is left is read after the publication's await, so the back-off signal never follows a batch that left nothing, L5K-R23), and while a job's adoption holds the last claimed file the next call waits for it (k-7) instead of returning at once (L5K-R17): the loop never spins (ADR-055). `collectStaging(keeping:)` keeps only the engine's claims (plus unclaimed files younger than `stagingClaimGrace`; a claimed file older than `stagingMaxAge` still goes), so every transfer still worked on is claimed, those with a publication in flight included (R14). Quota eviction's and orphan GC's `bytesFreed`/`filesRemoved` are bytes and blobs released to the reaper, unlinked later by `runDueWork()` (`reapedBytes`); a concurrent `collectGarbage` returns an empty result at once. A purge waits in the caller's task: a caller cancelled during its waits gets `.cancelled` although the purge is committed, bytes a non-acknowledging sink holds back are in neither `bytesFreed` nor `bytesDeferred`, and `purgeEpoch` moves twice when it purged something. An evicted record is re-adopted only at launch, so one whose new content is live in another blob is downloaded again (OI-15). A maintenance eviction overlapping a pruning `apply` can free up to the pruned bytes more than needed (OI-14; an engine that runs maintenance apart from a sync's `apply` never meets it). The synchronous dry run `evictionPlan(toFree:protecting:)` holds the actor about 2.5 ms at 10,000 records and about 38 ms at `maxEntries` (OI-4; an off-actor version is a Stage 3 contract change). Phase 3's durable commit before the `UserDefaults` key removal is shown by code reading only (`FakeFileSystem` makes `.barrier` and `.full` identical, OI-2): no fake test can prove it.
- **Retention scope (R-CAR-5, ADR-027):** `setRetentionScope(_:)` replaces the engine's scope (the next bulk run's) and pushes it to the store in an owned task (RE-S33); it starts no run.
- **Pins (R-CLN-4, ADR-044):** `setPinned(_:pinned:)` through the store in an owned task.
- **Memory pressure (R-CLN-11, ADR-044):** `observeMemoryPressure(epoch:handler:)` starts or stops one owned observer of `AssetEnvironment.memoryPressure`; only the newest epoch applies; the handler (the facade's relay) may stop it from inside; shutdown stops and joins it, from `created` too (L5-R2; ADR-054).
- **Storage report and diagnostics:** `storageReport()` adds `RemoteSource.sourceCopyBytes()` to the store's report (RE-S32); `diagnostics()` returns `EngineDiagnostics` (counters, the last 32 `DiagnosticError`s, app version, launch epoch, generation, stamp, retention scope). It answers while the engine is degraded or its store cannot answer: the error ring, the last-known index generation (never older than one the engine announced; 0 if none was ever read) and the last-known stamp, or `nil`; after `shutdown()` it fails with `.shutDown` (ADR-042); `metrics()` is unchanged (L4-R9; ADR-053).
- **Shutdown (R-SYN-15, ADR-039, ADR-042):** see ADR-039's order. `AssetClient.shutdown()` shuts the client's lifecycle down (no reopen afterwards, every registration refused, a running reopen awaited, then the current engine's `shutdown()`), then resets the mirror so `lookup` returns `nil` and closes the cache relay; `AssetClient.deinit` spawns the lifecycle's `shutdown()`. From every state, `created` included, the engine's `shutdown()` returns only after every owned task is cancelled and joined and every continuation resumed with `.shutDown`; from `created` it still never opens the store and never logs (a joined group join's `ensureStarted()` refuses during `shuttingDown`). Three spawn sites are reachable in `created`: a group's join, the memory-pressure observer and the release follow-up, which continues in any state but shutdown (L5-R2, G-14; ADR-054). Afterwards every engine entry point returns `.failure(.shutDown)` (`sync`: `.failed(.shutDown)`; `registerGroup`: holds nothing; a new event stream yields `clientShutDown` and finishes); releases and `shutdown()` are idempotent no-ops.

## 8. Facade (WP-G)

`AssetClient(configuration:environment: AssetEnvironment? = nil)`: resolves a missing environment to `.live(legacyDefaultsSuiteName: configuration.legacy.defaultsSuiteName)` (`resolvedEnvironment(_:for:)`, ADR-048), builds the store's configuration with `storeConfiguration(for:)` (location, policy, legacy layout, manifest limits, clamped snapshot install interval), stores values, creates the mirror, the client's lifecycle (`ClientLifecycle`, an internal actor holding the current store and engine pair and its generation, the first pair built synchronously; G-1, ADR-054), the background relay and the purgeable-cache relay; no I/O, no task, no `Bundle`/`UserDefaults` read, no URLSession (P-12, sync-2 S2-18). `start()` is single-flight; every async API awaits it (`setRetentionScope` and `subscribe()` included, G-3; the run-time scope is recorded before it is applied, so a reopen builds its replacement with the newest one; ADR-054). `lookup` (main actor) reads the mirror only. `resolve` returns a URL; `prefetch` returns a `PrefetchHandle` whose release is ordered after its registration and, for a re-lane, after the successor's registration (`cancel(after:)`), the task holding the old handle until that release ran so its deinit cannot release early (sync-1 S1-05, implemented and tested); `awaitRegistration()` reports `.shutDown`. The handle reaches the engine through the client's `ClientLifecycle`, the facade's `PrefetchRegistry`: facade tokens, each bound to the engine token of the generation it was registered with, so a release after a reopen never reaches another engine's token (G-1); `SyncEngine` conforms too, and the handle tests use a gated fake. With the engine's pending claims (§7.2, L5-R1) a re-lane's release never cancels or restarts a transfer the successor names (R-CAR-3; ADR-054). `PrefetchWindow.targets` (implemented): current, +1, −1, +2, −2…; `PrefetchWindowController(client:radius: Int? = nil)` defaults its radius to `configuration.prefetchRadius` (ADR-048) and creates the new handle before releasing the old. `withAsset` leases through the engine (so lease release re-arms the alarm). The fallback chain (`AssetFallbackChain`): `prepare(kinds:)` builds the bundle index in one unit on the client's bulk executor (C2, ADR-038); `locate` is bundle → local, synchronous, no file-system work on main; `fetch` is `resolve` plus the candidate-extension check (ADR-037). Retention scope (`configuration.retentionScope`, `setRetentionScope(_:)`), pins (`pin`, `unpin`) and purgeable caches (`register`, `unregister`, main actor, held weakly by `PurgeableCacheRelay`) are ADR-027 and ADR-044. Observability: `AssetLog` lives in the Model (subsystem `com.ztron.assetkit`, categories `store`/`sync`/`transport`/`apple`, `hashed(_:)`, `emit(_:sink:tap:)`), every line to the environment's `LogSink` (`OSLogSink` live, `TapOnlyLogSink` in tests) and to the optional `LogTap` with its subsystem (ADR-043, ADR-047); `DiagnosticReport.indexSchemaVersion` is `StoreLayout.indexSchemaVersion`; `MetricsSnapshot`; `DiagnosticReport` (Codable, no `AppKey`), built by `DiagnosticReportBuilder` from one engine's `diagnostics()`, metrics and storage report read inside one `perform` (never mixed across generations; empty figures when the store cannot answer, L4-R9); the configuration is summarised field by field (the app key as `<redacted>` or `none`; URLs cut to scheme, host, port and path; `manifestPath` and `catalogPath` cut at `?`/`#`), engine counts are named, the JSON is sorted with ISO 8601 dates and non-finite numbers as strings, and `moduleVersion` is the constant `"1.0.0"` (G-4; ADR-054). After `shutdown()` every API fails with `.shutDown` as ADR-042 lists. **Replaced root (ADR-050; B-15, B-17):** after `rootVanished` every store commit and mutating API throws `.storageUnavailable(.rootVanished)` while reads keep working; the facade owns the reopen, and calls `SnapshotMirror.reset()` before the replacement store's first install (§5), because the replacement's epoch can repeat or be smaller than the mirror's. **The reopen (ADR-054; G-1, G-5, G-12, G-18; L5-R4, L5-R8, L5-R20).** Every async API but `shutdown()` runs through one path (`perform`), except that `setRetentionScope` and `subscribe()` use it only for their `start()` (the lifecycle records and applies the scope, G-3, and registers the stream, G-16): a call that fails with `.storageUnavailable(.rootVanished)`, or with a failure that can stem from the file system (`.storageUnavailable(_)`, `.serverUnavailable(_)`, `.insufficientSpace`, `.integrityFailure`) when the store's on-demand root check answers `.rootVanished` (`AssetStore.verifyRootNow()`: the commit's own `StoreLock.verifyRoot`, one stat on the metadata executor, never on main, and no I/O when a reason is already recorded), reopens and is repeated on the replacement; `.cancelled`, `.shutDown`, `.notFound`, `.offline` and `.authRejected` never trigger the check, and every other result is returned exactly as the engine gave it (L4-R23), a read-only (B-34) store's `.ioFailure` included. `withAsset` reads `unavailableReason` before its lease (a lease is a read, which a replaced store still grants) and reopens first; a root that vanishes between that read and the lease leases from the vanished store (a read, accepted under L5-R4). A `.shutDown` from an engine a sibling call's reopen retired repeats the call on the replacement. A call reopens at most `AssetClient.reopenAttempts` (3) times; `sync`, `handleBackgroundEvents` and `diagnosticReport()` use the same path. The reopen (`ClientLifecycle`) is single-flight: the old engine is shut down (its store closes), the mirror is reset on the main actor, the replacement is built from the same configuration, environment and mirror with the run-time retention scope, every event follower is handed its stream, the replacement is installed, the newest memory-pressure request is applied (a request recorded during a reopen reaches only the replacement; `observeMemoryPressure` never waits for a reopen, G-12), every live prefetch group is registered again, and the replacement is started; a shutdown meanwhile builds nothing. Prefetch groups, the memory-pressure observer and the run-time retention scope survive a reopen; pins live in the vanished root's records and are lost with it (L5-R8). Between the reset and the replacement's first install `lookup` returns `nil`. `ClientLifecycle.current()`, where every `perform` call starts, waits for a reopen that is running when it is called until that reopen's task ends, after its tail (the memory-pressure request, the group re-registration and the replacement's `start()`); the reopen clears its single-flight entry in the turn that installs its replacement, so a call made during the tail returns the installed replacement at once, and a caller's `start()` joins the replacement's single-flight start (L5-R22 (5), as the code does it; ADR-054). G-1 and G-5 change no public signature (owner information, L5-R8). **Event streams across reopens (ADR-054; L5-R3, L5-R13, L5-R19; G-2, G-16, G-17).** `events` and `subscribe()` streams are pull-through forwarders (`EventForwarder`: at most one event waits in each, so the engine's backpressure holds, R-SYN-12). A forwarder becomes a follower of the lifecycle in the turn that chooses the engine it registers with, a `subscribe()` stream before `subscribe()` returns and an `events` stream on its first pull, and every reopen hands every follower the replacement's stream before installing it (the last look at the followers, the check of `closed` and the installation share one turn). A stream receives every event of its registration's generation from its registration on, and every event of each later generation from that generation's first event on, `clientStarted(generation:)` included, subject only to the engine's coalescing: progress per progress key, the other terminal events per subject (asset, breaker, maintenance, run); client lifecycle events never coalesce with each other (§7.9), so every generation whose `start()` finished announces itself with its `clientStarted` before its later events (one retired before its `start()` finished owes none). A retired engine's `clientShutDown` is hidden; a real shutdown's is forwarded and is the last event; an `events` stream made after shutdown yields only `clientShutDown`, and `subscribe()` throws `.shutDown`. Replacements may announce equal values (`clientStarted(generation: 1)`), and their snapshot generations restart (B-17). SPEC §4.1's "public re-exports" are one `@_exported import ZTronAssetModel` in `AssetClientConfiguration.swift`, so apps import only `ZTronAssetKit` (the only product); it is a compile-time attribute with no runtime effect, not a private API in the sense of R-SEC-3. **What the merged engine gives the facade (ADR-053).** `SyncEngine.ensureAvailable` reports its scope when its manifest is in hand, a member a throttle extension answered counting as missing with the report's `manifestFresh`; a fresh call whose manifest fetch the origin's throttle refuses, and whose scope the store cannot answer, fails with `.serverUnavailable(retryAfter:)` carrying the time left. The facade's `ensureAvailable` keeps that result faithful (L4-R23; WP-K's shims never call `ensureAvailable`, so it was not revisited, ADR-055). `SyncEngine.diagnostics()` answers while the engine is degraded or its store cannot answer (L4-R9), so `diagnosticReport()` can rely on it. Both hold in the merged facade (tests `R-SYN-10: the facade's ensureAvailable throws serverUnavailable with the time left when the origin's throttle refuses its manifest fetch`, `R-SYN-10: the facade's ensureAvailable reports a member a throttle answered as missing with a fresh manifest` and `R-OBS-3: diagnosticReport answers while the store cannot be opened, with the degraded lifecycle and empty storage figures`; ADR-054).

## 9. Hard problems: decisions and tests

Each test name below is the exact `@Test` display name the owning WP writes (the trace gate reads only `R-…` and `P-…` IDs, so tests are named by requirement). Names marked *(exists)* are in the repository now.

**H-1 Crash consistency.** Content-addressed immutable blobs, one atomically replaced whole-state index with a SHA-256 trailer, references made durable in dependency order, idempotent reconciliation (store.md §1; ADR-012, ADR-015, ADR-018); `FakeFileSystem` enumerates every mutation under four crash modes and kill→power-loss pairs across two lifetimes.
- `R-STO-5: crash at every file-system boundary during a first sync preserves I-1…I-5`
- `R-STO-5: a process kill followed by a power loss in the next lifetime preserves I-1…I-5`
- `R-STO-5: power loss after a durable commit keeps the store root reachable`
- `R-STO-4: a torn index.tmp is ignored and the newest valid generation wins`
- `R-STO-12: a header with a maximal generation or body length never traps`

**H-2 Snapshot vs deletion.** Epoch reclamation with acknowledged publication: delete only after an acknowledged snapshot of this epoch without the blob, awake-clock grace, a committed generation, no lease, no in-flight publication, and a fresh acknowledgement after the deadline (ADR-016, ADR-017).
- `R-CLN-5: deletion waits for the acknowledged snapshot without the blob, the grace window and every lease`
- `R-CLN-5: a sink that keeps an older or foreign snapshot never counts as acknowledgement`
- `R-CLN-5: deletion grace uses the awake clock and a fresh acknowledgement after a device sleep`
- `R-STO-13: the mirror ignores an out-of-order snapshot and acknowledges what readers see` *(exists)*

**H-3 Single-flight and cancellation.** One job per `TransferID`, ref-counted interests, continuations resumed only through `removeValue`, cancellation withdraws one token (ADR-020, §7.2).
- `R-SYN-4: 100 callers on one asset with seeded random cancellations share one transfer and each resumes exactly once`
- `R-SYN-4: the transfer is cancelled only after the last of 100 callers cancels`
- `R-SYN-4: two identifiers with the same checksum share one transfer and both become present`
- `R-SYN-10: ensureAvailable cancelled as its last member commits resumes exactly once`
- `R-CAR-3: a re-laned prefetch handle releases its old interest only after the successor registered` *(exists; fails on the round-1 release logic)*
- `R-CAR-4: a handle released without cancel() releases its interest on deinit` *(exists)*

**H-4 Priority.** Three lanes, a reserved user slot, in-place promotion through the synchronous handle, queue jump, preemption with resume (ADR-021, §7.4, §7.5).
- `R-SYN-3: a userInitiated request for an in-flight bulk asset promotes it without restarting the transfer`
- `R-SYN-3: with concurrency 1 a userInitiated request preempts the bulk transfer, which later resumes`
- `R-NET-10: a promoted asset's priority change reaches the transport before any other transfer starts` (reads `FakeRemoteSource.transportLog`, which orders the handles' controls and the source's starts in one sequence, round 4)
- `R-NET-10: AssetPriority maps to URLSessionTask priorities 0.75, 0.5 and 0.25` *(exists)*

**H-5 Actor reentrancy.** Epoch-validated jobs, re-validation after every await, the store re-checks its phase and the record revision, the ledger of §10 (each row is a `// REENTRANCY:` comment in code). Store rows are tested by holding the unit behind the await with `SteppedBlockingExecutor` and acting on the actor meanwhile (ADR-045).
- `R-SYN-2: 10,000 seeded random operations never leave the transition table and keep every engine invariant`
- `R-INT-2: a manifest change during the publish rename yields superseded and never returns the URL`
- `R-CLN-5: two identifiers with identical bytes published concurrently keep the blob until both are removed`

**H-6 Background transfers across relaunch.** Opt-in background session; attempt-unique, self-describing staging names; launch order session → tasks → staging → reconcile → GC (ADR-013, ADR-026, ADR-028).
- `R-NET-3: a live background task is re-attached after relaunch, not duplicated`
- `R-NET-3: a download completed while the app was dead is adopted from staging, not re-downloaded`
- `R-NET-3: an offline launch cancels no live background task`
- `R-CLN-3: a staged file handed off by a relaunched background session survives launch staging GC`

**H-7 Double residency.** Adopt-and-release with durable commit before release (`AssetStore.adoptionReport()`, atomic), keep-scope exclusions from the retention scope, source bytes reported through `RemoteSource.sourceCopyBytes()` (ADR-027).
- `R-CLN-10: a media pack is released only after every catalog entry in it is adopted or excluded` *(exists)*
- `R-CLN-10: a pack is released only after the adopting generation is durably committed`
- `R-CLN-10: a publish that lands during the adoption report's durable commit is not reported adopted`
- `R-CLN-10: entries outside the retention scope never block a pack release`
- `R-APL-3: an adopted asset is served from blobs and never from a pack location`
- `R-CLN-9: storageReport counts bytes the OS still holds in adopted packs`

**H-8 Time and determinism.** `TimeSource` (wall, monotonic, awake, sleep), one engine alarm, keyed randomness, `ManualTimeSource` (ADR-017, ADR-022, ADR-024).
- `R-ENV-1: ManualTimeSource wakes sleepers in deadline then registration order` *(exists)*
- `R-ENV-1: a cancelled ManualTimeSource sleeper throws promptly and leaves no sleeper behind` *(exists)*
- `R-SYN-5: backoff delays are full jitter keyed by asset and attempt and reproducible from the seed` *(exists)*
- `R-SYN-5: no transfer starts before a 429's Retry-After elapses on ManualTimeSource`

**H-9 Backpressure.** Pull-based bounded queues, newest-wins progress at ≤ 10 Hz, never-dropped terminal events coalesced per subject, client lifecycle events never coalesced with each other (one slot each, L5-R19), bulk outcomes folded into run progress (ADR-023, ADR-054).
- `R-SYN-12: a stalled subscriber's backlog never exceeds one slot per progress key and subject during a 10,000-asset sync` (its bound is read before `clientShutDown`'s own slot fills, L5-R19)
- `R-SYN-12: clientShutDown is the last event every subscriber receives`
- `R-SYN-12: a subscriber that reads nothing between clientStarted and clientShutDown receives both, clientStarted first` *(exists; L5-R19, ADR-054)*
- `R-SYN-12: transfer progress published to subscribers never exceeds 10 Hz per asset under ManualTimeSource`

**H-10 Migration.** Adopt-by-hash via same-volume rename in committed phases; phase 2 after open with adopt-on-demand; stamp migrated only on full adoption; key removed only after a durable commit (ADR-029). Since the level-5 WP-K merge adopt-on-demand also serves every job's pre-transfer read while migration is pending, one adoption per legacy file whoever asks, and no migration call spins or suspends behind its own coalesced publication (L5K-R1, L5K-R13, L5K-R17, L5K-R20; §4.7, §7.1; ADR-055).
- `R-STO-11: legacy files whose checksum verifies are moved into blobs, not re-downloaded`
- `R-STO-5: crash at every boundary during legacy migration preserves I-1…I-5 and adopts every verified file`
- `R-STO-11: lookups hit adopted legacy files while migration is still running, and no snapshot precedes reconciliation`
- `R-SEC-2: a legacy entry whose directory component is a symbolic link is dropped and nothing outside the legacy root moves`
- `R-STO-11: an explicit sync during legacy migration adopts every prior-art file still pending and downloads none (CCR-K1)` *(exists; L5K-R1, ADR-055)*
- `R-STO-11: an adopt-on-demand of a legacy file a batch is placing waits for that adoption and answers the location it bound (L5K-R13)` *(exists; ADR-055)*

## 10. REENTRANCY ledger (H-5, C3)

Every `await` inside an actor — production and `ZTronAssetTestSupport` alike (§10.3) — carries `// REENTRANCY: <row> — <what is re-validated>`. Two checks follow **every** await in both actors and are not repeated per row: the lifecycle/phase (engine: `running`, else unwind with `.shutDown`; store: `open`, else drop the mutation, store-1 S1-09) and, in the engine, the job's epoch (a stale continuation is abandoned). A new await without a row is a review failure, unless its row is proposed in the work package's `docs/adr-drafts/<wp>.md` and cited as such in the comment (§1.2); the orchestrator copies proposed rows into this section at merge under their proposed identifiers (ADR-049, ADR-050, ADR-052, ADR-053, ADR-054, ADR-055). Rows R16–R18 and RE-S35–RE-S42 were pre-added in round 4 for awaits the contract already requires.

### 10.1 `AssetStore`

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| R1 | units inside `open()` | phase `opening`; async APIs await `open`; synchronous APIs threw `.notOpen` meanwhile (store-2 S2-07); an `open()` parked on a running open re-reads nothing: the running open hands it its report, or `nil` → `.notOpen` (B-35; ADR-050) |
| R2 | hash units in `publish` | every bound record still has the expected identity and revision, else `.superseded` before more work |
| R3 | `acquireReference` awaiting an in-flight publication or deletion of the same key | `blobTable[k]` re-read and decided anew (live → dedupe, doomed → rescue if `unreferenced`, absent → rename) (store-2 S2-01) |
| R4 | claim and rename units in `publish` | identity and revision again (store-2 S2-15); on a change `k` is doomed with `droppedInGeneration = 0`, no URL is returned; `publishing[k]` removed; every table update is read-modify-write |
| R5 | commit unit | `committedGeneration = max(…)` with the generation allocated at capture, and `committedFile` as the unit's renames left it, the pending promotion cleared once its name moved, `consecutiveFailures` reset or incremented (B-28, B-29; ADR-050); mutations made meanwhile stay dirty; on failure `dirtyDirectories` restored; no new commit once closing |
| R6 | `sink.install` (immediate, or the trailing install in `runDueWork`, §5) | the ack counts only if the sink installed this very snapshot (`retired != nil`, `installed == offered`), which implies `installed.epoch == launchEpoch` (B-17; ADR-050); `ackedVersion = max`; ack time recorded on the awake clock; accesses merged through `recordAccesses` (WP-B); `retired` released here, off main; a publication that arrived during the install keeps the trailing install due |
| R7 | deletion unit | keys in `.deleting` are gone, or back to `doomed` with backoff on failure; waiting publishers resumed |
| R8 | quota eviction's candidate unit (bulk) and the no-op metadata unit between its chunks (ADR-052: folds the R8 amendment; no code awaits `Task.yield()` under R8 any more) | the phase and writability, then per candidate against its holders as they are then (`holders(of:known:scan:)`, from at most one scan of the records per chunk, made after the release, the previous chunk's dropped), under rules whose clock is read again after it: still present at the selected blob, unleased, not being placed, not accessed since selection, still evictable; before each candidate the need against every byte released since the measurement (R13); a chunk is whole candidates up to `EvictionState.chunk` records (C-21) |
| R9 | purge's grace wait (in the caller's engine-owned task); `awaitReclaim`'s no-op metadata unit between chunks of its pass, and its wait for a key another reaper run is deleting (`waitForKey`, as R3) (ADR-052: folds the R9 amendments of cycles 2 and 8) | deletable state recomputed from scratch; phase. Between chunks: each later key is classified from the blob table as it is then; the round's figures only decide whether to wait, install or stop, and the next round (or the reaper) sees what changed. The key wait carries nothing across it: the key was read `deleting` in the turn the wait registered, so the end of its deletion unit (or `close()`) resumes it; then the caller's cancellation (`.cancelled`, as the grace wait), and the loop re-checks the phase, reaps and classifies every pending key from the blob table again (C-5) |
| R10 | listing units in `collectGarbage`; its decision unit (bulk) after the full listing and its no-op metadata unit between doom chunks (ADR-052: folds the R10 amendment) | the sweep set is computed after the full listing against `blobTable ∪ publishing`, `mtime < now − orphanGrace`, not degraded. After the decision unit and between chunks: the phase, the caller's cancellation (before the first chunk only: nothing doomed then), degraded or unavailable (nothing doomed); a key counts as unknown only if the snapshot taken before the first listing unit lacks it too; each chunk's turn checks the reap window (`reapWindowClosed`: no key learnt since the start can have been reaped) and every key again in O(1) against the blob table and `publishing` (`doomOrphans`); runs are serialized; a key a record started expecting meanwhile is doomed but stays rescuable until the grace ends (C-23) |
| R11 | migration units | the legacy file still exists, its blob is still absent, its record still expects it |
| R12 | `runDueWork`'s fresh no-op install | the awake-clock deadline passed **before** the install; deletable state recomputed after the ack (ADR-017) |
| R13 | `freeSpace()` unit | the figures are a snapshot; `apply` re-validates each eviction victim. In quota eviction (ADR-052: folds the R13 amendments of cycles 6 and 7) `EvictionState.releasedBytes` is read in the turn the figures were summed, and every byte an eviction, an `apply`'s plan, a purge, a quarantine, a recorded failure or a record's last integrity failure releases afterwards (this run's included) counts toward the need, re-checked before each candidate, so a run that awaited while another released bytes frees only what is still needed (C-2) |
| R14 | `stagingInventory()` / `collectStaging` listing units | the kept set is the caller's claims at sweep time plus `stagingClaimGrace`; a file of ours older than `stagingMaxAge` goes even when claimed; foreign names go at once; the store adds no reservations of its own (C-9; ADR-052) |
| R15 | `adoptionReport()`'s durable commit | nothing: the report is the state captured **before** the await (manifest paths, present records, retention-scope exclusions); mutations made meanwhile are not reported; on failure throw and report nothing (store-1 S1-10) |
| R16 | hash units in `verifyIntegrity` (`hashFile(at:)`, WP-C), and the stat units around each hash (`blobIncarnation`; ADR-052: folds the R16 amendment) | the record still references the same blob (`present(blob:)` unchanged), the blob is still `live` (neither doomed nor deleting) and the store is not degraded; the identity and size under the name are the same before and after the hash and the key is not in `publishing`, else the result is discarded and the key stays due in this pass (C-8); a blob no longer live or without holders, or a store degraded, after that check: the result is discarded and the key counts as done in this pass; a mismatch quarantines through `setResidency(_:of:dooming: .corrupt)`; deadline and byte budget re-read |
| R17 | WP-C's trailing commit and snapshot awaits (`requireCommitted`, `publishSnapshot`) in `setPinned`, `recordFailure`, `recordIntegrityFailure`, `reportUnreadable`, `adoptLegacyNow`, `evictIfNeeded`, `purge` (its snapshot install and ordered commit after the last chunk; ADR-052) and the migration phases | nothing but the phase: every mutation was made synchronously before the await, so a later call's mutation is newer state and wins; the result reports what was captured before the await; a failed commit leaves the state dirty (R5). Except `migrateLegacy`'s batch publication, after which the queue, the claims and `installDue` are read again: an install that suspends at the sink lets an adopt-on-demand of the last claimed file end meanwhile (L5K-R23, §4.7; ADR-055) |
| R18 | `adoptLegacyNow`'s hash and adoption units (`hashFile`, `adoptVerifiedFile`) | as R11, plus the id is still un-adopted: a `migrateLegacy` batch that adopted it meanwhile wins and the location is read from the record; one adoption per legacy file, whoever asks (rows k-5, k-6, §10.7; L5K-R13, ADR-055) |
| b-1 | `close()`'s drain wait and its lock-release unit | the drain loop re-reads `unitsInFlight`; units resuming meanwhile see `closing` and drop their mutation; nothing after the release (closing is terminal) |
| b-2 | `requireCommitted`'s sleep until the coalescing window opens (caller's task) | the loop recomputes coverage, the in-flight flag, the window and the phase from scratch (the timer only pokes, as R9) |
| b-3 | the discard unit before a publication throws (`.unverifiedRejected`, integrity failure, superseded, cancelled claim hand-back) | nothing: the call throws right after |
| b-4 | the copy units of an extra-extension blob (`copyBlob`) | the phase; the copy has a name no one else writes (store-allocated sequence) and its destination key is held in `publishing`, so no other binder writes or places it; the bytes are verified (size and hash) before placement (B-18) |
| b-5 | `apply`'s orphan re-stat unit | the phase; a candidate counts only if it is still an orphan of the same size and still unknown to the blob table |
| b-6 | `apply`'s plan unit (`ApplyPlan.compute` on the bulk executor) | the phase and writability; `manifestRevision` unchanged, else the plan is made again; the writes re-read the live records and `adoptKnownBlob` re-validates every candidate (B-19) |
| b-7 | the `apply`/`admit` gate wait, and `Task.yield()` between `apply`'s chunks | the gate and the phase after the wait; after a yield nothing stops the writes (fix round 2, B-23): the commit fence is up, so no commit, adoption report or `close()` captures the half-applied state, and the writes run to the end even once closing; per item the record it touches is re-read (inserts replace through `updateValue`, adoption through `adoptKnownBlob`, prunes remove only records still present); the plan and the applied manifest cannot change meanwhile (gate); phase and writability are re-checked by `apply`'s commit after the last write (B-19) |
| b-8 | the commit-fence wait (`ApplyGate.writeWaiters`) in `requireCommitted`, `adoptionReport()` and `close()` | `requireCommitted`: its loop re-reads coverage, the phase, writability, the fence and the in-flight flag; `adoptionReport()`: the phase, writability and the fence before it captures anything; `close()`: the drain counter and the fence, then its durable commit (B-23) |
| b-9 | a later `close()`'s wait for the running one (`closeWaiters`, fix round 3) | the phase: the loop returns only on `closed`, which the running close sets after its durable commit and lock release, then resumes the waiters (review round 3, probe 2) |
| c-1 | the removal units of `collectStaging` and legacy phase 4 (`removeInUnits`, `removeAllInUnits`) | the phase; the next unit starts only after the caller's cancellation is checked (a cancellation ends the loop with the partial result, which the caller accounts for before it throws `.cancelled`, C-4); a failed removal does not stop the loop. (Since review round 3 orphan GC dooms instead of removing, C-23, so the per-unit re-check and the deadline are gone.) |
| c-2 | `storageReport()`'s staging listing unit, and its figures unit (bulk) | nothing: the sum and the figures are snapshots; the pending and orphan bytes are read from the state after the await (O(1) per lease and per orphan) |
| c-3 | `verifyIntegrity`'s trailing snapshot install | nothing but the phase (as R17): the quarantines were made synchronously before it and the report describes them |
