| c-4 | legacy phase 4's stats, manifest read, listing, removal-set unit (bulk) and removal units and the `UserDefaults` key removal; the key removal of phase 1 when no tree exists; the read and listing units of phases 1 and 4 (`readLegacyFile`, `listLegacyTree`; each listing unit also stats its directory, and the first one the store's, `LegacyListingUnit`); phase 1's bulk unit (folds the c-4 amendment) | after the durable "done" commit (or with no tree before migration) the prior art's files and the key are the migration's alone, and every step is idempotent; the root's kind is a snapshot taken without following it, and anything but a directory is unlinked, never listed; only items of the non-following listing that the prior art could have written are removed (C-15). The phase after every read and listing unit, in phase 4 also the caller's cancellation; a read or listing that failed decides nothing (phase 1) or removes nothing (phase 4), and an I/O error on either stat of a listing unit fails the walk as a failed listing (C-13, C-15); the key goes only once the tree is done. Phase 1's bulk unit builds the records and the queue from snapshots that nothing else writes while the store is `opening` (R1), and they replace the store's whole (C-24) |
| c-5 | `purge`'s selection unit (bulk) and its no-op metadata unit between chunks | the phase and writability; per record (`purgeChunk`): it still references the blob recorded at selection, and for `.evictable` (per blob: chunks end at blob boundaries) the blob is still live, unleased and not being placed and every current holder (`holders(of:known:scan:)`, from at most one scan of the records per chunk) is still neither essential nor pinned, else the whole blob stays; the epoch and the stamp were written before the first await and are written again in the turn of the last chunk when it purged something (C-5) |
| c-6 | `verifyIntegrity`'s due-list unit (bulk) | the phase and writability; per blob before its hash: live, not being placed, not verified in this pass meanwhile; after it R16; the holders read again with `holders(of:known:scan:)`, the blob's check and its quarantine sharing at most one scan of the records (C-8, C-21) |
| c-7 | phase 3's no-op metadata unit between chunks of its check (`legacyEntriesPresent`), and the return to `finishLegacyMigration` | the phase after each release; the pass answers "present" only if `commits.mutationSerial` is unchanged since it began (else it starts again; after three interrupted passes the call decides nothing and the next call checks again, C-24); a missing entry is missing in the turn it is found; then writability, and the migration's phase, queue and claims (a call that finished migration meanwhile, or a file back in the queue, leaves it) (C-24) |
| c-8 | `apply`'s eviction of a plan larger than `EvictionState.chunk` records (`applyEvictionInChunks`, `AssetStore+Apply.swift`; ruling R6): the bulk unit that cuts the plan (`ApplyEvictionChunks`), the no-op metadata unit between chunks, and, for a plan that names a blob apart, the bulk unit that restores plan order | nothing stops the writes (as b-7): the fence is up, so nothing persists or reports the half-done eviction and `close()` waits for it; the cut and the reorder are pure computations over the plan (a value) and the ids evicted; the hook re-validates each chunk's victims in that chunk's turn (still present at the planned blob, unleased, not being placed, not accessed since the plan, still evictable under rules whose clock is read in that turn, against their holders as they are then, from that turn's own scan), so a victim accessed, leased, pinned, purged or re-bound meanwhile is kept; the bytes each chunk dooms count toward a running maintenance eviction's need in that turn (R13) |

Rows b-1…b-9 are WP-B's, proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/b.md` and kept under those identifiers (ADR-050); the code cites them as "b-n (proposed)". Rows c-1…c-8 are WP-C's, proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/c.md` and kept under those identifiers (ADR-052); the code cites them as "c-n (proposed)". The amendments WP-C proposed to R8, R9 (cycles 2 and 8), R10, R13 (cycles 6 and 7), R16 and c-4 are folded into those rows; the code cites the base row (R8, R9, R13, R16, c-4), except "R10 amendment" (`Cleanup/OrphanCollection.swift`) and "R9 amendment (cycle 8)" (`Cleanup/StorePurge.swift`), which name the folded text. Two citations name another row than the one whose text covers them: legacy phase 1's bulk unit cites "R1, R11" and phase 1's key removal without a tree "R1" (`Migration/LegacyPhases.swift`); row c-4 describes both (ADR-052). Every await in WP-C's four directories, `StoreInternals.swift` and `AssetStore+Apply.swift` has a `// REENTRANCY:` line. Row g-12, the await in WP-B's `AssetStore.verifyRootNow()` (L5-R20), is in §10.6 with WP-G's rows (L5-R23; ADR-054). Rows k-5…k-7, WP-K's awaits in `Migration/` (the waits of one adoption per legacy file, L5K-R13, and a batch's wait for an adopt-on-demand of the last claimed files, L5K-R17), are in §10.7, and R17 names the batch publication's re-read since L5K-R23; every await WP-K added in `Migration/` has a `// REENTRANCY:` line (ADR-055).

### 10.2 `SyncEngine` (sync.md §5.4 as amended)

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| RE-S01 | `start` task: `store.open()` | L; if shutdown began, close the store and finish with `.shutDown`; the snapshot is adopted only if newer |
| RE-S02 | `start`: `appVersion.currentAppVersion()` | L |
| RE-S03 | `start`: `remoteSources.makeRemoteSource(…)` | L; a source built after shutdown began is invalidated at once |

| RE-S05 | run: decode on the bulk executor | L; run epoch |
| RE-S06 | run: `store.freeSpace()` | L; run epoch |
| RE-S07 | run: planner off the actor | L; run epoch; the store may have advanced (tolerated: `apply` diffs against current state, ADR-030) |
| RE-S08 | *(removed: LRU stamps arrive through `InstallAck`, X-5)* | — |
| RE-S09 | run: `store.apply(manifest, etag:, eviction:)` | jobs of pruned ids → T21; jobs whose identity changed → T22; no generation guard (sync-1 S1-12) |
| RE-S10 | run: awaiting run completion (token table) | L; the outcome is computed from the run ledger at resume time |
| RE-S11 | run: `store.markSynced(_:ifPurgeEpoch:)` | L; `.purgeRaced` leaves the sync due |
| RE-S12 | driver: `remote.transfer(request)` | E; job still `downloading` with interests; otherwise the handle is cancelled at once |
| RE-S13 | driver: each element of `handle.updates` | E; terminal elements apply T5/T6/T7; a stream that ends without one fails the job (sync-1 S1-11) |
| RE-S14 | driver: `store.publish(staged, binding:, expecting:, urgency:)` (urgency = the job's lane, §5) | E; `.superseded` → T22/T11; the result goes to the interests present **now** |
| RE-S15 | *(merged into RE-S14: publication is one call, X-3)* | — |
| RE-S16 | alarm: `time.sleep(until:)`; the held-turn test seam (`awaitAlarmTurnRelease`, reached only while `holdAlarmTurns()` is armed) and `fireDue()` (ADR-053) | the alarm token is current; due entries recomputed from `monotonicNow()`, never from the scheduled deadline |
| RE-S17 | watcher: `conditions.conditions()` | watcher token current and still needed, else the stream is dropped |
| RE-S18 | watcher: each element | watcher token; L; apply, then `pump()`; exit when idle |
| RE-S19 | point admission: manifest fetch | each unknown-id waiter still registered; answered from the newest manifest seen (sync-1 S1-13) |
| RE-S20 | `ensureAvailable`: group completion | L; group still registered; report recomputed from the store |
| RE-S21 | shutdown: joining owned tasks; an owned task's single hop onto the actor (pump, re-admission, space-sweep, release and throttle-answer follow-ups, the alarm and run spawns, entry tasks; ADR-053) | lifecycle `shuttingDown`; nothing new spawns; the loop iterates a copy. For a hop: nothing; the callee re-validates the lifecycle, and what it reads, after each of its awaits |
| RE-S22 | shutdown: `remote.invalidate()` then `store.close()` | — (terminal); invalidate returns after the **foreground** delegate's last callback (transport-2 T2-04) and the Apple source's in-flight `writeStaged`; a background session is released without waiting (ADR-039) |
| RE-S23 | maintenance: each store batch | L; deadline; `Task.isCancelled` |
| RE-S24 | purge: `store.purge(request)` | `purgeEpoch` was bumped before the await; afterwards jobs for purged ids are removed or re-queued (user waiters) |
| RE-S25 | the `join` family (`withTaskCancellationHandler` + `withCheckedContinuation`) | nothing: the body runs synchronously on the actor |
| RE-S26 | every entry point awaiting `start` | L (start may have degraded or shutdown begun) |
| RE-S27 | alarm: `store.runDueWork()` | L; reaped bytes wake `awaitingSpace` jobs; re-arm from the store's new `nextDeadline()` (ADR-022), applied through `applyStoreDeadline`: when another update of the store's deadline landed while the call was in flight, the earlier of the two deadlines is kept (L5K-R16; ADR-055) |
| RE-S28 | `start`: `remote.reattachedTransfers()` (activates the background session), `store.stagingInventory()` | L; the reconciliation plan is computed after all three, in that order (ADR-028) |
| RE-S29 | migration batch: `store.migrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` | L; calls again while `!progress.finished` and the lifecycle is running; when a call returns `remaining == 0 && !finished` (phase 3 left its decision to a later call, or phase 4 could not finish) it backs off, for example to the next maintenance window, rather than looping (ruling R9; ADR-052) |
| RE-S30 | entry-point owned tasks (purge, leases, pins, maintenance, `reportUnreadable`) | L; the caller's waiter still registered; a lease acquired after shutdown began is released at once |
| RE-S31 | memory-pressure observer: each element, then the `handler` call | L; the observer's epoch is still the applied one, else exit; a stop requested from inside the handler ends the loop after it returns |
| RE-S32 | `storageReport`: `store.storageReport()`, then `remote.sourceCopyBytes()` | L; the two figures are independent snapshots; 0 when no source exists yet |
| RE-S33 | `setRetentionScope`: owned task pushing the scope to the store | L; a scope set meanwhile has a newer sequence number and wins (the stale push is skipped) |
| RE-S34 | pack release: `store.adoptionReport()`, then `remote.reclaimSourceCopies(report)` | L; the report is the store's durable capture (R15); a release that races an eviction of a reported entry costs only a re-download, never data |
| RE-S35 | driver / failure path: `store.recordFailure(ids, identity:, reason:)`, `store.recordIntegrityFailure(ids, identity:)` (§7.6, ADR-032) | L; E; the job still exists with the content identity that failed, else the outcome is dropped (the store re-checks identity itself and leaves changed records alone, and in `recordFailure` also records bound to a live blob at that identity, L4-R16; the engine re-reads presence before the call, f-10; ADR-053); the job's next state comes from the disposition computed before the await |
| RE-S36 | `awaitAsset` and `acquireLease`: `store.location(for:extensions:recordingAccess:)`, then `store.adoptLegacyNow(id)` | L; the waiter is still registered, else nothing is answered; a `nil` location falls through to the job path, which re-reads the record |
| RE-S37 | run and point admission: `store.planningView()`, `store.evictionPlan(toFree:protecting:)`, `store.admit(entry)` | L; run epoch (run) or the waiter still registered (admission); the view and the eviction plan are snapshots: `apply` diffs against current state and re-validates every victim (ADR-030, R8) |
| RE-S38 | read-only entry points: `store.availability(of:)`, `store.planningView()` (`isSyncDue`, `lastSyncedAt`), `store.metrics()` (`metrics`, `diagnostics`) | L; the answer is the store's snapshot at the time it answered; nothing engine-side is mutated afterwards |
| RE-S39 | `start` after launch reconciliation: `store.collectStaging(keeping:)`; any path that makes a sync due: `store.markSyncDue()` | L; the kept set was computed before the await; the engine claims every transfer it still works on, those with a publication in flight included, because the store adds no reservations of its own (R14, C-9; ADR-052); `markSyncDue` is idempotent |
| RE-S40 | re-arming outside the alarm: `store.nextDeadline()` after a lease release, publish, apply or purge, and, since the level-5 WP-K merge, after every other store call that can leave an install due (L5K-R16's enumeration; ADR-055): the pre-transfer read, after each bulk-urgency adoption (k-4; L5K-R23), each `migrateLegacy` call, returned or thrown (RE-S29), point admission's `admit`, launch reconciliation's publication, a failed maintenance step, a failed `reportUnreadable`, `recordFailure`/`recordIntegrityFailure` when the job ended meanwhile, and `publish` when the job ended meanwhile or the store's call threw | L; the alarm token re-read; the heap's store entry is replaced by the newest deadline and a stale one is dropped; an answer applied after another update of the store's deadline landed while it was in flight keeps the earlier of the two deadlines (`applyStoreDeadline`): the store answers in its order and the engine resumes in priority order, so the two may have crossed (L5K-R16) |
| RE-S41 | launch reconciliation and preemption: `remote.cancelTransfer(name)` | L; the plan's remaining steps run against the inventory captured before the first cancel; a cancel of a transfer that ended meanwhile is a no-op at the source |
| RE-S42 | `backgroundSessionEventsPending`: `remote.handleBackgroundEvents(identifier:)` | L; the relay's completion runs once, after the events that preceded it were staged; after shutdown it runs at once (§6.4) |
| f-1 | `runStart`: `store.setRetentionScope` (after `open()`, and again in a loop just before the engine is marked running), `store.makeStagingSink` | The lifecycle (shutdown may have begun). `setRetentionScope` while start runs only replaces the engine's copy, so start pushes again while the sequence it pushed is not the newest; the engine is marked running in the turn of the last comparison, after which every scope pushes itself (fix round 1, F-18). A failed `makeStagingSink` degrades the engine (no start task is left to settle later callers). |
| f-2 | `drive`/`resumePoint`: sidecar reads for the next attempt's resume point; launch reconciliation's sidecar read for a launch resume (ADR-053) | The lifecycle and the job epoch; the resume point is kept only for the job (and attempt) that read it, and at launch only for a transfer still wanted (a vanished sidecar means a fresh start). Since close-out cycle 10 (F-30), in `paused` also the lane's block under the conditions then current: a policy pause parks the job only while its lane is still refused (with the block it gives now), and re-queues it at the front otherwise. |
| f-3 | `staging.discard(_:)` of copies and partials nobody wants | Nothing: the removal is idempotent and completes whatever happens meanwhile; the callers' following steps re-read the job by epoch. |
| f-4 | `refreshFreeSpace`, `spaceFailed`: `store.freeSpace()`; since close-out cycle 6 (F-26) also `settleSpaceWaitsOnFreshRead` (the read that decides jobs parked `awaitingSpace` with no wake left) and `spaceMayHaveBeenReclaimed` (after a purge); since close-out cycle 8 (F-28) the read after the alarm's reaped bytes | The lifecycle, and in `spaceFailed` the job epoch; the headroom ledger's base is replaced whole by the fresh read (S2-09: committed bytes stay until the next read). In `settleSpaceWaitsOnFreshRead` every job's state (only those still `awaitingSpace` are decided) and the wakes armed meanwhile (a store deadline, the alarm's store work) are re-read after it; in `spaceMayHaveBeenReclaimed` the jobs still parked `awaitingSpace` are re-queued and gated on it. These three read through `readFreeSpace` and then re-check every job parked for space in one sweep (`sweepSpaceParked(.reclaimed)` or `(.settle)`), which re-reads each job's state and the wakes (F-28). |
| f-5 | `performAwaitAsset`: `store.planningView()` before the join | The lifecycle; the record (quarantined, failed, present) decides T2 before the join, which is synchronous. Since close-out cycle 3 (F-23) the view's manifest is followed only through `followStoreView` (not when an `apply` was in flight during the read or the engine's copy changed since it began), and the id's binding is recorded only when no binding was forgotten during the reads. |
| f-6 | `EventSubscription` (the stream's pulling actor): `engine.pull`, `engine.unsubscribe` | `done` once the stream is over; `next()` re-reads its own state after every await; a cancellation during registration releases at once; a released stream unsubscribes once. |
| f-7 | `waitForConditions` (Apple-tier and update-checking manifest fetches wait for the engine's own `DeviceConditions`, E-17) | Resumed by the first conditions element or by shutdown; the lifecycle and the gate are re-read after it. |
| f-8 | Test seams `waitUntil`, `awaitCondition`, `waitForHandle`, `waitForJobEnd`, `waitForAllHandles`; since close-out cycle 5 the apply follow-up hold in `applyAndFollow` (`holdNextApplyFollow`, armed only by tests); since the level-5 WP-K merge the store-deadline read hold in `refreshStoreDeadline` (`holdNextStoreDeadlineRead()`, armed only by tests; L5K-R16, ratified by L5K-R22; ADR-055) | Nothing: terminal waits registered synchronously, resumed exactly once by the mutation that meets them, by their caller's cancellation (CensusWaiting pattern, l2fu-1) or by shutdown; the held follow-up is resumed once by `releaseApplyFollow()` or by shutdown and then re-reads the lifecycle as it does without the hold, and so is the held re-read, by `releaseStoreDeadlineRead()` or by shutdown. |
| f-9 | `catchUpWithStore`: `store.planningView()` after the store superseded content the engine's copy still names (fix round 2, F-20) | The lifecycle; the copy is replaced only by a store view whose manifest differs from it (then T21/T22 follow, `followApplied`), and the held jobs are released in the same turn (`manifestCaughtUp`). Since close-out cycle 1 (L4-R7) the same turn also announces the ids the view no longer lists (`assetRemoved(.pruned)`), takes the view's generation, publishes `snapshotChanged`, and re-checks every remembered binding against the view (L4-R5). Since close-out cycle 3 (F-23) the view is followed only when no `apply` was in flight during the read and the copy did not change since it began (`appliesInFlight`, `copySerial`); otherwise the read is owed again (`catchUpAfterFailedApply`) once no `apply` is in flight. |
| f-10 | `failed` (`permanentMissing`): `store.location(for:extensions:recordingAccess:)` per id of the job, before `store.recordFailure` (close-out cycle 1, L4-R5); since close-out cycle 3 the same reads (`sparePresent`) before `store.recordIntegrityFailure` in `integrityFailed` (transport and publication integrity failures, F-23) | The lifecycle and the job epoch: the ids the store serves at their current content (a location that is not stale) while the engine's copy maps them to the failed content are answered from the store (only the interests the job still holds then) and left out of the failure; the failure is recorded for the job's other ids whether or not the job still exists; the present ids' bindings are recorded only when no binding was forgotten during the reads. A binding the store makes between the read and the store write is not seen by the engine; since close-out cycle 11 the store's guard leaves such a record unchanged in `recordFailure` (L4-R16, F-31), while `recordIntegrityFailure` keeps the window (F-21; L4-R24(3), Stage 3). |
| f-11 | `drive` → `answeredFromStore`: `store.location(for:extensions:recordingAccess:)` per id of the job, or, while legacy migration is pending, `store.adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency:)` at `.userInitiated` for a job of the user-initiated lane and `.bulk` otherwise; after each bulk-urgency read, the RE-S40 re-read of `store.nextDeadline()`; the same reads of the ids the job gained meanwhile, until none is new; then, when the job holds a user-initiated interest and an id was answered by a read that installed nothing at once, one `store.adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency: .userInitiated)` (when that id is no longer present, the earlier such ids, latest first; a thrown error stops it), and the reads of any id that joined during it (row k-4; L5K-R1, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R21, L5K-R23, L5K-R25; ADR-055); all before `remote.transfer` (close-out cycle 3, F-23) | The lifecycle, the job epoch and the attempt (still without a handle): the ids the store serves at their current content while the engine's copy maps them to this content are answered from the store (only the interests the job holds then); an attempt left with no interest ends before it asks for bytes (marked terminal, so no drain is recorded, and retired); an attempt the reads leave no longer current (its job gone, or its epoch or attempt moved on) sends no request: `drive` returns before `remote.transfer`, ends the attempt's draining record and pumps (L5K-R25; an engine no longer running sends none either, F-24); an attempt with an absent id goes on to `remote.transfer`, whose existing RE-S12 checks apply. Bindings are recorded only when no binding was forgotten during the reads (an adoption is such a binding). The job's interests are checked for a user-initiated one after the last ordinary await, and nothing is awaited between the publication that check may make and the answer but the reads of ids that joined during it, after which the check is made again (L5K-R21, L5K-R23). |
| f-12 | `performReportUnreadable` → `blobHolders`: `store.planningView()` and the off-actor scan of its records for the reported id's blob, before `store.reportUnreadable` (close-out cycle 3, F-23) | Nothing: the holders are a snapshot; after the quarantine each one is re-read from the records `scheduleHeals` reads (its RE-S30 read) and announced and healed only if quarantined or failed then; the ledger is dropped whatever the snapshot found. |
| f-13 | `applyAndFollow` → `takeApplyTurn`: waiting for the engine's apply turn (close-out cycle 5, F-25) | The lifecycle and the turn: resumed once by `passApplyTurn` (after the previous `apply` was followed) or by shutdown (`releaseApplyQueue`); the loop re-reads the turn (an `apply` that never waited may have taken it first; the waiter keeps its ticket) and an `apply` whose turn came after the engine stopped running sends nothing to the store (`.failure(.closed)`). |
| f-14 | `joinGroup`: the `Task.yield()` between batches of a prefetch group's members (close-out cycle 8, F-28) | The lifecycle, the source and the group still registered (a release drops it); the engine's manifest copy: a changed copy sends the members left through a fresh `planningView()` (this function again), whose view is followed only when the read cannot predate the copy. Each member's binding is checked as before (L4-R5). |

Rows f-1…f-14 are WP-F's, proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/f.md` and kept under those identifiers (L4-R13; ADR-053); the code cites them as "f-n (proposed)". f-2 also names launch reconciliation's sidecar read, which the code cites as f-2. RE-S16 and RE-S21 are widened to the awaits the code cites them for (the alarm-turn test seam and `fireDue()`; an owned task's single hop onto the actor, about 27 of RE-S21's 29 citations since the level-5 WP-K merge added the lease release's spawn hop), and RE-S35 names the store's guard (L4-R16). Every `// REENTRANCY:` in `ZTronAssetSync` cites an f-n row, an RE-S row or, since the level-5 WP-K merge, k-4 (§10.7) (scripted at the level-4 merge: each of f-1…f-14 is cited at least once, and no other f-n; again at the level-5 WP-K merge, with RE-S40 cited 31 times and f-8 9 times). The level-5 merge adds no engine row: WP-G's edits under L5-R1, L5-R2, L5-R15, L5-R19 and L5-R21 add no await (the pending claims are synchronous bookkeeping; the release follow-up, a join's large claim give-up and the shutdown from `created` reuse RE-S21's spawn and join loop; `spawnJoin` keeps RE-S30) (ADR-054). The level-5 WP-K merge adds row k-4 (§10.7: the pre-transfer read's adoptions, L5K-R1) and widens f-8, f-11, RE-S27 and RE-S40 (above); its other awaits reuse RE-S21 and RE-S40 (ADR-055).

### 10.3 `ZTronAssetTestSupport` actors (round 3)

C3 binds every actor in `Sources/`, the test fakes included. Each await of TS1–TS3 is a terminal wait or runs outside the actor, so the row says what is (not) re-read; the `LoopbackHTTPServer` rows h-5…h-7 re-read `stopped`. A new await in TestSupport needs a row here.

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| TS1 | `FakeDeviceConditionsSource` / `FakeMemoryPressureSource`: `onTermination`'s `Task { await self?.removeObserver(id) }` | nothing: the task is not the actor; `removeObserver` re-reads the table on the actor and a second removal of the same id is a no-op |
| TS2 | `waitForObservers(equalTo:)` and `requireObservers(equalTo:)` (both sources), `SteppedBlockingExecutor.waitForHeldUnits(atLeast:)` and `requireHeldUnits(atLeast:)`, `ScriptedSnapshotSink.waitForInstalls(atLeast:)` and `requireInstalls(atLeast:)`, and their `registered:` test seams, all through `CensusWaiting.awaitCensus` (l2fu-1) | nothing: a terminal wait; the waiter is registered synchronously in the continuation body and resumed exactly once, by the actor turn that reached the count or by the caller's cancellation (l2fu-1, l2fu-2: the `Void` forms then return with the census unmet, the `require…` forms throw `CancellationError`, the seams return `false`; ADR-050); no state is read afterwards (the caller re-reads the census) |
| TS3 | `SteppedBlockingExecutor.run(_:)` while holding | nothing to re-validate: `sequence` is the call's own local, its gate was removed by the release that resumed it, and `completed` is append-only, so units released out of order record completion in the order they ran; other units running meanwhile is the interleaving the executor exists to expose (H-5) |
| h-1 | `EventProbe.next(where:)`: the waiter's continuation | nothing: a terminal wait; registered synchronously in the continuation body and resumed only by its removal from `waiters` (a matching event, finish, stop or the caller's cancellation) |
| h-2 | `EventProbe`'s consumer task (created in `init`): each `await self.receive(event)`, the stop-signal child, `group.next()`, `finish()` | nothing: the task is not the actor; `receive` appends on the actor in stream order and `finish` is idempotent |
| h-3 | `EventProbe.next`'s `onCancel` task: `await self.cancelWaiter(id)` | nothing: the task is not the actor; `cancelWaiter` re-reads the table and is a no-op for a waiter already resumed |
| h-4 | `EventProbe.waitForWaiters(atLeast:)` and its `registered:` seam | nothing: a terminal wait as TS2 (through `awaitCensus`, l2fu-1), resumed by the count, by `finish()` or by the caller's cancellation (both `false`; ADR-050) |
| h-5 | `LoopbackHTTPServer`: the listener's `newConnectionHandler` task (`accept`) and the per-connection task (`serve`) | nothing: the tasks are not the actor's turn; `accept` re-reads `stopped` and cancels a late connection, `serve` re-reads `stopped` after each of its awaits |
| h-6 | `LoopbackHTTPServer.start()`: the waiter's continuation (the listener coming up), inside `withTaskCancellationHandler` | `stopped`: a start resumed with a port throws if `stop()` ran before its turn. The waiter is registered by id synchronously in the continuation body and resumed exactly once, only by actor code that removed it first: `resumeStartWaiters` (empties the list) with the port on `.ready`, with the error on `.failed`/`.waiting`, with `.stopped` from `stop()`; `cancelStartWaiter` with `CancellationError` (fix round 3); concurrent starts wait for the one current listener |
| h-7 | `LoopbackHTTPServer.serve`/`respond`: `respond`, `receive`, the injected delay, `send` | `stopped`: no response is sent and no connection is served after `stop()`; the request log entry was appended before the await; a closing connection removes only its own entry |
| h-8 | `LoopbackHTTPServer`: the listener's `stateUpdateHandler` task (`listenerStateChanged`); `receive`/`send` (nonisolated static bridges) | nothing: the handler task is not the actor's turn, and `listenerStateChanged` ignores a state of any listener but the current one (generation number) and a repeated `.ready`, however late, often or rarely Network reports it (a `.failed`/`.waiting` after `.ready` drops the listener and its port, fix round 3); the bridges touch no actor state and Network calls each completion exactly once |
| h-9 | `LoopbackHTTPServer.start()`'s `onCancel` task: `await self.cancelStartWaiter(id)` (fix round 3) | nothing: the task is not the actor; `cancelStartWaiter` re-reads the table and is a no-op for a waiter already resumed |
| l2fu-1 | `CensusWaiting.awaitCensus` (`CensusWaiters.swift`): the continuation, inside `withTaskCancellationHandler` | nothing: a terminal wait, registered synchronously in the continuation body unless the census is already met (`true`) or the caller is already cancelled (`false`); resumed exactly once, by the census turn that met its target (`true`), by `cancelCensusWait(id)` (`false`) or by `resumeAll()` (`EventProbe.finish`, `false`); no state is read afterwards (the caller re-reads the census) |
| l2fu-2 | `awaitCensus`'s `onCancel` task: `await self.cancelCensusWait(id)` | nothing: the task is not the actor's turn; `cancelCensusWait` re-reads the table and is a no-op for a waiter the census already resumed |

Rows h-1…h-9 are WP-H's, proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/h.md` and kept under those identifiers (ADR-049; copied here at the level-2 merge, ADR-050); the code cites them as "h-n (proposed)". Rows l2fu-1 and l2fu-2 come from the Level 2 follow-up (`docs/adr-drafts/l2fu.md`, item 1), which also made TS2 and h-4 end on the caller's cancellation (the code cites "TS2 … (l2fu-1)" and "l2fu-n (proposed)"; ADR-050).

### 10.4 Transport (WP-D; `SessionGate`, `BackgroundTransferCoordinator`, `ObservationRegistry`; ADR-050)

Rows D-1…D-12 keep the identifiers proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/d.md` as their final numbers; the code cites them as "D-n (proposed)", the word being historical (as for h-n, ADR-049). They are REENTRANCY rows, distinct from the decision entries D-1…D-20 of the same draft.

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| D-1 | `SessionGate.invalidate`: the one reader task's `for await` over `sessionEvents`, and each caller's `withCheckedContinuation` | `finish()` re-reads only `waiters` (drains the callers registered by then); a caller re-reads `invalidated` when it registers, so one arriving after `finish()` returns at once |
| D-2 | `BackgroundTransferCoordinator.start`: `activate()`, `driver.snapshot()` | `invalidated`, the outcome buffer, the processed names, the cancelling names and the waiters are read only after both: a listed task whose terminal the pump processed meanwhile counts as gone (a new download is created, never a wait on a dead task), and a task a cancel or pause began to end meanwhile is not joined |
| D-3 | `start`: `driver.startDownload` | nothing: the waiter was registered before the await, so a completion that races ahead is delivered to it by the pump |
| D-4 | `liveTransfers`: `activate()`, `driver.snapshot()` | `priorities` is read after the listing |
| D-5 | `stop` (behind `cancel`, `control`, `consumerGone`): `activate()`, `driver.snapshot()`, `driver.cancel` | the name enters `cancelling` before the first await (so no start joins the task meanwhile) and leaves when its terminal is processed or the listing shows no such task; the task is looked up in the listing taken after every earlier await and a name processed meanwhile counts as gone; `pauses[name]` is re-read after `driver.cancel` (the cancellation may have been reported during it, then the pause is delivered here, or not yet, then by the pump); `control` and `consumerGone` check the attach token before their one await and read nothing after it |
| D-6 | `invalidate`: `driver.invalidate()` | `invalidated` was set before the await, so no waiter can be added meanwhile; tables are drained after it |
| D-7 | `reattached`: `activate()`, `driver.snapshot()`, `driver.cancel` of foreign tasks | until the last await only the listing's foreign identifiers are used; the report is built after it from current state: a listed task whose terminal was processed (or buffered) meanwhile is neither inserted into `live` nor reported running, and each buffered success is reported once and handed over |
| D-8 | `awaitBackgroundEvents`: `activate()`, `withCheckedContinuation` | `invalidated` and `unclaimedFinishes` are read after activation; the continuation is a terminal wait resumed by the pump or `invalidate()` |
| D-9 | `setPriority(_:of:token:)`: `driver.snapshot()`, `driver.setPriority` | the token is checked before the first await **and again after the listing** (a start may join the task and replace the attach meanwhile, review round 2); after the listing the name is re-checked against the processed terminals, so `priorities` is written only for a task still live; nothing is read after the last await |
| D-10 | `activate()`: `driver.activate()` | `events` is re-read: a concurrent caller may have stored the stream meanwhile; the driver returns one stream per session, the first stored is kept |
| D-11 | `ObservationRegistry.wait(for:registered:)`: `withCheckedContinuation` inside `withTaskCancellationHandler` (amended at the Level 2 close, row l2close-1) | nothing: a terminal wait registered synchronously in the continuation body (unless the count already matches or the caller is already cancelled), resumed exactly once by the `wake()` whose count matched (`true`) or by `cancel(id)` (`false`) |
| l2close-2 | `wait(for:registered:)`'s `onCancel` task: `await self.cancel(id)` (Level 2 close, ADR-050) | nothing: the task is not the actor's turn; `cancel(id)` re-reads the waiter table and is a no-op for a waiter `wake()` already resumed |
| D-12 | `BackgroundTransferCoordinator.startIfAdmitted`: `start(job)` | nothing is read after it; the reservation taken before it (in the same turn as the cap check) counts in `admits()` while it runs and is released after it, when the transfer is in `live` or was refused, so concurrent admissions never exceed `maxTasks` (review round 3) |

The coordinator's pump is a `Task.detached` (not actor code): each event hops onto the actor through `process(_:)`, which re-reads every table it touches. Control hops from `BackgroundTransferHandle` and the stream's `onTermination` are unstructured tasks outside the actor. No other actor await exists in `ZTronAssetTransport` (`LiveBackgroundSessionDriver`, `LiveDeviceConditionsSource` and `BackgroundTransferHandle` are classes; the holder actor inside `BackgroundSessionBackend.swift` does not await).

### 10.5 `ZTronAssetAppleHosted` actors (WP-E; `AppleSourceState`, `PackAvailabilityCoordinator`; ADR-050)

Rows e-1…e-8 keep the identifiers proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/e.md` as their final numbers; the code cites them as "e-n (proposed)", the word being historical. `AppleHostedRemoteSource` is a final class, not an actor.

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| e-1 | `AppleSourceState.drain()`: `withCheckedContinuation` | nothing: a terminal wait registered synchronously and resumed only by the `endStaging()` turn that reached zero; `close()` ran first, so nothing is admitted afterwards |
| e-2 | `AppleSourceState.scheduleUpdateCheck`'s task: `backend.checkForUpdates()` | `finishCheck` runs on the actor and only clears `checkTask` and appends findings; a `close()` meanwhile is harmless (no new check starts after close, and none starts while this one is recorded as running) |
| e-3 | `AppleSourceState.settleUpdateCheck()` (test seam): `task.value` | nothing: a terminal wait; the caller re-reads the report |
| e-4 | `PackAvailabilityCoordinator.ensure`: `withTaskCancellationHandler` + `withCheckedContinuation` | nothing is read after resumption (the outcome travels in the continuation); `join`, `leave`, `complete` and `close` each re-read the waiter and flight tables, and a waiter is resumed exactly once by whichever removes it |
| e-5 | `PackAvailabilityCoordinator.wait(for:registered:)`, and the `await self.wait(…)` calls in `waitForWaiters` / `waitForStaleCompletions` (test seams; cited at each call since fix round 2) | nothing: a terminal wait resumed by the turn that made the condition hold, by `close`, or by the cancellation turn; the caller re-reads the census |
| e-6 | the flight task created in `join`: `await PackFlight.fly(...)` | nothing runs after it; `fly` reports through `broadcast` and `complete`, which drop a stale flight by id |
| e-7 | `AppleSourceState.waitForDrain()` (test seam) | nothing: a terminal wait resumed by the `drain()` turn that registered its waiter, the `endStaging()` turn that reached zero, or the cancellation turn (e-8) |
| e-8 | `Task { await self.leave(id) / releaseConditionWaiters() / releaseDrainObservers() }` in the `onCancel` handlers of `ensure`, the coordinator's seam waits and `waitForDrain` | a hop onto the actor from a cancellation handler, which cannot touch actor state synchronously; the hopped call re-reads the table it changes, so a continuation already resumed and removed by the regular path is never resumed twice |

### 10.6 `ZTronAssetKit` actors (WP-G; `ClientLifecycle`, `EventForwarder`; ADR-054)

Rows g-1…g-12 keep the identifiers proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/g.md` as their final numbers; the code cites them as "g-n (proposed)", the word being historical. Row g-12's await is in WP-B's `AssetStore.swift` (`verifyRootNow()`, L5-R20); it is kept here with WP-G's rows (L5-R23), and §10.1 points to it. `AssetClient` is a final class, not an actor; the awaits of the nonisolated statics `EventForwarder.subscribed`, `pump` and `forward` run off the actor and need no row (g-7 covers the pump's entry).

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| g-1 | `ClientLifecycle.current()` and `reopen(replacing:)`: waiting for a running reopen's task (since round 2 not `observeMemoryPressure`, which never waits for a reopen, G-12) | `core` (the reopen installed it) and `closed`, read after the wait; a caller whose pair was already replaced gets the replacement without a second reopen |
| g-2 | `ClientLifecycle.performReopen` (and the reopen task's call of it): the old engine's `shutdown()`, then the mirror reset on the main actor | `closed` after each await: a shutdown meanwhile ends the reopen with no replacement (`endReopen(nil)`) |
| g-3 | `performReopen`: the replacement engine's `observeMemoryPressure` and `start()`; `observeMemoryPressure` on the current engine, called only while no reopen runs (G-12) | Nothing: the engine orders memory-pressure requests by epoch (a concurrent request with the same or a newer epoch wins either way), and a reopen that begins during the call applies the newest recorded request to its replacement; the start's result is left to the callers, whose calls start the engine too |
| g-4 | `ClientLifecycle.registerGroup`/`bind`: `current()`, then `engine.registerGroup`, then the registration seam (`nil` in production, G-10); `performReopen`'s re-registration loop | The group (a release meanwhile removed it: the engine token is given back), `closed` (a shutdown meanwhile: the token is given back and `.shutDown` reported) and the generation (a reopen meanwhile: bind again on the replacement); a group already bound to the current generation is left as it is; the reopen loop leaves a group whose bind is in flight to that bind, and stops once a newer reopen replaced its generation (that reopen's loop owns the rest) |
| g-5 | `ClientLifecycle.shutdown`: waiting for a running reopen, then the current engine's `shutdown()` | `core`, read after the wait (the reopen stops at its next check of `closed`); then nothing (terminal) |
| g-6 | `releaseGroup` / `bind`: `engine.releaseGroup` | Nothing: terminal for that engine token |
| g-7 | `EventForwarder.next`/`offer` (terminal waits resumed once by `offer`, `end` or `stop`), `next`'s registration of an `events` stream on its first pull (`ClientLifecycle.registerEvents`, close-out cycle 2, L5-R13), `stop()`'s and `end()`'s return of the follower entry (`unfollow`), the stream's unfolding closure, `cancel()`'s hop (from `onCancel` or from the `Consumer` token's deinit when the stream is dropped), the pump's entry | Nothing for the terminal waits: each is resumed exactly once (waiting consumers are queued, so none is overwritten); after the registration, `finished`: a consumer that stopped meanwhile leaves the registration unused (its stream goes with it) and gives the follower entry back; `stop()` and `end()` take the key before their await, so the entry is given back once; `next()` and `offer` re-read their state after every await; the pump runs off the actor and re-enters only through `offer`, `takeHandover` and `end`, and never follows or registers by itself (L5-R13); `handOver`, `takeHandover` and `registered` are synchronous, and a forwarder that finished, or that holds a stream of the handed generation or a newer one, drops a stream handed to it |
| g-8 | `ClientLifecycle.setRetentionScope`: `current()`, then `engine.setRetentionScope` | `closed` after the wait; after the apply, `closed`, the request epoch and the generation: a newer request or a replacement engine means another pass with the newest recorded scope (the engine records a scope on entry, whatever the caller's cancellation) |
| g-9 | `ClientLifecycle`'s test seam (`seam`, `nil` in production; G-10) at the start of `performReopen`, before the old engine's shutdown; in `performReopen` after the replacement was built, before the hand-over (`replacementBuilt`, close-out cycle 2, L5-R13: the event model's oracle subscribes there); in `performReopen` after the replacement's memory-pressure request (`replacementObservesPressure`, close-out, L5-R2); in `observeMemoryPressure`, after the request was recorded; in `shutdown()`, before it waits for a running reopen (its sixth point, in `bind`, is g-4's) | `performReopen` before the shutdown: nothing at the seam (`closed` is re-read after the shutdown, g-2); after the replacement was built: nothing (the hand-over re-reads the followers and `closed` at the top of each pass, g-10); after the pressure request: nothing (the group loop re-reads the generation, `bind` re-reads `closed`, and the start answers `.shutDown` after a shutdown); `observeMemoryPressure`: `closed` and `reopening`, re-read after the seam and before the current engine is called; `shutdown()`: nothing (the wait for the reopen follows, then `core` is read, g-5) |
| g-10 | `ClientLifecycle.performReopen`'s hand-over (L5-R3, rewritten in close-out cycle 2 for L5-R13): the replacement's `subscribe()` for each follower, then that forwarder's `handOver` | The followers and `closed`, re-read at the top of each pass (a follower recorded meanwhile is handed a stream too; a shutdown meanwhile installs nothing, and the forwarders take only a stream of an installed generation); the last look at the followers, the check of `closed` and the installation happen in one turn, so every follower recorded before the installation holds a stream of the replacement from its first event on; the replacement cannot publish meanwhile: it is `created` and not installed, so no caller reaches it |
| g-11 | `ClientLifecycle.register(_:)` (close-out cycle 2, L5-R13): the chosen engine's eager `subscribe()`, after the follower was recorded and the engine chosen in one turn; for a registration that engine answered `.shutDown` because a reopen retired it, the wait for that reopen; `registerEvents(_:)`'s call of `register` | After the answer: whether a reopen retired the chosen engine (`hasRetired`): the registration is then not repeated, because that reopen's hand-over carries it; after the wait: `closed` and `core` (a replacement installed since the follow turn holds a stream handed to this follower: success with no stream of its own; otherwise the client was shut down: the entry is given back and `.shutDown` reported); any other answer gives the entry back; `registerEvents` reads `core` only after a failed registration (the client is shut down: the last engine's stream, which yields only `clientShutDown`) |
| g-12 | `AssetStore.verifyRootNow()` (WP-B's `AssetStore.swift`, close-out cycle 3, L5-R20): the root check's unit on the metadata executor (`StoreLock.verifyRoot`, one stat of the lock file) | `unavailable`, re-read after the unit: a commit's detection meanwhile recorded the same reason, which is kept; the answer is about the root this store locked, so a close begun meanwhile changes nothing (the reason outlives the close, and `close()` drains the unit first) |

Every `// REENTRANCY:` in `ZTronAssetKit` cites a g-n row (scripted at the level-5 merge: g-1 ×3, g-2 ×3, g-3 ×3, g-4 ×5, g-5 ×2, g-6 ×2, g-7 ×9, g-8 ×2, g-9 ×5, g-10 ×2, g-11 ×3; g-12 ×1, in `AssetStore.swift`); every g-n of the draft is cited, and no other.

### 10.7 `ZTronAssetCompatibilityTests` and WP-K's engine and store rows (WP-K; ADR-055)

Rows k-1…k-7 keep the identifiers proposed in `docs/adr-drafts/k.md` as their final numbers (L5K-R28); the code cites them as "k-n (proposed)", the word being historical. k-1…k-3 are main-actor awaits of the shim `CDNSyncCoordinator` (a test-target type, ADR-033); k-4 is a `SyncEngine` await in WP-F's `EnginePresence.swift` (§10.2 points to it); k-5…k-7 are `AssetStore` awaits in WP-C's `Migration/LegacyPhases.swift` and `StoreMigration.swift` (§10.1 points to them). The shims' other awaits (`CDNAssetResolver`, `StaticManifestCDNProvider`, `NetworkAvailabilityMonitor`, whose registry belongs to one task) run in nonisolated types and need no row.

| Row | Await | Re-validated afterwards |
|---|---|---|
| k-1 | `CDNSyncCoordinator.run(_:)`: `client.subscribe()`, `client.sync(mode)`, then `follower.value`; and the `await self.run(_:)` of `syncIfNeeded()` and `verifyAndRepair()`, after which nothing is read (fix round 1) | Nothing of the coordinator is read across them. `progress` is written only by this call's follower while the run is in flight and by the outcome once the follower was cancelled **and awaited**, so no progress event lands after the outcome. A concurrent call joins the same single-flight run and publishes the same outcome (its follower may republish a `.syncing` value until its own outcome lands). |
| k-2 | the follower's `for await event in events` (a main-actor task) | Each event is applied in the turn it arrives, after `self` is re-read (weak: a released coordinator publishes nothing). Progress never moves backwards within a run by construction (sync.md §10.6: the engine's completed count is monotone and one subscription delivers a run's progress in order, newest-wins), so no guard is needed. |
| k-3 | `CDNSyncCoordinator.verifyFullyAvailable()`: `cdn.fetchManifest()` | Nothing of the coordinator; the client's snapshot is read after the fetch, so the comparison uses what is stored when the manifest arrived. |
| k-4 | `SyncEngine.storedLocation(of:lane:)` (`EnginePresence.swift`, WP-F's file, close-out rulings L5K-R1, L5K-R14, L5K-R16, L5K-R18, L5K-R21, L5K-R23, L5K-R25; `storedLocation(of:in:lane:)` in cycle 3): `store.adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency:)` while legacy migration is pending, called once per id of a job from `answeredFromStore` (row f-11) before any bytes, at `.userInitiated` for a job of the user-initiated lane and `.bulk` otherwise; and, since L5K-R21, `answeredFromStore`'s answer-site `store.adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency: .userInitiated)` | Nothing in the callee: `answeredFromStore` re-validates after its reads exactly as f-11 says (the lifecycle, the job epoch and the attempt still without a handle; the interests the job holds then; bindings recorded only when nothing was forgotten meanwhile, the adoption being such a binding). A thrown `StoreError` (the caller's cancellation at shutdown, a store that refuses) reads as "nothing stored", so the attempt of a running engine that is still current goes on to the transfer, as the resolve path's own adoption does (ADR-029; f-11 for the other exits). After each bulk-urgency read (one that answered nothing or threw included), before the job's next read, the store's deadline is re-read into the heap (RE-S40, O(1) while the alarm already wakes within about two install intervals), so that adoption's trailing install runs on the alarm however long the later reads take (L5K-R16; L5K-R23: L5K-R21 had moved the re-read after all the reads, L5K-C4-01). The ids the job gained during the reads (another id of the same content joining it) are read the same way, round after round until none is new (L5K-R23, L5K-C4-02). The last read, or its re-read, is the last ordinary await: the answer site then checks the job's interests (L5K-R21), and when the job holds a user-initiated interest and an id was answered by a read that installed nothing at once (a `.bulk` adoption, a present asset whose trailing install may still be due, a plain location read), one `adoptLegacyNow(id, urgency: .userInitiated)` installs the whole state at once (through the last such id; when that id is no longer present, through the earlier ones, latest first, until one answers a location, a thrown error stopping it, L5K-R25); an id that joined during that publication is read too and the check is made again after its read (L5K-R23); otherwise nothing is awaited before the synchronous answer: a promotion during any read or re-read is seen by the check, one during that publication finds the snapshot it installs (every answered id was bound before it), one after the check cannot interleave. L5K-R18's per-read second ask is folded into that check. The answer of each read is the outcome of the store's one adoption of the prior-art file (L5K-R13, rows k-5 and k-6): a queued file is claimed before its hash, so no batch takes it; a call that finds the file being processed by another adopt-on-demand, or adopted by a batch, waits for that processing and reads the location it bound; when a batch has not finished hashing the file, both may hash it and the one whose bytes verify second waits out the other's adoption. So `nil` means no prior-art file to adopt, or one dropped or kept, never a failure the other adopter caused (the round-1 text of this row claimed the first placement always bound before the other call decided; L5K-C1-00 showed it did not). |
| k-5 | `AssetStore.performAdoptLegacyNow(_:urgency:)` (`Migration/LegacyPhases.swift`, WP-C's file, close-out ruling L5K-R13): the wait for the processing of the same prior-art file in flight, another adopt-on-demand's (`MigrationState.onDemand`, from its claim to its published snapshot, installed at once only at `.userInitiated`) or another call's adoption (`MigrationState.adopting`), `awaitLegacyFlight(of:)`; and the loop's `awaitOpen()` after it | Nothing is carried across the wait: the call starts again and reads the location, the phase, the plan commit, the queue, the claims and the flights anew. The processing it waited for has ended: adopted (the location is present: the call installs a snapshot at its urgency and answers the location read after that install), dropped or kept (the file is neither queued nor claimed: `nil`), or ended without an outcome for the file (its caller was cancelled, or a transient failure deferred it back to the queue: this call takes the file and processes it itself, as a call arriving then would). `close()` resumes it: it drains the units and resumes the key and commit waiters, so the processing it waits for returns from its await, throws `.closed` at its next phase check, and ends its mark (every exit path of `processLegacyFile` and `processLegacyFileOnDemand` does), which resumes this call; its `awaitOpen()` then throws `.closed`. |
| k-6 | `AssetStore.processLegacyFile(_:)` (`Migration/LegacyPhases.swift`, L5K-R13): after its hash, whether it succeeded or failed, the wait until no other call's adoption of the file is in flight, `awaitLegacyAdoption(of:)` | The loop re-reads `adopting[file]` and the phase (`checkLive`, so a closing store answers `.closed`); it returns in the turn in which no adoption is in flight, and the caller then reads the records as that adoption left them: bound (`.alreadyAdopted`; for a failed hash, `legacyFailureOutcome` sees the binding), no longer wanted (`.dropped`), or still wanted (the other adoption failed: this call adopts with its own verified hash, marking `adopting` in that same turn, so at most one adoption runs at a time). A `notFound` read caused by the other call's rename, or a key in `publishing`, is therefore never classified before that call's binding. `close()` resumes it as for k-5. |
| k-7 | `AssetStore.performMigrateLegacy(maxFiles:)` (`Migration/LegacyPhases.swift`, close-out ruling L5K-R17): with the queue empty and every claimed file an adopt-on-demand's (`MigrationState.onDemand`), the wait for one such processing, `awaitLegacyFlight(of:)`, then `checkLive()` | The queue, the claims and the phase, re-read by the loop in the turn the wait returns: the processing it waited for has ended, adopted, dropped or kept (the file is no longer claimed), or deferred or cancelled (the file is back in the queue, which ends the wait); while every claimed file still is an adopt-on-demand's the call waits for the next one, so it never returns with nothing it could do, and once the claims are empty it goes on to phases 3 and 4 itself (the finish check that follows the loop). A closing store answers `.closed` (`checkLive`); `close()` resumes the wait through the processing it waits for, as for k-5. Each wait is bounded by one adoption. Since L5K-R20 a call reaches this wait only with no install of its own left due: a batch that still leaves files to process once its publication returned returns right after a coalesced publication (`remaining > 0`; what is left is read after the publication's await since L5K-R23, L5K-C4-00), so the wait is made by the next call, which published nothing; the last batch, and a batch that finds nothing left once its publication returned, install at once. |

Every k-n of the draft is cited, and no other (scripted at the level-5 WP-K merge: k-1 ×5, k-2 ×1 and k-3 ×1 in `CDNSyncCoordinator.swift`; k-4 ×3 in `EnginePresence.swift`; k-5 ×4, in `StoreMigration.swift` ×1 and `LegacyPhases.swift` ×3; k-6 ×6 and k-7 ×1 in `LegacyPhases.swift`).

## 11. Requirements and budgets → target → work package → test

Every one of the 90 requirements and 14 budgets. *Test* is the `@Test` display name (or names) the work package writes; *(exists)* marks tests already in the repository. Gates that run outside Swift Testing are named where they are the primary proof (Q-n of SPEC §7.1). WP-I owns documentation; WP-H the fakes every test uses. Where the WP column names several packages, each test names its writer in parentheses, so every test has exactly one writer with a path (§1.2) at a level where everything it needs is merged (round 4); a test without a mark belongs to the row's only WP, or to the first one listed when the rest only implement. The package-wide scans (R-CAR-9, R-SEC-3, R-ENV-2…5) are the orchestrator's, in `Tests/ZTronAssetModelTests/PackageScanContractTests.swift`.

| ID | Level | Target | WP | Test (display name) |

| R-MAN-2 | MUST | Model | A | `R-MAN-2: every structural violation rejects the manifest and every per-entry violation quarantines only that entry`<br>`R-MAN-2: limit boundaries accept the limit and reject one past it` |
| R-MAN-3 | MUST | Model | A | `R-MAN-3: seeded fuzz of random, truncated, mutated and deeply nested manifests never traps and stays within 1 s and 4x the payload` |
| R-MAN-4 | MUST | Model | A | `R-MAN-4: applying diff(a, b) to a yields b and every identifier is classified exactly once` *(exists)*<br>`R-MAN-4: seeded manifests diff against themselves as all unchanged` |
| R-MAN-5 | MUST | Model | A | `R-MAN-5: a checksum-less entry changes content identity only when its remotePath changes` *(exists)* |
| R-MAN-6 | SHOULD | Model | A | `R-MAN-6: absent tags and policy reproduce the prior art's behaviour` |

| R-CLN-4 | MUST | Storage | C | `R-CLN-4: eviction removes least-recently-used first, larger first on ties, down to 90 % of budget`<br>`R-CLN-4: pending reclaim decides a second eviction within the grace window, against a reserve and a fractional budget (store-2 S2-06)`<br>`R-CLN-4: eviction, apply's eviction plan and an evictable purge scan the records at most once per actor turn for ids bound to their blobs after the selection`<br>`R-CLN-4: an alias bound while eviction releases the actor between chunks keeps a later chunk's blob: each chunk scans the records afresh`<br>`R-CLN-4: the holders scan's prefilter never rejects a blob of the turn and lets few others through, however many blobs the turn re-validates`<br>`R-CLN-4: two quota evictions measured together free what one frees: bytes the other doomed meanwhile count toward the need`<br>`R-CLN-4: a quota eviction measured before apply evicted its plan frees only what is still needed`<br>`R-CLN-4: bytes apply's eviction plan dooms while quota eviction releases the actor between chunks count toward its need`<br>`R-CLN-4: bytes a purge dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need`<br>`R-CLN-4: bytes a quarantine or a recorded failure dooms while quota eviction computes its candidates count toward its need` (ruling R7)<br>`R-CLN-4: eviction releases the actor after a chunk's worth of records, cut at blob boundaries, however many ids share a blob`<br>`R-CLN-4: a blob several records share is a candidate only when all of them are evictable, whichever the order of the records`<br>`R-CLN-4: an apply or admit within the deletion grace never adopts an evicted asset again; only a relaunch does` (ruling R3; ADR-052) |
| R-CLN-5 | MUST | Storage, Sync | B, C, F | `R-CLN-5: a sink that keeps an older or foreign snapshot never counts as acknowledgement` (B)<br>`R-CLN-5: deletion grace uses the awake clock and a fresh acknowledgement after a device sleep` (B)<br>`R-CLN-5: deletion waits for the acknowledged snapshot without the blob, the grace window and every lease` (C, needs leases)<br>`R-CLN-5: reopening the same root in one process never deletes a blob within the grace window of its last acknowledgement` (C: store-2 S2-07, leases end at close; ADR-050)<br>`R-CLN-5: a deref during an in-flight commit or install never makes its blob deletable before a later commit and acknowledgement` (C: store-2 S2-13, through WP-C's dereferencing APIs as well as `apply`; ADR-050)<br>`R-CLN-5: every URL handed out stays readable for at least the grace window` (F: store-2 S2-12, the URLs the engine returns; ADR-050) |

| P-5 | BUDGET | Storage (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | B | `P-5: index commit of 10,000 records is under budget and a bulk sync commits at most 4 per second` |
| P-6 | BUDGET | Storage (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | B | `P-6: streaming SHA-256 of 256 MiB meets the throughput floor, the one-shot ratio and the memory cap` |
| P-7 | BUDGET | Sync (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | F (round 4; was "F, G", which the later-level rule would have put in `BenchKit.swift`) | `P-7: a 1 GiB synthetic bulk sync adds at most sync_memory_delta_mib_max MiB and does not grow with catalog size` |
| P-8 | BUDGET | Kit (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | G | `P-8: UI-facing calls make zero main-thread file-system calls and each stays within ui_call_ms_max` *(exists)*<br>`P-8: the fallback chain's bundle step does no work on the main thread` (§12.4 S2-13's regression name, added at the level-4 merge, ADR-053) *(exists)* |
| P-9 | BUDGET | Sync, Kit (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | F, G | `P-9: 64 concurrent resolves plus hashing keep the heartbeat gap p99 within budget` (G: `BenchKit.swift`) *(exists)* |
| P-10 | BUDGET | Transport (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | D | `P-10: 500 small assets over a loopback server use at most connections_max TCP connections` |
| P-11 | BUDGET | Transport, Sync (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | D, F | `P-11: assets with server latency sync at least speedup_min times faster at the configured concurrency than at 1` (F; its timed sync overlaps part of the launch run, so the measured speedup is a lower bound of the steady-state one, F-12; ADR-053) |
| P-12 | BUDGET | Kit (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | G | `P-12: AssetClient.init completes within init_ms_max and performs no file-system call` *(exists)* |
| P-13 | BUDGET | Storage (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | B | `P-13: reclaiming 10,000 blobs finishes under budget in batches of bounded duration` |
| P-14 | BUDGET | Sync (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks`) | F | `P-14: after quiescence the engine owns at most idle_timers_max timers and idle_tasks_max tasks` |

Counts: 90 requirements (78 MUST, 11 SHOULD, 1 MAY) and 14 budgets = 104 IDs. Every SHOULD and the MAY is planned for implementation; none is declined in this round. Benchmarks read every threshold from `Scripts/quality-gates.json` through `QualityBudgets` (`Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/QualityBudgets.swift`), and live in the benchmark target (ADR-010). They run serialized under one parent suite, `@Suite(.serialized) struct ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks {}` (`Tests/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks/ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks.swift`, round 3; sync-2 S2-14): every work package adds its benchmarks as a nested suite inside `extension ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks { … }` in its own file, never as a top-level suite, and each budget is written in exactly one file — `BenchModel.swift` (A: P-3), `BenchStorage.swift` (B: P-2, P-5, P-6, P-13), `BenchTransport.swift` (D: P-10), `BenchSync.swift` (F: P-4, P-7, P-11, P-14), `BenchKit.swift` (G: P-1, P-8, P-9, P-12); `BenchCleanup.swift` (C) carries no budget, only the C2 measurements of WP-C's actor holds (`CleanupBenchmarks`; ruling R4, ADR-052). `BenchSync.swift` also carries ten C2 benchmarks of WP-F's actor turns at 10,000 members (the probe chain at 1,000; suite `SyncBenchmarks`), asserted on the best of three against the C2 rule's 10 ms (`c2Budget`, a literal: no ruling of Level 4 authorises it, and ADR-053 records it as the level-3 ruling R4 precedent of `BenchCleanup` (ADR-052) applied to Sync), some also against P-9's `heartbeat_gap_ms_p99_max` from `QualityBudgets` on the median, and a ratio benchmark of a 10,000-id prune announced to a stalled subscriber (R-SYN-12); among them `R-SYN-5: a 429 that extends the throttle under 10,000 user-initiated waiters holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn` and `R-SYN-5: a chain of half-open probes ending without a verdict over 1,000 prefetch members parked by the breaker holds the engine's actor at most C2's 10 ms per turn, and reports the chain's total time` (L4-R22's baseline) (ADR-053). Where the WP column names two packages, the later level writes the benchmark. P-7's catalog is streamed by `FakeTransferStep.stageGenerated(seed:byteCount:)` with checksums from `GeneratedPayload.sha256(seed:byteCount:)`, so the fake never holds it in memory. The existing budget check is nested the same way (`ZTronAssetKitBenchmarks.BudgetsBenchmarks`).

## 12. Review findings → resolution

All 86 critical and major findings of the six Phase B reviews (19 critical, 67 major), then the 41 minor ones. *Resolved* means the contract or this document now prescribes the fix (with the ADR or section that holds it) and names the regression test the owning WP writes; *(exists)* marks a test already in the repository. No critical or major finding is rejected. At the Level 2 close (ADR-050) every §12.1/§12.2 regression name no merged WP-B test carried was mapped to the WP-B test that verifies it or reassigned to WP-C or WP-F; a reassigned name is also a row of §11 with that WP, so the level scripts pick it up. At the level-3 merge (ADR-052) the names reassigned to WP-C exist and are marked *(exists)*. At the level-4 merge (ADR-053) every sync and storage regression name no merged test carried was re-pointed to the merged test that covers the finding, saying what it covers, or marked "not written at the level-4 merge" and assigned: to Stage 3 hardening, or to WP-G where the finding is the facade's (those names are also §11 rows of WP-G). The benchmark names of §12.4 S2-12, S2-13, S2-14 and S2-18 stay WP-G's (Level 5, L4-R11) and are §11 rows P-8, P-9 and P-12. At the level-5 merge (ADR-054) the names assigned to WP-G exist and are marked *(exists)*; S1-05 end to end also needed the engine's pending claims (§7.2, L5-R1). At the level-5 WP-K merge (ADR-055) no name was reassigned; WP-K's close-out widened store-2 S2-09's adopt-on-demand to every job's pre-transfer read during migration, with one adoption per legacy file (L5K-R1, L5K-R13), and that row names WP-K's regression tests.

### 12.1 store-1 (crash consistency)

| ID | Sev | Resolution | Regression test |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1-01 | critical | Resolved, ADR-015, §4.1, §4.5: `open()` dirties `blobs/` and barrier-syncs the root before launch deletions; every adoption dirties its fan-out; `FakeFileSystem.processKill` keeps the volatile split; R-STO-5 enumerates kill→power-loss pairs | `R-STO-5: a process kill followed by a power loss in the next lifetime preserves I-1…I-5` |
| S1-02 | critical | Resolved, ADR-015, §3: `<base>` barrier-synced after creating `ZTronAssetKit`; durable commits full-sync `ZTronAssetKit` and `<base>` (`StoreLayout.container`) | `R-STO-5: power loss after a durable commit keeps the store root reachable` |
| S1-03 | critical | Resolved, ADR-018: `DoomReason` persisted; adoption never binds purged, quarantined or failed records; a failed unlink stays doomed | `R-CLN-6: a purge is not undone by a power loss after the reaper ran`; `R-CLN-8: a quarantined blob resurrected by power loss is never re-adopted` |
| S1-04 | critical | Resolved, §4.2: overflow-checked `nextGeneration`, generations ≥ 2⁶² malformed, `bodyLength` bound checked before arithmetic | `R-STO-12: a header with a maximal generation or body length never traps` |
| S1-05 | major | Resolved, ADR-018, §4.3: adoption iterates records, `apply` adopts before deref, checksum-less and rescue publishes commit before returning, `apply` diffs against current state (any digest short-circuit is the engine's, §7.3; ADR-050) | `R-STO-5: crash at every file-system boundary during a first sync preserves I-1…I-5` *(exists)* (B: its first sync holds a dedupe onto a live blob and a checksum-less, unverified entry; after a kill nothing whose publication returned is downloaded again, and after a power loss the checksum-less one is not either); `R-CLN-5: a rescued publication is committed before it returns` *(exists)* (B: the rescue is in `index.bin` when `publish` returns) (ADR-050) |
| S1-06 | major | Resolved, ADR-018: `corrupt` doom reason; a verified publish replaces a corrupt name; EEXIST compares sizes | `R-CLN-7: a truncated blob is replaced by the next verified download` *(exists)* (C, at the level-3 merge, ADR-052; WP-B's parts are `R-CLN-7: a wrong-sized blob makes its records needsHeal and is quarantined as corrupt, never adopted`, `R-STO-5: a commit during a corrupt key's replacement keeps the key doomed, so a crash never adopts the rotten bytes` and `R-INT-1: a copy replaces a corrupt name under the second extension`) |
| S1-07 | major | Resolved, ADR-015, §4.1: ENOSPC at commit removes `index.tmp`, and `index.prev` only while `index.bin` is the file known to hold the committed generation (B-28), and retries once; the scheduled retry backs off exponentially to 5 minutes (B-29); `requireCommitted` throws `.fileSystem(.noSpace)` (mapped to `insufficientSpace`); `open()` never fails on a post-load commit (it serves the loaded state with the commit due; ADR-050) | `R-SYN-11: eviction frees space while every index commit fails with ENOSPC`; `R-CLN-6: purge on a full volume returns bytes freed` |
| S1-08 | major | Resolved: `FileLockToken.identity` (dev, ino) checked before every commit, and on demand by `AssetStore.verifyRootNow()` after a facade failure that can stem from the file system (L5-R20; ADR-054); lock released on every throwing path of `open()`; directories to `v1` → lock → attributes | `R-STO-9: a replaced root is detected before the next commit`; `R-STO-9: a failed open releases the lock` |
| S1-09 | major | Resolved, ADR-039, `AssetStore` contract: phase `closing`, in-flight units drained, every resumption re-checks the phase | `R-SYN-15: no store mutation or commit happens after close returns` (WP-F, §11; ADR-050: WP-B's `R-STO-9: close() drains an in-flight publication, which unwinds with closed, and the next launch adopts its blob` checks the drain and the `.closed` unwind, not the absence of later mutations) |
| S1-10 | major | Resolved, ADR-027, §4.6a: `AssetStore.adoptionReport()` captures synchronously, commits durably and reports only what it captured (round 2; REENTRANCY R15); release after that durable commit | `R-CLN-10: a pack is released only after the adopting generation is durably committed`; `R-CLN-10: a publish that lands during the adoption report's durable commit is not reported adopted` |
| S1-11 | major | Resolved, §4.2: in degraded mode DOOMED keys are protected like orphans until the next apply plus adoption | `R-STO-4: a degraded fallback deletes no blob listed in the older generation's doomed set` |
| S1-12 | major | Resolved, §4.3, ADR-013: `publish` claims `.part` → `.verifying` before hashing; producers never hold a descriptor after completion | `R-STO-3: bytes appended to a staged file after publication begins are never published` |
| S1-13 | major | Resolved, ADR-017, ADR-022: no reaper loop; `nextDeadline()` covers time-only blockers; lease/ack/commit re-arm the engine's alarm | `R-CLN-5: a doomed key blocked only by a lease keeps no deadline, and releasing the lease makes it deletable at once` (B) *(exists)* (the store owns no task, ADR-022; re-pointed at the level-4 merge, ADR-053) |
